\begin{split}\sum_{h,k\leq Q^{\vartheta}}\frac{\lambda_{h}\overline{\lambda_{k}}}{\sqrt{hk}}\mathcal{L}^{r}(h,k)\ll_{\varepsilon}(XQ)^{\varepsilon}\sum_{c>C}\sum_{d=1}^{\infty}W\left(\frac{cd}{Q}\right)\sum_{u|d}\,\sideset{}{{}^{\flat}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\psi\bmod u\\
\psi\neq\psi_{0}\end{subarray}}\Bigg|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\leq Q^{\vartheta}\\
(h,q)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\lambda_{h}\psi(h)}{\sqrt{h}}\Bigg|^{2},\end{split}

where we again use
\flat
to denote that the sum is over even primitive characters. We substitute
d=ru
and write the
d
-sum as a double sum over
r
and
u
to arrive at

\begin{split}\sum_{h,k\leq Q^{\vartheta}}\frac{\lambda_{h}\overline{\lambda_{k}}}{\sqrt{hk}}\mathcal{L}^{r}(h,k)\ll_{\varepsilon}(XQ)^{\varepsilon}\sum_{c>C}\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\sum_{u=1}^{\infty}W\left(\frac{cru}{Q}\right)\,\sideset{}{{}^{\flat}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\psi\bmod u\\
\psi\neq\psi_{0}\end{subarray}}\Bigg|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\leq Q^{\vartheta}\\
(h,q)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\lambda_{h}\psi(h)}{\sqrt{h}}\Bigg|^{2}.\end{split}

Since
W
is bounded and compactly supported, it follows that

\begin{split}\sum_{h,k\leq Q^{\vartheta}}\frac{\lambda_{h}\overline{\lambda_{k}}}{\sqrt{hk}}\mathcal{L}^{r}(h,k)\ll_{\varepsilon}(XQ)^{\varepsilon}\sum_{C<c\ll Q}\sum_{r\ll\frac{Q}{c}}\sum_{u\ll\frac{Q}{cr}}\,\sideset{}{{}^{\flat}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\psi\bmod u\\
\psi\neq\psi_{0}\end{subarray}}\Bigg|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\leq Q^{\vartheta}\\
(h,q)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\lambda_{h}\psi(h)}{\sqrt{h}}\Bigg|^{2}.\end{split}

The large sieve (see, for example,
[
Dav00
, §27, Theorem 4]
) implies that

\sum_{u\ll\frac{Q}{cr}}\,\sideset{}{{}^{\flat}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\psi\bmod u\\
\psi\neq\psi_{0}\end{subarray}}\Bigg|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\leq Q^{\vartheta}\\
(h,q)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\lambda_{h}\psi(h)}{\sqrt{h}}\Bigg|^{2}\ll\bigg(Q^{\vartheta}+\frac{Q^{2}}{c^{2}r^{2}}\bigg)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\leq Q^{\vartheta}\\
(h,q)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{|\lambda_{h}|^{2}}{h}.

Hence, since
\lambda_{h}\ll_{\varepsilon}h^{\varepsilon}
, it follows that

(7.1.2)

\begin{split}\sum_{h,k\leq Q^{\vartheta}}\frac{\lambda_{h}\overline{\lambda_{k}}}{\sqrt{hk}}\mathcal{L}^{r}(h,k)&\ll_{\varepsilon}(XQ)^{\varepsilon}\sum_{C<c\ll Q}\sum_{r\ll\frac{Q}{c}}\bigg(Q^{\vartheta}+\frac{Q^{2}}{c^{2}r^{2}}\bigg)Q^{\varepsilon}\\
&\ll_{\varepsilon}(XQ)^{\varepsilon}\sum_{C<c\ll Q}\bigg(\frac{Q^{1+\vartheta}}{c}+\frac{Q^{2}}{c^{2}}\bigg)\\
&\ll_{\varepsilon}(XQ)^{\varepsilon}\bigg(Q^{1+\vartheta+\varepsilon}+\frac{Q^{2}}{C}\bigg).\end{split}

As mentioned in Section
5
, we will eventually choose
C
as a specific positive power of
Q
to control this error term.

7.2.
Preparing
\mathcal{L}^{0}(h,k)
for eventual cancellation

The goal of this subsection is to put
\mathcal{L}^{0}(h,k)
into a form that, as we will eventually see in Section
8.3
, cancels with a term arising from our analysis of
\mathcal{U}(h,k)
. To this end, let us first focus on the
c,d
-sum in the definition (
7.2
) of
\mathcal{L}^{0}(h,k)
. We complete the
c
-sum by writing

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}c>C,d\geq 1\\
cd=q\end{subarray}}\mu(c)=\sum_{c|q}\mu(c)-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}c\leq C,d\geq 1\\
cd=q\end{subarray}}\mu(c)=\left\lfloor\frac{1}{q}\right\rfloor-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}c\leq C,d\geq 1\\
cd=q\end{subarray}}\mu(c).

The latter
c,d
-sum equals
1
if
q=1
, and so it follows that

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}c>C,d\geq 1\\
cd=q\end{subarray}}\mu(c)=\begin{cases}\displaystyle-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}c\leq C,d\geq 1\\
cd=q\end{subarray}}\mu(c)&\text{if }q>1\\
\\
\hphantom{---}0&\text{if }q=1.\end{cases}

From this and the definition (
7.2
) of
\mathcal{L}^{0}(h,k)
, we arrive at

\mathcal{L}^{0}(h,k)=-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1<q<\infty\\
(q,hk)=1\end{subarray}}W\left(\frac{q}{Q}\right)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq m,n<\infty\\
(mn,q)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A}(m)\tau_{B}(n)}{\sqrt{mn}}V\left(\frac{m}{X}\right)V\left(\frac{n}{X}\right)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}c\leq C,d\geq 1\\
cd=q\end{subarray}}\mu(c).

Without loss of generality, we may ignore the condition
q>1
and simply sum over all
1\leq q<\infty
because the
q=1
term is zero for large enough
Q
, as
W
is supported away from
0
. We substitute
q=cd
and interchange the order of summation to deduce that

(7.2.1)

\mathcal{L}^{0}(h,k)=-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq c\leq C\\
(c,hk)=1\end{subarray}}\mu(c)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq m,n<\infty\\
(mn,c)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A}(m)\tau_{B}(n)}{\sqrt{mn}}V\left(\frac{m}{X}\right)V\left(\frac{n}{X}\right)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq d<\infty\\
(d,mhnk)=1\end{subarray}}W\left(\frac{cd}{Q}\right).

To evaluate the latter
d
-sum, we use Stieltjes integration and the fact that

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d\leq x\\
(d,m)=1\end{subarray}}1=x\frac{\phi(m)}{m}+E(x,m)

for some function
E(x,m)
such that
E(x,m)=O(m^{\varepsilon})
uniformly for all
x>0
and positive integers
m
. This results to

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq d<\infty\\
(d,mnhk)=1\end{subarray}}W\left(\frac{cd}{Q}\right)=W\left(\frac{c}{Q}\right)+\frac{\phi(mnhk)}{mnhk}\int_{1}^{\infty}W\left(\frac{cx}{Q}\right)\,dx+\int_{1}^{\infty}W\left(\frac{cx}{Q}\right)\,dE.

Note that
W(c/Q)\ll 1
. Moreover, we may integrate by parts to see that the last integral is
O((mnhk)^{\varepsilon})
by the bound on
E(x,m)
and the fact that
W
is compactly supported. By a change of variables, we have

\frac{c}{Q}\int_{1}^{\infty}W\left(\frac{cx}{Q}\right)\,dx=\int_{0}^{\infty}W(x)\,dx-\int_{0}^{c/Q}W(x)\,dx=\int_{0}^{\infty}W(x)\,dx+O\left(\frac{c}{Q}\right).

Combining these estimates with (
7.2.1
), we find that

(7.2.2)

\begin{split}\mathcal{L}^{0}(h,k)&=-Q\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq c\leq C\\
(c,hk)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(c)}{c}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq m,n<\infty\\
(mn,c)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A}(m)\tau_{B}(n)}{\sqrt{mn}}V\left(\frac{m}{X}\right)V\left(\frac{n}{X}\right)\frac{\phi(mnhk)}{mnhk}\int_{0}^{\infty}W(x)\,dx\\
&\hskip 108.405pt+O\big((Xhk)^{\varepsilon}XC\big).\end{split}

In Section
8.3
, we will show that a part of
\mathcal{U}(h,k)
cancels with the main term above.

8.
Preparing the term
\mathcal{U}(h,k)
for analysis

There are two goals for this section. The first is to switch to the complementary modulus by making a change of variables in the definition (
5.5
) of
\mathcal{U}(h,k)
and then express the divisibility condition in terms of character sums. The second goal is to dissect the contribution of the principal characters in order to isolate the part of it containing the predicted one-swap terms.

8.1.
\mathcal{U}(h,k)
: Switching to the complementary modulus

Recall the definition (
5.5
) of
\mathcal{U}(h,k)
. We substitute
q=cd
and rearrange the sum to deduce that

(8.1.1)

\mathcal{U}(h,k)=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq c\leq C\\
(c,hk)=1\end{subarray}}\mu(c)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq m,n<\infty\\
(mn,c)=1\\
mh\neq nk\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A}(m)\tau_{B}(n)}{\sqrt{mn}}V\left(\frac{m}{X}\right)V\left(\frac{n}{X}\right)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq d<\infty\\
(d,mhnk)=1\\
d|mh\pm nk\end{subarray}}\phi(d)W\left(\frac{cd}{Q}\right).

Let
g=(mh,nk)
. Then the condition that
d|mh\pm nk
and
(d,mhnk)=1
is equivalent to the condition that
d|\frac{mh}{g}\pm\frac{nk}{g}
and
(d,g)=1
. From this and the fact that
\phi(d)=\sum_{ef=d}\mu(e)f
, we see that the
d
-sum in (
8.1.1
) equals

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq d<\infty\\
(d,g)=1\\
d|\frac{mh}{g}\pm\frac{nk}{g}\end{subarray}}\phi(d)W\left(\frac{cd}{Q}\right)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq e<\infty\\
(e,g)=1\end{subarray}}\mu(e)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq f<\infty\\
(f,g)=1\\
ef|\frac{mh}{g}\pm\frac{nk}{g}\end{subarray}}fW\left(\frac{cef}{Q}\right).

Use Möbius inversion to detect the condition
(f,g)=1
and write the above as

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq e<\infty\\
(e,g)=1\end{subarray}}\mu(e)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq f<\infty\\
ef|\frac{mh}{g}\pm\frac{nk}{g}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a|f\\
a|g\end{subarray}}\mu(a)fW\left(\frac{cef}{Q}\right)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq e<\infty\\
(e,g)=1\end{subarray}}\mu(e)\sum_{a|g}\mu(a)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq f<\infty\\
a|f\\
ef|\frac{mh}{g}\pm\frac{nk}{g}\end{subarray}}fW\left(\frac{cef}{Q}\right).

Make a change of variables
f=ab
in the
f
-sum to see that this equals

(8.1.2)

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq e<\infty\\
(e,g)=1\end{subarray}}\mu(e)\sum_{a|g}a\mu(a)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq b<\infty\\
eab|\frac{mh}{g}\pm\frac{nk}{g}\end{subarray}}bW\left(\frac{ceab}{Q}\right).

Now define the “complementary modulus”
\ell
by

|mh\pm nk|=geab\ell,

and use it to make a change of variables in the
b
-sum to write (
8.1.2
) as

(8.1.3)

\begin{split}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq e<\infty\\
(e,g)=1\end{subarray}}&\mu(e)\sum_{a|g}a\mu(a)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq\ell<\infty\\
ea\ell|\frac{mh}{g}\pm\frac{nk}{g}\end{subarray}}\frac{|mh\pm nk|}{gea\ell}W\left(\frac{c|mh\pm nk|}{g\ell Q}\right)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq e<\infty\\
(e,g)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(e)}{e}\sum_{a|g}\mu(a)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq\ell<\infty\\
ea\ell|\frac{mh}{g}\pm\frac{nk}{g}\end{subarray}}\frac{|mh\pm nk|}{g\ell}W\left(\frac{c|mh\pm nk|}{g\ell Q}\right).\end{split}

Since
g
is defined by
g=(mh,nk)
, we must have that
ea\ell
is coprime to each of
mh/g
and
nk/g
, because if not then the condition
ea\ell|(mh\pm nk)/g
would imply that
mh/g
and
nk/g
are not coprime, contradicting the definition of
g
. Thus the orthogonality of character sums implies

\frac{1}{\phi(ea\ell)}\sum_{\psi\bmod ea\ell}\psi\left(\frac{mh}{g}\right)\overline{\psi}\left(\mp\frac{nk}{g}\right)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}1&\text{if }ea\ell|\frac{mh}{g}\pm\frac{nk}{g}\\
\\
0&\text{else}.\end{array}\right.

Hence, we may replace the condition
ea\ell|(mh\pm nk)/g
in (
8.1.3
) with the above multiplier to conclude that the
d
-sum appearing in (
8.1.1
) is equal to

\begin{split}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq e<\infty\\
(e,g)=1\end{subarray}}&\frac{\mu(e)}{e}\sum_{a|g}\mu(a)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq\ell<\infty\\
(ea\ell,\frac{mh}{g}\cdot\frac{nk}{g})=1\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{\phi(ea\ell)}\sum_{\psi\bmod ea\ell}\psi\left(\frac{mh}{g}\right)\overline{\psi}\left(\mp\frac{nk}{g}\right)\\
&\hskip 36.135pt\times\frac{|mh\pm nk|}{g\ell}W\left(\frac{c|mh\pm nk|}{g\ell Q}\right).\end{split}

It follows that

(8.1.4)

\begin{split}\mathcal{U}(h,k)&=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq c\leq C\\
(c,hk)=1\end{subarray}}\mu(c)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq m,n<\infty\\
(mn,c)=1\\
mh\neq nk\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A}(m)\tau_{B}(n)}{\sqrt{mn}}V\left(\frac{m}{X}\right)V\left(\frac{n}{X}\right)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq e<\infty\\
(e,g)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(e)}{e}\sum_{a|g}\mu(a)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq\ell<\infty\\
(ea\ell,\frac{mh}{g}\cdot\frac{nk}{g})=1\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{\phi(ea\ell)}\sum_{\psi\bmod ea\ell}\psi\left(\frac{mh}{g}\right)\overline{\psi}\left(\mp\frac{nk}{g}\right)\frac{|mh\pm nk|}{g\ell}W\left(\frac{c|mh\pm nk|}{g\ell Q}\right).\end{split}

Write this as

(8.1.5)

\mathcal{U}(h,k)=\mathcal{U}^{0}(h,k)+\mathcal{U}^{r}(h,k),

where
\mathcal{U}^{0}(h,k)
is the contribution of the principal character in the
\psi
-sum, and
\mathcal{U}^{r}(h,k)
is the contribution of the non-principal characters. In other words,
\mathcal{U}^{0}(h,k)
and
\mathcal{U}^{r}(h,k)
are defined by

(8.1.6)

\begin{split}\mathcal{U}^{0}(h,k)&:=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq c\leq C\\
(c,hk)=1\end{subarray}}\mu(c)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq m,n<\infty\\
(mn,c)=1\\
mh\neq nk\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A}(m)\tau_{B}(n)}{\sqrt{mn}}V\left(\frac{m}{X}\right)V\left(\frac{n}{X}\right)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq e<\infty\\
(e,g)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(e)}{e}\sum_{a|g}\mu(a)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq\ell<\infty\\
(ea\ell,\frac{mh}{g}\cdot\frac{nk}{g})=1\end{subarray}}\frac{|mh\pm nk|}{g\ell\phi(ea\ell)}W\left(\frac{c|mh\pm nk|}{g\ell Q}\right)\end{split}

and

(8.1.7)

\begin{split}\mathcal{U}^{r}(h,k)&:=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq c\leq C\\
(c,hk)=1\end{subarray}}\mu(c)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq m,n<\infty\\
(mn,c)=1\\
mh\neq nk\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A}(m)\tau_{B}(n)}{\sqrt{mn}}V\left(\frac{m}{X}\right)V\left(\frac{n}{X}\right)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq e<\infty\\
(e,g)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(e)}{e}\sum_{a|g}\mu(a)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq\ell<\infty\\
(ea\ell,\frac{mh}{g}\cdot\frac{nk}{g})=1\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{\phi(ea\ell)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\psi\bmod ea\ell\\
\psi\neq\psi_{0}\end{subarray}}\psi\left(\frac{mh}{g}\right)\overline{\psi}\left(\mp\frac{nk}{g}\right)\frac{|mh\pm nk|}{g\ell}W\left(\frac{c|mh\pm nk|}{g\ell Q}\right),\end{split}

respectively, where
\psi_{0}
denotes the principal character mod
ea\ell
.

8.2.
The principal contribution
\mathcal{U}^{0}(h,k)

Our goal in this subsection is to separate out a part of
\mathcal{U}^{0}(h,k)
that we will eventually prove contains the one-swap terms that are predicted by the recipe. We apply Mellin inversion to write

(8.2.1)

\frac{|mh\pm nk|}{g\ell}W\left(\frac{c|mh\pm nk|}{g\ell Q}\right)=\frac{Q}{2\pi ic}\int_{(\varepsilon)}\ell^{-w}\Upsilon_{\pm}(w;mh,nk)\,dw,

where

(8.2.2)

\Upsilon_{\pm}(w;mh,nk)=\Upsilon_{\pm}(w;mh,nk;c,Q):=\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{c|mh\pm nk|}{gxQ}W\left(\frac{c|mh\pm nk|}{gxQ}\right)x^{w-1}\,dx.

We insert (
8.2.1
) into (
8.1.6
), then interchange the order of summation and write the
\ell
-sum as an Euler product using the following lemma.

Lemma 8.1
.

[
CIS19
, Lemma 6]

Let
s
be a complex number with
\re(s)>0
, and let
u
and
v
be coprime natural numbers. Then

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell=1\\
(\ell,v)=1\end{subarray}}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\phi(u\ell)\ell^{s}}=\frac{1}{\phi(u)}\zeta(1+s)R(s;u,v),

where

(8.2.3)

R(s;u,v)=\prod_{p|v}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{s+1}}\right)\prod_{p\nmid uv}\left(1+\frac{1}{p^{s+1}(p-1)}\right)

converges absolutely in
\re(s)>-1
.

The result is

(8.2.4)

\begin{split}\mathcal{U}^{0}(h,k)&=\frac{Q}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq c\leq C\\
(c,hk)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(c)}{c}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq m,n<\infty\\
(mn,c)=1\\
mh\neq nk\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A}(m)\tau_{B}(n)}{\sqrt{mn}}V\left(\frac{m}{X}\right)V\left(\frac{n}{X}\right)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq e<\infty\\
(e,g)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(e)}{e}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a|g\\
(ea,\frac{mh}{g}\cdot\frac{nk}{g})=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(a)}{\phi(ea)}\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{(\varepsilon)}\Upsilon_{\pm}(w;mh,nk)\zeta(1+w)R(w;ea,mhnk/g^{2})\,dw.\end{split}

Note that
\Upsilon_{\pm}(w;mh,nk)
has rapid decay as
|w|\rightarrow\infty
by (
8.2.2
) and a repeated application of integration by parts. Hence, we may move the line of integration in (
8.2.4
) to Re
(w)=-\epsilon
. Doing so leaves a residue at
w=0
from the pole of
\zeta(1+w)
, and we arrive at

(8.2.5)

\mathcal{U}^{0}(h,k)=\mathcal{U}^{1}(h,k)+\mathcal{U}^{2}(h,k),

where
\mathcal{U}^{1}(h,k)
is the residue, i.e.,

(8.2.6)

\begin{split}\mathcal{U}^{1}(h,k):=&\frac{Q}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq c\leq C\\
(c,hk)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(c)}{c}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq m,n<\infty\\
(mn,c)=1\\
mh\neq nk\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A}(m)\tau_{B}(n)}{\sqrt{mn}}V\left(\frac{m}{X}\right)V\left(\frac{n}{X}\right)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq e<\infty\\
(e,g)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(e)}{e}\\
&\times\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a|g\\
(ea,\frac{mh}{g}\cdot\frac{nk}{g})=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(a)}{\phi(ea)}\Upsilon_{\pm}(0;mh,nk)R(0;ea,mhnk/g^{2}),\end{split}

and
\mathcal{U}^{2}(h,k)
is defined by

(8.2.7)

\begin{split}&\mathcal{U}^{2}(h,k):=\frac{Q}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq c\leq C\\
(c,hk)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(c)}{c}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq m,n<\infty\\
(mn,c)=1\\
mh\neq nk\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A}(m)\tau_{B}(n)}{\sqrt{mn}}V\left(\frac{m}{X}\right)V\left(\frac{n}{X}\right)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq e<\infty\\
(e,g)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(e)}{e}\\
&\times\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a|g\\
(ea,\frac{mh}{g}\cdot\frac{nk}{g})=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(a)}{\phi(ea)}\cdot\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{(-\epsilon)}\Upsilon_{\pm}(w;mh,nk)\zeta(1+w)R(w;ea,mhnk/g^{2})\,dw.\end{split}

8.3.
The term
\mathcal{U}^{1}(h,k)
approximately cancels with
\mathcal{L}^{0}(h,k)

In this subsection, we show that the term
\mathcal{U}^{1}(h,k)
defined by (
8.2.6
) cancels with the main contribution of
\mathcal{L}^{0}(h,k)
, which we have evaluated in (
7.2.2
). We first focus on the
e,a
-sum in
(
8.2.6
)
. To express it as an Euler product, we observe that Lemma
3.2
and the definition (
8.2.3
) of
R
implies for
\re(w)>-1
that

(8.3.1)

\begin{split}&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq e<\infty\\
(e,g)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(e)}{e}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a|g\\
(ea,\frac{mh}{g}\cdot\frac{nk}{g})=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(a)}{\phi(ea)}R(w;ae,mnhk/g^{2})\\
&\hskip 72.26999pt=\prod_{p|mnhk/g^{2}}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+w}}\right)\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}p|g\\
p\nmid mnhk/g^{2}\end{subarray}}\left(1+\frac{1}{p^{w+1}(p-1)}-\frac{1}{p-1}\right)\\
&\hskip 108.405pt\times\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}p\nmid g\\
p\nmid mnhk/g^{2}\end{subarray}}\left(1+\frac{p^{-w}-1}{p(p-1)}\right)\end{split}

(this is the same as (7.7) of
[
CIS19
]
). It follows from this with
w=0
that

(8.3.2)

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq e<\infty\\
(e,g)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(e)}{e}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a|g\\
(ea,\frac{mh}{g}\cdot\frac{nk}{g})=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(a)}{\phi(ea)}R(0;ae,mnhk/g^{2})=\frac{\phi(mnhk)}{mnhk}.

Now the definition (
8.2.2
) of
\Upsilon_{\pm}
and a change of variables gives

\Upsilon_{+}(0;mh,nk)+\Upsilon_{-}(0;mh,nk)=2\int_{0}^{\infty}W(u)\,du.

From this, (
8.2.6
), and (
8.3.2
), we deduce that

(8.3.3)

\mathcal{U}^{1}(h,k)=Q\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq c\leq C\\
(c,hk)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(c)}{c}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq m,n<\infty\\
(mn,c)=1\\
mh\neq nk\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A}(m)\tau_{B}(n)}{\sqrt{mn}}V\left(\frac{m}{X}\right)V\left(\frac{n}{X}\right)\frac{\phi(mnhk)}{mnhk}\int_{0}^{\infty}W(u)\,du.

In order to show that
\mathcal{U}^{1}(h,k)
cancels with the main term of
\mathcal{L}^{0}(h,k)
given in (
7.2.2
), we must complete the sum above to include the terms
mh=nk
. In order to do this successfully, we must show that the total contribution of the terms with
mh=nk
is small. By (
3.2
) and our assumption that
V
and
W
have compact support, the sum of the terms with
mh=nk
is at most

(8.3.4)

\ll Q\sum_{1\leq c\leq C}\frac{1}{c}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq m,n\ll X\\
mh=nk\end{subarray}}\frac{(mn)^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{mn}}.

Observe that
mh=nk
if and only if there is an integer
\ell
such that
m=\ell K
and
n=\ell H
, where, as before,
H
and
K
are defined by
H:=h/(h,k)
and
K:=k/(h,k)
. Thus (
8.3.4
) is

\displaystyle\ll(HK)^{-1/2+\varepsilon}Q(\log C)\sum_{1\leq\ell\ll X}\frac{1}{\ell^{1-\varepsilon}}\ll X^{\varepsilon}(HK)^{-1/2+\varepsilon}Q\log C.

Hence, including the
mh=nk
terms in (
8.3.3
) gives

(8.3.5)

\begin{split}\mathcal{U}^{1}(h,k)&=Q\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq c\leq C\\
(c,hk)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(c)}{c}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq m,n<\infty\\
(mn,c)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A}(m)\tau_{B}(n)}{\sqrt{mn}}V\left(\frac{m}{X}\right)V\left(\frac{n}{X}\right)\frac{\phi(mnhk)}{mnhk}\int_{0}^{\infty}W(u)\,du\\
&\hskip 36.135pt+O\bigg(Q\frac{(XCHK)^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{HK}}\bigg).\end{split}

The main term here cancels with the main term from our analysis of
\mathcal{L}^{0}(h,k)
, given in (
7.2.2
). More precisely, it follows from (
7.2.2
) and (
8.3.5
) that

(8.3.6)

\mathcal{U}^{1}(h,k)=-\mathcal{L}^{0}(h,k)+O\big((Xhk)^{\varepsilon}XC\big)+O\bigg(Q\frac{(XCHK)^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{HK}}\bigg).

Summarizing this section, we deduce from (
8.1.5
), (
8.2.5
), and (
8.3.6
) that

(8.3.7)

\mathcal{U}(h,k)=-\mathcal{L}^{0}(h,k)+\mathcal{U}^{2}(h,k)+\mathcal{U}^{r}(h,k)+O\big((Xhk)^{\varepsilon}XC\big)+O\bigg(Q\frac{(XCHK)^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{HK}}\bigg).

Looking forward, we show in Section
9
that
\mathcal{U}^{2}(h,k)
is, up to an admissible error term, equal to the one-swap terms
\mathcal{I}_{1}(h,k)
predicted by the recipe. In Section
10
, we bound the average of
(hk)^{-1/2}\mathcal{U}^{r}(h,k)
over
h,k
and show that
\mathcal{U}^{r}(h,k)
is an acceptable error term.

9.
The term
\mathcal{U}^{2}(h,k)
: extracting the one-swap terms

Recall that
\mathcal{U}^{2}(h,k)
, defined by (
8.2.7
), does not include the diagonal terms
mh=nk
. As in the analysis of
\mathcal{U}^{1}(h,k)
, we will find it advantageous to add these terms back in, and so we must show that the total contribution of these terms is acceptably small. The analysis that follows is similar to that of
\mathcal{U}^{1}(h,k)
in Subsection
8.3
. However, the treatment of
\Upsilon_{\pm}(w;mk,nk)
is more delicate because the variables
m
and
n
are entangled in the factor
|mh\pm nk|
. To ameliorate this challenge, we first introduce a bit of averaging as in Section 7 of
[
CIS19
]
. This averaging will lead to expressions with absolutely convergent integrals after separating the variables
m
and
n
in
\Upsilon_{\pm}(w;mk,nk)
(Proposition
9.2
below). The absolute convergence, in turn, will allow us to interchange the order of summation in our analysis of
\mathcal{U}^{2}(h,k)
and extract the predicted one-swap terms in the subsections that follow.

To begin, we state and prove the averaging result that we will apply as just described.

Lemma 9.1
.

Let
f:[0,\infty)\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
be a continuously differentiable function of compact support such that
f
is zero in a neighborhood of zero. Let
x,y,v\in\mathbb{R}
, with
v>0
. Then the function

t\longmapsto f(v|x-ty|)

is continuously differentiable on
\mathbb{R}
. Moreover, if
\,0<\delta<1
, then

f(v|x-y|)=\frac{1}{2\delta}\int_{-\delta}^{\delta}f(v|x-e^{\xi}y|)\,d\xi+O(|vy|\delta),

where the implied constant depends only on
f
.

Proof.

That the function
t\longmapsto f(v|x-ty|)
is continuously differentiable on
\mathbb{R}
follows by the chain rule and the assumption that
f
is zero in a neighborhood of zero. Moreover,
f^{\prime}(x)=O(1)
uniformly on
\mathbb{R}
because
f
has compact support, and so

\frac{d}{dt}f(v|x-ty|)=\pm vyf^{\prime}(v|x-ty|)\ll|vy|.

It follows from this and the fundamental theorem of calculus that, for
0<\delta<1
,

\displaystyle\int_{-\delta}^{\delta}f(v|x-e^{\xi}y|)\,d\xi-\int_{-\delta}^{\delta}f(v|x-y|)\,d\xi

\displaystyle=\int_{-\delta}^{\delta}\int_{1}^{e^{\xi}}\frac{d}{dt}f(v|x-ty|)\,dt\,d\xi

\displaystyle\ll|vy|\int_{-\delta}^{\delta}|\xi|\,d\xi\ll|vy|\delta^{2}.

Rearranging the terms gives the lemma.
∎

Before we apply Lemma
9.1
to the sum
\mathcal{U}^{2}(h,k)
defined by (
8.2.7
), we first truncate the
w
-integral in (
8.2.7
). Doing so will enable us to easily deal with the error term arising from the application of Lemma
9.1
. To this end, observe that if
\xi\in\mathbb{R}
, then a change of variables implies

(9.1)

\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{c|mh\pm e^{\xi}nk|}{gxQ}W\left(\frac{c|mh\pm e^{\xi}nk|}{gxQ}\right)x^{w-1}\,dx=\left(\frac{c|mh\pm e^{\xi}nk|}{gQ}\right)^{w}\widetilde{W}(1-w).

If
w,c,m,h,n,k
are as in (
8.2.7
), then
|mh\pm nk|\geq 1
since
mh\neq nk
, and so the definition (
8.2.2
) of
\Upsilon_{\pm}(w;mh,nk)
, (
9.1
) with
\xi=0
, and (
3.10
) imply that

(9.2)

\Upsilon_{\pm}(w;mh,nk)\ll_{\nu}\frac{(gQ)^{\varepsilon}}{|w|^{\nu}}

for any positive integer
\nu
. Now the definition (
8.2.3
) of
R
implies that if
\re(w)=-\varepsilon
, then

(9.3)

R(w;ea,mhnk/g^{2})\ll(mhnk)^{\varepsilon}.

From this and (
9.2
), we see that the part of the integral in (
8.2.7
) that has
|\text{Im}(w)|\geq(XQ)^{\varepsilon}
is negligible. Thus, using also (
3.2
), the definition
g=(mh,nk)
, and the assumption that
V
has compact support, we deduce that

(9.4)

\begin{split}&\mathcal{U}^{2}(h,k)=\frac{Q}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq c\leq C\\
(c,hk)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(c)}{c}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq m,n<\infty\\
(mn,c)=1\\
mh\neq nk\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A}(m)\tau_{B}(n)}{\sqrt{mn}}V\left(\frac{m}{X}\right)V\left(\frac{n}{X}\right)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq e<\infty\\
(e,g)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(e)}{e}\\
&\times\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a|g\\
(ea,\frac{mh}{g}\cdot\frac{nk}{g})=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(a)}{\phi(ea)}\cdot\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{-\epsilon-i(XQ)^{\varepsilon}}^{-\epsilon+i(XQ)^{\varepsilon}}\Upsilon_{\pm}(w;mh,nk)\zeta(1+w)R(w;ea,mhnk/g^{2})\,dw\\
&\hskip 108.405pt+O\big((Chk)^{\varepsilon}Q^{-99}\big).\end{split}

Having truncated the integral in (
8.2.7
), we now apply Lemma
9.1
. Recall that the support of
W
is a compact subset of
(0,\infty)
. Use Lemma
9.1
with
f(u)=uW(u)
and
\delta
defined by (
3.4
) to deduce that the integrand in (
8.2.2
) satisfies

\displaystyle\frac{c|mh\pm nk|}{gxQ}W\left(\frac{c|mh\pm nk|}{gxQ}\right)=\frac{1}{2\delta}\int_{-\delta}^{\delta}\frac{c|mh\pm e^{\xi}nk|}{gxQ}W\left(\frac{c|mh\pm e^{\xi}nk|}{gxQ}\right)\,d\xi+O\left(\frac{cnk\delta}{gxQ}\right).

We insert this into the definition (
8.2.2
) of
\Upsilon_{\pm}(w;mh,nk)
. The contribution of the error term is

\displaystyle\ll\frac{cnk\delta}{gQ}\left(\frac{c|mh\pm nk|}{gQ}\right)^{-\varepsilon-1}\ll Xk\delta(gQ)^{\varepsilon}

for
w,c,m,h,n,k
satisfying the conditions in (
9.4
), because
|mh\pm nk|\geq 1
,
c\geq 1
,
n\ll X
, and, by the support of
W
, the integrand in (
8.2.2
) is zero unless
x\asymp c|mh\pm nk|/(gQ)
. We arrive at

\Upsilon_{\pm}(w;mh,nk)=\frac{1}{2\delta}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{-\delta}^{\delta}\frac{c|mh\pm e^{\xi}nk|}{gxQ}W\left(\frac{c|mh\pm e^{\xi}nk|}{gxQ}\right)x^{w-1}\,d\xi\,dx+O\big(Xk\delta(gQ)^{\varepsilon}\big).

This and (
9.1
) imply

\Upsilon_{\pm}(w;mh,nk)=\frac{1}{2\delta}\int_{-\delta}^{\delta}\left(\frac{c|mh\pm e^{\xi}nk|}{gQ}\right)^{w}\widetilde{W}(1-w)\,d\xi+O\big(Xk\delta(gQ)^{\varepsilon}\big).

We insert this into (
9.4
) to deduce that

(9.5)

\begin{split}\mathcal{U}^{2}(h,k)&=\frac{Q}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq c\leq C\\
(c,hk)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(c)}{c}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq m,n<\infty\\
(mn,c)=1\\
mh\neq nk\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A}(m)\tau_{B}(n)}{\sqrt{mn}}V\left(\frac{m}{X}\right)V\left(\frac{n}{X}\right)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq e<\infty\\
(e,g)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(e)}{e}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a|g\\
(ea,\frac{mh}{g}\cdot\frac{nk}{g})=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(a)}{\phi(ea)}\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{-\epsilon-i(XQ)^{\varepsilon}}^{-\epsilon+i(XQ)^{\varepsilon}}\zeta(1+w)R(w;ea,mhnk/g^{2})\left(\frac{c}{gQ}\right)^{w}\widetilde{W}(1-w)\\
&\hskip 36.135pt\times\frac{1}{2\delta}\int_{-\delta}^{\delta}|mh\pm e^{\xi}nk|^{w}\,d\xi\,dw+O\big((XChk)^{\varepsilon}kX^{2}Q^{-97}\big),\end{split}

where, to bound the error term, we have used (
3.2
), (
9.3
), the definition
g=(mh,nk)
, the definition (
3.4
) of
\delta
, and the assumption that
V
has compact support.

The following proposition, which is Proposition 2 in
[
CIS19
]
, enables us to separate the variables
m
and
n
in the expression
|mh\pm e^{\xi}nk|
and thus write the
m,n,e,a
-sum in (
9.5
) in terms of an Euler product.

Proposition 9.2

(Proposition 2 of
[
CIS19
]
)
.

Let
\omega
be a complex number with
\re(\omega)>0
. Then for any
0<c<\re(\omega)
, and
r>0
with
r\neq 1
, we have

|1+r|^{-\omega}+|1-r|^{-\omega}=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{(c)}\mathcal{H}(z,\omega)r^{-z}\,dz.

Therefore, for any
\delta>0
,

(9.6)

\frac{1}{2\delta}\int_{-\delta}^{\delta}|1+e^{\xi}r|^{-\omega}+|1-e^{\xi}r|^{-\omega}\,d\xi=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{(c)}\mathcal{H}(z,\omega)r^{-z}\frac{e^{\delta z}-e^{-\delta z}}{2\delta z}\,dz,

where
\mathcal{H}(z,\omega)
is defined by (
3.13
). The
z
-integral in (
9.6
) converges absolutely for
\re(\omega)<1
.

We apply Proposition
9.2
with
\omega=-w
,
\re(w)=-\epsilon
,
c=\epsilon/2
, and
r=nk/(mh)
, which is
\neq 1
in (
9.5
), to deduce that

\frac{1}{2\delta}\int_{-\delta}^{\delta}\left|1+e^{\xi}\frac{nk}{mh}\right|^{w}+\left|1-e^{\xi}\frac{nk}{mh}\right|^{w}\,d\xi=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{(\epsilon/2)}\mathcal{H}(z,-w)\left(\frac{nk}{mh}\right)^{-z}\frac{e^{\delta z}-e^{-\delta z}}{2\delta z}\,dz.

Multiply both sides by
(mh)^{w}
to find that

\frac{1}{2\delta}\int_{-\delta}^{\delta}|mh+e^{\xi}nk|^{w}+|mh-e^{\xi}nk|^{w}\,d\xi=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{(\epsilon/2)}\mathcal{H}(z,-w)(mh)^{w+z}(nk)^{-z}\frac{e^{\delta z}-e^{-\delta z}}{2\delta z}\,dz.

We insert this into (
9.5
) and arrive at

(9.7)

\begin{split}\mathcal{U}^{2}(h,k)&=\frac{Q}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq c\leq C\\
(c,hk)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(c)}{c}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq m,n<\infty\\
(mn,c)=1\\
mh\neq nk\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A}(m)\tau_{B}(n)}{\sqrt{mn}}V\left(\frac{m}{X}\right)V\left(\frac{n}{X}\right)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq e<\infty\\
(e,g)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(e)}{e}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a|g\\
(ea,\frac{mh}{g}\cdot\frac{nk}{g})=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(a)}{\phi(ea)}\\
&\hskip 10.84006pt\times\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{-\epsilon-i(XQ)^{\varepsilon}}^{-\epsilon+i(XQ)^{\varepsilon}}\zeta(1+w)R(w;ea,mhnk/g^{2})\left(\frac{c}{gQ}\right)^{w}\widetilde{W}(1-w)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{(\epsilon/2)}\mathcal{H}(z,-w)(mh)^{w+z}(nk)^{-z}\frac{e^{\delta z}-e^{-\delta z}}{2\delta z}\,dz\,dw+O\big((XChk)^{\varepsilon}kX^{2}Q^{-97}\big).\end{split}

By (
3.2
), (
3.15
), (
3.10
), (
9.3
), and the assumption that
V
has compact support, we may extend the
w
-integral in (
9.7
) to infinity by introducing a negligible error. We then insert (
8.3.1
) to deduce that

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}^{2}(h,k)

\displaystyle=\frac{Q}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq c\leq C\\
(c,hk)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(c)}{c}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq m,n<\infty\\
(mn,c)=1\\
mh\neq nk\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A}(m)\tau_{B}(n)}{\sqrt{mn}}V\left(\frac{m}{X}\right)V\left(\frac{n}{X}\right)\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{(-\epsilon)}\zeta(1+w)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.06749pt\times\widetilde{W}(1-w)\left(\frac{c}{gQ}\right)^{w}\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{(\epsilon/2)}\mathcal{H}(z,-w)(mh)^{w+z}(nk)^{-z}\frac{e^{\delta z}-e^{-\delta z}}{2\delta z}

\displaystyle\hskip 36.135pt\times\prod_{p|mnhk/g^{2}}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+w}}\right)\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}p|g\\
p\nmid mnhk/g^{2}\end{subarray}}\left(1+\frac{1}{p^{w+1}(p-1)}-\frac{1}{p-1}\right)

(9.8)

\displaystyle\hskip 54.2025pt\times\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}p\nmid g\\
p\nmid mnhk/g^{2}\end{subarray}}\left(1+\frac{p^{-w}-1}{p(p-1)}\right)\,dz\,dw+O\big((XChk)^{\varepsilon}kX^{2}Q^{-97}\big).

We next add the
mh=nk
terms to complete the
m,n
-sum. Let us first show that their total, which is the above main term expression with the condition
mh\neq nk
replaced with
mh=nk
, is acceptably small. As we have seen in the discussion below (
8.3.4
),
mh=nk
if and only if
m=\ell K
and
n=\ell H
for some integer
\ell
. For such an
\ell
, the condition
(mn,c)=1
is equivalent to
(\ell,c)=1
because
(c,hk)=1
. Moreover, if
mh=nk
, then the definition
g=(mh,nk)
implies
g=mh=nk
. Thus the total contribution of the
mh=nk
terms is

\displaystyle\frac{Q}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq c\leq C\\
(c,hk)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(c)}{c}

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq\ell<\infty\\
(\ell,c)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A}(\ell K)\tau_{B}(\ell H)}{\ell\sqrt{HK}}V\left(\frac{\ell K}{X}\right)V\left(\frac{\ell H}{X}\right)\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{(-\epsilon)}\zeta(1+w)

\displaystyle\times\widetilde{W}(1-w)\left(\frac{c}{Q}\right)^{w}\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{(\epsilon/2)}\mathcal{H}(z,-w)\frac{e^{\delta z}-e^{-\delta z}}{2\delta z}

(9.9)

\displaystyle\hskip 36.135pt\times\prod_{p|\ell hk}\left(1+\frac{1}{p^{w+1}(p-1)}-\frac{1}{p-1}\right)\prod_{p\nmid\ell hk}\left(1+\frac{p^{-w}-1}{p(p-1)}\right)\,dz\,dw.

We may restrict the
\ell
sum to
1\leq\ell\ll X
because
V
is compactly supported. The product over
p|\ell hk
is bounded by
(hk\ell)^{\varepsilon}
, and the infinite product over
p\nmid\ell hk
is absolutely convergent since
\re(w)=-\epsilon
. Thus (
9.9
) is

(9.10)

\begin{split}&\ll Q^{1+\varepsilon}\sum_{1\leq c\leq C}\frac{1}{c^{1+\varepsilon}}\sum_{1\leq\ell\ll X}\frac{(hk\ell)^{\varepsilon}}{\ell\sqrt{HK}}\\
&\hskip 36.135pt\times\int_{(-\epsilon)}\int_{(\epsilon/2)}\left|\zeta(1+w)\widetilde{W}(1-w)\mathcal{H}(z,-w)\frac{e^{\delta z}-e^{-\delta z}}{2\delta z}\right|\,|dz|\,|dw|\end{split}

To bound the latter
w,z
-integral, observe that if
\re(w)=-\epsilon
and
\re(z)=\epsilon/2
, then (
3.10
) and (
3.15
) imply that
\widetilde{W}(1-w)\mathcal{H}(z,-w)
is
O(|w|^{-99}|z|^{\varepsilon-1})
for
|w-z|\geq|z|/2
, and is
O(|w|^{-99}|z|^{-99})
for
|w-z|\leq|z|/2
since
|w|\asymp|z|
and
|w-z|\geq\epsilon/2
in this case. Hence

(9.11)

\begin{split}\int_{(-\epsilon)}&\int_{(\epsilon/2)}\left|\zeta(1+w)\widetilde{W}(1-w)\mathcal{H}(z,-w)\frac{e^{\delta z}-e^{-\delta z}}{2\delta z}\right|\,|dz|\,|dw|\\
&\ll\int_{(-\epsilon)}\int_{(\epsilon/2)}|w|^{-98}|z|^{\varepsilon-1}\min\left\{1,\frac{1}{\delta|z|}\right\}\,|dz|\,|dw|\ll\left(\frac{1}{\delta}\right)^{\varepsilon}.\end{split}

From this, (
9.10
), and the definition (
3.4
) of
\delta
, we deduce that the total contribution of the
mh=nk
terms is

(9.12)

\ll Q^{1+\varepsilon}\sum_{1\leq c\leq C}\frac{1}{c^{1+\varepsilon}}\sum_{1\leq\ell\ll X}\frac{(hk\ell)^{\varepsilon}}{\ell\sqrt{HK}}\ll X^{\varepsilon}Q^{1+\varepsilon}\frac{(hk)^{\varepsilon}(h,k)}{(hk)^{1/2}}.

We now complete the
m,n
-sum in (
9.8
) by including the
mh=nk
terms. As we have just shown, this introduces an error of size (
9.12
). Then, we apply Mellin inversion to
V(m/X)
and
V(n/X)
and arrive at

(9.13)

\begin{split}\mathcal{U}^{2}(h,k)&=\frac{Q}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq c\leq C\\
(c,hk)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(c)}{c}\cdot\frac{1}{(2\pi i)^{2}}\int_{(2)}\int_{(2)}X^{s_{1}+s_{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq m,n<\infty\\
(mn,c)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A}(m)\tau_{B}(n)}{m^{\frac{1}{2}+s_{1}}n^{\frac{1}{2}+s_{2}}}\widetilde{V}(s_{1})\widetilde{V}(s_{2})\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{(-\epsilon)}\zeta(1+w)\widetilde{W}(1-w)\left(\frac{c}{gQ}\right)^{w}\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{(\epsilon/2)}\mathcal{H}(z,-w)(mh)^{w+z}(nk)^{-z}\\
&\hskip 36.135pt\times\frac{e^{\delta z}-e^{-\delta z}}{2\delta z}\prod_{p|mnhk/g^{2}}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+w}}\right)\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}p|g\\
p\nmid mnhk/g^{2}\end{subarray}}\left(1+\frac{1}{p^{w+1}(p-1)}-\frac{1}{p-1}\right)\\
&\hskip 54.2025pt\times\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}p\nmid g\\
p\nmid mnhk/g^{2}\end{subarray}}\left(1+\frac{p^{-w}-1}{p(p-1)}\right)\,dz\,dw\,ds_{2}\,ds_{1}\\
&\hskip 72.26999pt+O\left(X^{\varepsilon}Q^{1+\varepsilon}\frac{(hk)^{\varepsilon}(h,k)}{(hk)^{1/2}}+(XChk)^{\varepsilon}kX^{2}Q^{-97}\right).\end{split}

We have chosen the
s_{1}
- and
s_{2}
-lines to be at
\re(s_{1})=\re(s_{2})=2
to ensure the absolute convergence of the
m,n
-sum.

Our next task is to express the
m,n
-sum in (
9.13
) as an Euler product. This sum is

(9.14)

\begin{split}&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq m,n<\infty\\
(mn,c)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A}(m)\tau_{B}(n)}{m^{\frac{1}{2}+s_{1}}n^{\frac{1}{2}+s_{2}}}g^{-w}m^{w+z}n^{-z}\prod_{p|mnhk/g^{2}}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+w}}\right)\\
&\hskip 36.135pt\times\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}p|g\\
p\nmid mnhk/g^{2}\end{subarray}}\left(1+\frac{1}{p^{1+w}(p-1)}-\frac{1}{p-1}\right)\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}p\nmid g\\
p\nmid mnhk/g^{2}\end{subarray}}\left(1+\frac{p^{-w}-1}{p(p-1)}\right)\\
&=\sum_{1\leq m,n<\infty}\prod_{p}f(m,n,p),\end{split}

where
f(m,n,p)
is defined by

f(m,n,p):=F_{1}(m,n,p)F_{2}(m,n,p)F_{3}(m,n,p)

with
F_{1},F_{2},F_{3}
defined by

F_{1}(m,n,p):=\begin{cases}1&\text{if }p\nmid c\\
\\
1&\text{if }p|c\text{ and }\text{ord}_{p}(m)=\text{ord}_{p}(n)=0\\
\\
0&\text{if }p|c\text{ and }\text{ord}_{p}(mn)>0,\end{cases}

F_{2}(m,n,p):=\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{\text{ord}_{p}(m)})\tau_{B}(p^{\text{ord}_{p}(n)})}{p^{(\frac{1}{2}+s_{1}-w-z)\text{ord}_{p}(m)}p^{(\frac{1}{2}+s_{2}+z)\text{ord}_{p}(n)}p^{w\min\{\text{ord}_{p}(m)+\text{ord}_{p}(h),\text{ord}_{p}(n)+\text{ord}_{p}(k)\}}},

and

F_{3}(m,n,p):=\begin{cases}\displaystyle 1-\frac{1}{p^{1+w}}&\text{if }p|\frac{mnhk}{g^{2}}\\
\\
\displaystyle 1+\frac{1}{p^{1+w}(p-1)}-\frac{1}{p-1}&\text{if }p|g\text{ and }p\nmid\frac{mnhk}{g^{2}}\\
\\
\displaystyle 1+\frac{p^{-w}-1}{p(p-1)}&\text{if }p\nmid\frac{mnhk}{g},\end{cases}

respectively. We can rewrite the conditions in
F_{3}(m,n,p)
in terms of
\text{ord}_{p}(m)
,
\text{ord}_{p}(n)
,
\text{ord}_{p}(h)
, and
\text{ord}_{p}(k)
, as follows. Since
g=(mh,nk)
, a prime
p
divides
mnhk/g^{2}
if and only if

\text{ord}_{p}(m)+\text{ord}_{p}(h)+\text{ord}_{p}(n)+\text{ord}_{p}(k)-2\min\{\text{ord}_{p}(m)+\text{ord}_{p}(h),\text{ord}_{p}(n)+\text{ord}_{p}(k)\}>0.

Since two real numbers
x,y
satisfy
x+y-2\min\{x,y\}>0
if and only if
x\neq y
, it follows that
p|mnhk/g^{2}
if and only if
\text{ord}_{p}(m)+\text{ord}_{p}(h)\neq\text{ord}_{p}(n)+\text{ord}_{p}(k)
. A similar argument shows that a prime
p
satisfies
p\nmid mnhk/g
if and only if
p\nmid mhnk
. Thus the definition of
F_{3}(m,n,p)
is equivalent to

F_{3}(m,n,p)=\begin{cases}\displaystyle 1-\frac{1}{p^{1+w}}&\text{if }\text{ord}_{p}(m)+\text{ord}_{p}(h)\neq\text{ord}_{p}(n)+\text{ord}_{p}(k)\\
\\
\displaystyle 1+\frac{1}{p^{1+w}(p-1)}-\frac{1}{p-1}&\text{if }\text{ord}_{p}(m)+\text{ord}_{p}(h)=\text{ord}_{p}(n)+\text{ord}_{p}(k)>0\\
\\
\displaystyle 1+\frac{p^{-w}-1}{p(p-1)}&\text{if }\text{ord}_{p}(m)+\text{ord}_{p}(h)=\text{ord}_{p}(n)+\text{ord}_{p}(k)=0.\end{cases}

If
p|c
, then
F_{1}(m,n,p)=0
unless
m=n=1
, in which case

f(1,1,p)=F_{1}(1,1,p)F_{2}(1,1,p)F_{3}(1,1,p)=1+\frac{p^{-w}-1}{p(p-1)}

because
(c,hk)=1
. Thus, from (
9.14
) and Lemma
3.2
, we deduce that the
m,n
-sum in (
9.13
) equals

\displaystyle\prod_{p}\sum_{0\leq m,n<\infty}f(p^{m},p^{n},p)

\displaystyle=\prod_{p|c}\left(1+\frac{p^{-w}-1}{p(p-1)}\right)\prod_{p\nmid c}\sum_{0\leq m,n<\infty}F_{2}(m,n,p)F_{3}(m,n,p)

\displaystyle=\prod_{p|c}\left(1+\frac{p^{-w}-1}{p(p-1)}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.06749pt\times\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}p\nmid c\\
p|hk\end{subarray}}\Bigg(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m,n<\infty\\
m+\text{ord}_{p}(h)=n+\text{ord}_{p}(k)\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})\left(1+\frac{1}{p^{1+w}(p-1)}-\frac{1}{p-1}\right)}{p^{m(\frac{1}{2}+s_{1}-w-z)}p^{n(\frac{1}{2}+s_{2}+z)}p^{w\min\{m+\text{ord}_{p}(h),n+\text{ord}_{p}(k)\}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 54.2025pt+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m,n<\infty\\
m+\text{ord}_{p}(h)\neq n+\text{ord}_{p}(k)\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+w}}\right)}{p^{m(\frac{1}{2}+s_{1}-w-z)}p^{n(\frac{1}{2}+s_{2}+z)}p^{w\min\{m+\text{ord}_{p}(h),n+\text{ord}_{p}(k)\}}}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.06749pt\times\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}p\nmid c\\
p\nmid hk\end{subarray}}\Bigg(1+\frac{p^{-w}-1}{p(p-1)}+\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{m})\left(1+\frac{1}{p^{1+w}(p-1)}-\frac{1}{p-1}\right)}{p^{m(1+s_{1}+s_{2})}}

\displaystyle\hskip 54.2025pt+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m,n<\infty\\
m\neq n\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+w}}\right)}{p^{m(\frac{1}{2}+s_{1}-w-z)}p^{n(\frac{1}{2}+s_{2}+z)}p^{w\min\{m,n\}}}\Bigg).

We substitute this for the
m,n
-sum in (
9.13
). For convenience, we also make a change of variables
w\mapsto 1-w
. The result is

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}^{2}(h,k)

\displaystyle=\frac{Q}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq c\leq C\\
(c,hk)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(c)}{c}\cdot\frac{1}{(2\pi i)^{4}}\int_{(2)}\int_{(2)}X^{s_{1}+s_{2}}\widetilde{V}(s_{1})\widetilde{V}(s_{2})\int_{(1+\epsilon)}\zeta(2-w)\widetilde{W}(w)\left(\frac{c}{Q}\right)^{1-w}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.06749pt\times\int_{(\epsilon/2)}\mathcal{H}(z,w-1)\frac{e^{\delta z}-e^{-\delta z}}{2\delta z}h^{1-w+z}k^{-z}\prod_{p|c}\left(1+\frac{p^{w-1}-1}{p(p-1)}\right)

\displaystyle\times\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}p\nmid c\\
p|hk\end{subarray}}\Bigg(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m,n<\infty\\
m+\text{ord}_{p}(h)=n+\text{ord}_{p}(k)\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})\left(1+\frac{p^{w}}{p^{2}(p-1)}-\frac{1}{p-1}\right)}{p^{m(-\frac{1}{2}+s_{1}+w-z)}p^{n(\frac{1}{2}+s_{2}+z)}p^{(1-w)\min\{m+\text{ord}_{p}(h),n+\text{ord}_{p}(k)\}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 36.135pt+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m,n<\infty\\
m+\text{ord}_{p}(h)\neq n+\text{ord}_{p}(k)\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})\left(1-\frac{p^{w}}{p^{2}}\right)}{p^{m(-\frac{1}{2}+s_{1}+w-z)}p^{n(\frac{1}{2}+s_{2}+z)}p^{(1-w)\min\{m+\text{ord}_{p}(h),n+\text{ord}_{p}(k)\}}}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\times\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}p\nmid c\\
p\nmid hk\end{subarray}}\Bigg(1+\frac{p^{w-1}-1}{p(p-1)}+\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{m})\left(1+\frac{p^{w}}{p^{2}(p-1)}-\frac{1}{p-1}\right)}{p^{m(1+s_{1}+s_{2})}}

\displaystyle\hskip 36.135pt+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m,n<\infty\\
m\neq n\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})\left(1-\frac{p^{w}}{p^{2}}\right)}{p^{m(-\frac{1}{2}+s_{1}+w-z)}p^{n(\frac{1}{2}+s_{2}+z)}p^{(1-w)\min\{m,n\}}}\Bigg)\,dz\,dw\,ds_{2}\,ds_{1}

(9.15)

\displaystyle+O\left(X^{\varepsilon}Q^{1+\varepsilon}\frac{(hk)^{\varepsilon}(h,k)}{(hk)^{1/2}}+(XChk)^{\varepsilon}kX^{2}Q^{-97}\right).

9.1.
Analysis of the predicted one-swap terms from the recipe

Before we continue our treatment of
\mathcal{U}^{2}(h,k)
, we first break down the predicted one-swap terms into several parts via the residue theorem. Afterward, we will show that
\mathcal{U}^{2}(h,k)
is equal to the sum of the same parts plus admissible error terms.

Recall that the definition of
\mathcal{I}_{1}^{*}(h,k)
is given by (
4.8
) with
\ell=1
. For each term in the definition of
\mathcal{I}_{1}^{*}(h,k)
, we denote the element of
U
by
\alpha
and the element of
V
by
\beta
, and we multiply the integrand by
\zeta(w-1+\alpha+s_{1}+\beta+s_{2})
and divide it by the Euler product of
\zeta(w-1+\alpha+s_{1}+\beta+s_{2})
. This “factoring out” of the zeta-function gives us a further analytic continuation of the integrand and allows us to evaluate its residues when shifting contours. With these notations and factorization, we thus write
\mathcal{I}_{1}^{*}(h,k)
as

(9.1.1)

\begin{split}\mathcal{I}_{1}^{*}(h,k)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in A\\
\beta\in B\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{2(2\pi i)^{3}}\int_{(\varepsilon)}\int_{(\varepsilon)}\int_{(2+\varepsilon)}X^{s_{1}+s_{2}}Q^{w}\widetilde{V}(s_{1})\widetilde{V}(s_{2})\widetilde{W}(w)\\
\times\mathscr{X}(\tfrac{1}{2}+\alpha+s_{1})\mathscr{X}(\tfrac{1}{2}+\beta+s_{2})\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in A_{s_{1}}\smallsetminus\{\alpha+s_{1}\}\cup\{-\beta-s_{2}\}\\
\delta\in B_{s_{2}}\smallsetminus\{\beta+s_{2}\}\cup\{-\alpha-s_{1}\}\end{subarray}}\zeta(1+\gamma+\delta)\\
\times\zeta(w-1+\alpha+s_{1}+\beta+s_{2})\mathcal{K}(s_{1},s_{2},w)\,dw\,ds_{2}\,ds_{1},\end{split}

where
\mathcal{K}(s_{1},s_{2},w)
is defined by

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}(s_{1},s_{2},w)=

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}(s_{1},s_{2},w;A,B,\alpha,\beta,h,k)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\prod_{p|hk}\Bigg\{\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{w-1+\alpha+s_{1}+\beta+s_{2}}}\right)\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in A_{s_{1}}\smallsetminus\{\alpha+s_{1}\}\cup\{-\beta-s_{2}\}\\
\delta\in B_{s_{2}}\smallsetminus\{\beta+s_{2}\}\cup\{-\alpha-s_{1}\}\end{subarray}}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+\gamma+\delta}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 36.135pt\times\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m,n<\infty\\
m+\text{ord}_{p}(h)=n+\text{ord}_{p}(k)\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A_{s_{1}}\smallsetminus\{\alpha+s_{1}\}\cup\{-\beta-s_{2}\}}(p^{m})\tau_{B_{s_{2}}\smallsetminus\{\beta+s_{2}\}\cup\{-\alpha-s_{1}\}}(p^{n})}{p^{m/2}p^{n/2}}\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{p\nmid hk}\Bigg\{\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{w-1+\alpha+s_{1}+\beta+s_{2}}}\right)\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in A_{s_{1}}\smallsetminus\{\alpha+s_{1}\}\cup\{-\beta-s_{2}\}\\
\delta\in B_{s_{2}}\smallsetminus\{\beta+s_{2}\}\cup\{-\alpha-s_{1}\}\end{subarray}}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+\gamma+\delta}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 36.135pt\times\Bigg(1+\frac{p-2}{p^{w+\alpha+s_{1}+\beta+s_{2}}}+\left(1-\frac{1}{p}\right)^{2}\frac{p^{2(1-w-\alpha-s_{1}-\beta-s_{2})}}{1-p^{1-w-\alpha-s_{1}-\beta-s_{2}}}

(9.1.2)

\displaystyle\hskip 54.2025pt+\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\frac{\tau_{A_{s_{1}}\smallsetminus\{\alpha+s_{1}\}\cup\{-\beta-s_{2}\}}(p^{m})\tau_{B_{s_{2}}\smallsetminus\{\beta+s_{2}\}\cup\{-\alpha-s_{1}\}}(p^{m})}{p^{m}}\Bigg)\Bigg\}.

To facilitate our estimations, we first prove the following lemma, which will allow us to move lines of integration and bound the integrals that remain after applying the residue theorem.

Lemma 9.3
.

Suppose that
\epsilon>0
is arbitrarily small. Let
\alpha\in A
and
\beta\in B
, and let
h
and
k
be positive integers. If
s_{1},s_{2},w
are complex numbers such that

(i)

\re(w-1+\alpha+s_{1}+\beta+s_{2})\geq\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon
,

(ii)

-\frac{1}{2}+5\epsilon\leq\re(s_{1}+s_{2})\leq 2\epsilon
, and

(iii)

either
|\re(s_{1})|\leq\epsilon
or
|\re(s_{2})|\leq\epsilon
,

then the product (
9.1.2
) defining
\mathcal{K}(s_{1},s_{2},w;A,B,\alpha,\beta,h,k)
converges absolutely and we have

\mathcal{K}(s_{1},s_{2},w;A,B,\alpha,\beta,h,k)\ll_{\varepsilon}(hk)^{\varepsilon}.

Proof.

Since
\re(w-1+\alpha+s_{1}+\beta+s_{2})\geq\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon
, we have

(9.1.3)

\frac{1}{p^{w-1+\alpha+s_{1}+\beta+s_{2}}}\ll\frac{1}{p^{\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}}.

Moreover, each term of the form
p^{-1-\gamma-\delta}
in the definition (
9.1.2
) of
\mathcal{K}(s_{1},s_{2},w)
satisfies
p^{-1-\gamma-\delta}\ll p^{-\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}
because
-\frac{1}{2}+5\epsilon\leq\re(s_{1}+s_{2})\leq 2\epsilon
and each element of
A\cup B
is
\ll 1/\log Q
. We may thus multiply out the product and apply the definition (
3.1
) of
\tau_{E}
to deduce that

(9.1.4)

\begin{split}&\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{w-1+\alpha+s_{1}+\beta+s_{2}}}\right)\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in A_{s_{1}}\smallsetminus\{\alpha+s_{1}\}\cup\{-\beta-s_{2}\}\\
\delta\in B_{s_{2}}\smallsetminus\{\beta+s_{2}\}\cup\{-\alpha-s_{1}\}\end{subarray}}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+\gamma+\delta}}\right)\\
&=1-\frac{1}{p^{w-1+\alpha+s_{1}+\beta+s_{2}}}-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in A_{s_{1}}\smallsetminus\{\alpha+s_{1}\}\cup\{-\beta-s_{2}\}\\
\delta\in B_{s_{2}}\smallsetminus\{\beta+s_{2}\}\cup\{-\alpha-s_{1}\}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{p^{1+\gamma+\delta}}+O\left(\frac{1}{p^{1+\varepsilon}}\right)\\
&=1-\frac{1}{p^{w-1+\alpha+s_{1}+\beta+s_{2}}}-\frac{\tau_{A_{s_{1}}\smallsetminus\{\alpha+s_{1}\}\cup\{-\beta-s_{2}\}}(p)\tau_{B_{s_{2}}\smallsetminus\{\beta+s_{2}\}\cup\{-\alpha-s_{1}\}}(p)}{p}+O\left(\frac{1}{p^{1+\varepsilon}}\right).\end{split}

We may assume that
|\re(s_{2})|\leq\epsilon
as the proof for the case with
|\re(s_{1})|\leq\epsilon
is similar. Since
-\frac{1}{2}+5\epsilon\leq\re(s_{1}+s_{2})\leq 2\epsilon
, it then follows that
-\frac{1}{2}+4\epsilon\leq\re(s_{1})\leq 3\epsilon
. This, the inequality
|\re(s_{2})|\leq\epsilon
, and the bound (
3.2
) imply

(9.1.5)

\tau_{A_{s_{1}}\smallsetminus\{\alpha+s_{1}\}\cup\{-\beta-s_{2}\}}(p^{m})\ll_{\varepsilon}p^{m(\frac{1}{2}-4\epsilon+\varepsilon)},

and

(9.1.6)

\tau_{B_{s_{2}}\smallsetminus\{\beta+s_{2}\}\cup\{-\alpha-s_{1}\}}(p^{n})\ll_{\varepsilon}p^{n(3\epsilon+\varepsilon)}.

Therefore

(9.1.7)

\sum_{m=2}^{\infty}\frac{\tau_{A_{s_{1}}\smallsetminus\{\alpha+s_{1}\}\cup\{-\beta-s_{2}\}}(p^{m})\tau_{B_{s_{2}}\smallsetminus\{\beta+s_{2}\}\cup\{-\alpha-s_{1}\}}(p^{m})}{p^{m}}\ll\sum_{m=2}^{\infty}\frac{p^{m(\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon+\varepsilon)}}{p^{m}}\ll\frac{1}{p^{1+\varepsilon}}

and

(9.1.8)

\begin{split}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m,n<\infty\\
m+\text{ord}_{p}(h)=n+\text{ord}_{p}(k)\end{subarray}}&\frac{\tau_{A_{s_{1}}\smallsetminus\{\alpha+s_{1}\}\cup\{-\beta-s_{2}\}}(p^{m})\tau_{B_{s_{2}}\smallsetminus\{\beta+s_{2}\}\cup\{-\alpha-s_{1}\}}(p^{n})}{p^{m/2}p^{n/2}}\\
&\hskip 36.135pt\ll\sum_{0\leq m,n<\infty}\frac{p^{m(\frac{1}{2}-4\epsilon+\varepsilon)}p^{n(3\epsilon+\varepsilon)}}{p^{m/2}p^{n/2}}\\
&\hskip 36.135pt\ll 1.\end{split}

From (
9.1.3
), (
9.1.4
), (
9.1.5
) with
m=1
, (
9.1.6
) with
n=1
, and (
9.1.8
), we deduce that if
p|hk
then the local factor in (
9.1.2
) corresponding to
p
is
O(1)
. On the other hand, from (
9.1.3
), (
9.1.4
), and (
9.1.7
), we deduce that if
p\nmid hk
then the local factor in (
9.1.2
) corresponding to
p
is
1+O(p^{-1-\varepsilon})
. It follows that the right-hand side of (
9.1.2
) converges absolutely, and is
\ll(hk)^{\varepsilon}
since
\prod_{p|\nu}O(1)\ll{\nu}^{\varepsilon}
for any positive integer
\nu
.
∎

We now move the
w
-line in (
9.1.1
) to
\re(w)=\frac{3}{2}+\varepsilon
. This leaves a residue from the pole at
w=2-\alpha-s_{1}-\beta-s_{2}
. To bound the new integral that has
\re(w)=\frac{3}{2}+\varepsilon
, we use Lemma
9.3
, (
3.6
), and (
3.10
). Since the residue of
\zeta(s)
at
s=1
is
1
, we arrive at

(9.1.9)

\mathcal{I}_{1}^{*}(h,k)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in A\\
\beta\in B\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{2(2\pi i)^{2}}\int_{(\epsilon)}\int_{(\epsilon)}\mathcal{J}\,ds_{2}\,ds_{1}+O\big(X^{\varepsilon}Q^{\frac{3}{2}+\varepsilon}(hk)^{\varepsilon}\big),

where, for brevity, we define
\mathcal{J}
by

(9.1.10)

\begin{split}\mathcal{J}:=X^{s_{1}+s_{2}}Q^{2-\alpha-s_{1}-\beta-s_{2}}\widetilde{V}(s_{1})\widetilde{V}(s_{2})\widetilde{W}(2-\alpha-s_{1}-\beta-s_{2})\\
\times\mathscr{X}(\tfrac{1}{2}+\alpha+s_{1})\mathscr{X}(\tfrac{1}{2}+\beta+s_{2})\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in A_{s_{1}}\smallsetminus\{\alpha+s_{1}\}\cup\{-\beta-s_{2}\}\\
\delta\in B_{s_{2}}\smallsetminus\{\beta+s_{2}\}\cup\{-\alpha-s_{1}\}\end{subarray}}\zeta(1+\gamma+\delta)\\
\times\mathcal{K}(s_{1},s_{2},2-\alpha-s_{1}-\beta-s_{2}).\end{split}

Notice that we have now specified the lines of integration in (
9.1.9
) to be
\re(s_{1})=\epsilon
and
\re(s_{2})=\epsilon
, with
\epsilon
fixed and arbitrarily small. The purpose of this is to make the succeeding estimations more explicit.

Next, we move the
s_{2}
-line in (
9.1.9
) to
\re(s_{2})=-\frac{1}{2}+5\epsilon
. This leaves residues from the pole at
s_{2}=0
due to the factor
\widetilde{V}(s_{2})
, the pole at
s_{2}=-s_{1}-\alpha-\beta
due to the factor
\zeta(1-\alpha-s_{1}-\beta-s_{2})
, and the poles at
s_{2}=-s_{1}-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime}
due to the factors
\zeta(1+\alpha^{\prime}+s_{1}+\beta^{\prime}+s_{2})
, where
\alpha^{\prime}
runs through the elements of
A\smallsetminus\{\alpha\}
and
\beta^{\prime}
runs through the elements of
B\smallsetminus\{\beta\}
. To bound the new integral that has
\re(s_{2})=-\frac{1}{2}+5\epsilon
, we use Lemma
9.3
, (
3.6
), and (
3.10
). We arrive at

\begin{split}\mathcal{I}_{1}^{*}(h,k)&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in A\\
\beta\in B\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{4\pi i}\int_{(\epsilon)}\underset{s_{2}=0}{\text{Res}}\ \mathcal{J}\,ds_{1}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in A\\
\beta\in B\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{4\pi i}\int_{(\epsilon)}\underset{s_{2}=-s_{1}-\alpha-\beta}{\text{Res}}\ \mathcal{J}\,ds_{1}\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in A\\
\beta\in B\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha^{\prime}\neq\alpha\\
\beta^{\prime}\neq\beta\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{4\pi i}\int_{(\epsilon)}\underset{s_{2}=-s_{1}-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime}}{\text{Res}}\ \mathcal{J}\,ds_{1}\\
&\hskip 36.135pt+O\big(X^{-\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}Q^{\frac{5}{2}}(hk)^{\varepsilon}\big)+O\big(X^{\varepsilon}Q^{\frac{3}{2}+\varepsilon}(hk)^{\varepsilon}\big).\end{split}

For brevity, write this as

(9.1.11)

\mathcal{I}_{1}^{*}(h,k)=J_{1}+J_{2}+J_{3}+O\big(X^{-\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}Q^{\frac{5}{2}}(hk)^{\varepsilon}\big)+O\big(X^{\varepsilon}Q^{\frac{3}{2}+\varepsilon}(hk)^{\varepsilon}\big).

We first evaluate the contribution
J_{1}
of the residue at
s_{2}=0
. By (
3.9
) and the definition (
9.1.10
) of
\mathcal{J}
, we have

(9.1.12)

\begin{split}\underset{s_{2}=0}{\text{Res}}\ \mathcal{J}&=X^{s_{1}}Q^{2-\alpha-s_{1}-\beta}\widetilde{V}(s_{1})\widetilde{W}(2-\alpha-s_{1}-\beta)\mathscr{X}(\tfrac{1}{2}+\alpha+s_{1})\mathscr{X}(\tfrac{1}{2}+\beta)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in A_{s_{1}}\smallsetminus\{\alpha+s_{1}\}\cup\{-\beta\}\\
\delta\in B\smallsetminus\{\beta\}\cup\{-\alpha-s_{1}\}\end{subarray}}\zeta(1+\gamma+\delta)\mathcal{K}(s_{1},0,2-\alpha-s_{1}-\beta).\end{split}

We move the line of integration in the definition

J_{1}:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in A\\
\beta\in B\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{4\pi i}\int_{(\epsilon)}\underset{s_{2}=0}{\text{Res}}\ \mathcal{J}\,ds_{1}

to
\re(s_{1})=-\frac{1}{2}+5\epsilon
. We find residues from the pole at
s_{1}=0
due to the factor
\widetilde{V}(s_{1})
in (
9.1.12
), the pole at
s_{1}=-\alpha-\beta
due to the factor
\zeta(1-\alpha-s_{1}-\beta)
, and the poles at
s_{1}=-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime}
due to the factors
\zeta(1+\alpha^{\prime}+s_{1}+\beta^{\prime})
, where
\alpha^{\prime}
runs through the elements of
A\smallsetminus\{\alpha\}
and
\beta^{\prime}
runs through the elements of
B\smallsetminus\{\beta\}
. To bound the new integral that has
\re(s_{1})=-\frac{1}{2}+5\epsilon
, we use Lemma
9.3
, (
3.6
), and (
3.10
). We deduce that

\begin{split}J_{1}&=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in A\\
\beta\in B\end{subarray}}\underset{s_{1}=0}{\text{Res}}\ \underset{s_{2}=0}{\text{Res}}\ \mathcal{J}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in A\\
\beta\in B\end{subarray}}\underset{s_{1}=-\alpha-\beta}{\text{Res}}\ \underset{s_{2}=0}{\text{Res}}\ \mathcal{J}\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in A\\
\beta\in B\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha^{\prime}\neq\alpha\\
\beta^{\prime}\neq\beta\end{subarray}}\underset{s_{1}=-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime}}{\text{Res}}\ \underset{s_{2}=0}{\text{Res}}\ \mathcal{J}+O\big(X^{-\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}Q^{\frac{5}{2}}(hk)^{\varepsilon}\big).\end{split}

For brevity, we write this as

(9.1.13)

J_{1}=J_{11}+J_{12}+J_{13}+O\big(X^{-\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}Q^{\frac{5}{2}}(hk)^{\varepsilon}\big).

We deduce from (
3.9
) and (
9.1.12
) that

(9.1.14)

\begin{split}J_{11}&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in A\\
\beta\in B\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{2}Q^{2-\alpha-\beta}\widetilde{W}(2-\alpha-\beta)\mathscr{X}(\tfrac{1}{2}+\alpha)\mathscr{X}(\tfrac{1}{2}+\beta)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in A\smallsetminus\{\alpha\}\cup\{-\beta\}\\
\delta\in B\smallsetminus\{\beta\}\cup\{-\alpha\}\end{subarray}}\zeta(1+\gamma+\delta)\mathcal{K}(0,0,2-\alpha-\beta).\end{split}

Since
\mathscr{X}(\tfrac{1}{2}-\beta)\mathscr{X}(\tfrac{1}{2}+\beta)=1
by the definition (
3.11
) of
\mathscr{X}
and the residue of
\zeta(1-\alpha-s_{1}-\beta)
at
s_{1}=-\alpha-\beta
is
-1
, it follows from (
9.1.12
) that term
J_{12}
in (
9.1.13
) equals

(9.1.15)

\begin{split}J_{12}&=-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in A\\
\beta\in B\end{subarray}}X^{-\alpha-\beta}Q^{2}\widetilde{V}(-\alpha-\beta)\widetilde{W}(2)\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha\\
\hat{\beta}\neq\beta\end{subarray}}\zeta(1-\alpha-\beta+\hat{\alpha}+\hat{\beta})\\
&\times\prod_{\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha}\zeta(1+\hat{\alpha}-\alpha)\prod_{\hat{\beta}\neq\beta}\zeta(1+\hat{\beta}-\beta)\mathcal{K}(-\alpha-\beta,0,2).\end{split}

Next, since the residue of
\zeta(1+\alpha^{\prime}+s_{1}+\beta^{\prime})
at
s_{1}=-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime}
is
1
, it follows from (
9.1.12
) that the term
J_{13}
in (
9.1.13
) equals

(9.1.16)

\begin{split}&J_{13}=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in A\\
\beta\in B\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha^{\prime}\neq\alpha\\
\beta^{\prime}\neq\beta\end{subarray}}X^{-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime}}Q^{2-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime}}\widetilde{V}(-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime})\widetilde{W}(2-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime})\\
&\hskip 7.22743pt\times\mathscr{X}(\tfrac{1}{2}+\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime})\mathscr{X}(\tfrac{1}{2}+\beta)\zeta(1-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime})\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha\\
\hat{\beta}\neq\beta\\
(\hat{\alpha},\hat{\beta})\neq(\alpha^{\prime},\beta^{\prime})\end{subarray}}\zeta(1-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime}+\hat{\alpha}+\hat{\beta})\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\prod_{\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha}\zeta(1+\hat{\alpha}-\alpha)\prod_{\hat{\beta}\neq\beta}\zeta(1+\hat{\beta}-\beta)\mathcal{K}(-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime},0,2-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime}).\end{split}

This, (
9.1.13
), (
9.1.14
), and (
9.1.15
) complete our evaluation of
J_{1}
.

Having estimated
J_{1}
, we next turn to the integral
J_{2}
from (
9.1.11
). Recall its definition

(9.1.17)

J_{2}:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in A\\
\beta\in B\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{4\pi i}\int_{(\epsilon)}\underset{s_{2}=-s_{1}-\alpha-\beta}{\text{Res}}\ \mathcal{J}\,ds_{1}.

Since
\mathscr{X}(\tfrac{1}{2}+\alpha+s_{1})\mathscr{X}(\tfrac{1}{2}-\alpha-s_{1})=1
by the definition (
3.11
) of
\mathscr{X}
and the residue of
\zeta(1-\alpha-s_{1}-\beta-s_{2})
at
s_{2}=-s_{1}-\alpha-\beta
is
-1
, we see from the definition (
9.1.10
) of
\mathcal{J}
that

(9.1.18)

\begin{split}\underset{s_{2}=-s_{1}-\alpha-\beta}{\text{Res}}\ \mathcal{J}&=-X^{-\alpha-\beta}Q^{2}\widetilde{V}(s_{1})\widetilde{V}(-s_{1}-\alpha-\beta)\widetilde{W}(2)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha\\
\hat{\beta}\neq\beta\end{subarray}}\zeta(1+\hat{\alpha}-\alpha+\hat{\beta}-\beta)\prod_{\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha}\zeta(1+\hat{\alpha}-\alpha)\prod_{\hat{\beta}\neq\beta}\zeta(1+\hat{\beta}-\beta)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\mathcal{K}(s_{1},-s_{1}-\alpha-\beta,2).\end{split}

We move the line of integration in (
9.1.17
) to
\re(s_{1})=-\epsilon-\re(\alpha)-\re(\beta)
. This leaves residues from the poles at
s_{1}=0
and
s_{1}=-\alpha-\beta
due to the factors
\widetilde{V}(s_{1})
and
\widetilde{V}(-s_{1}-\alpha-\beta)
in (
9.1.18
), and we arrive at

\begin{split}J_{2}=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in A\\
\beta\in B\end{subarray}}\underset{s_{1}=0}{\text{Res}}\ \underset{s_{2}=-s_{1}-\alpha-\beta}{\text{Res}}\ \mathcal{J}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in A\\
\beta\in B\end{subarray}}\underset{s_{1}=-\alpha-\beta}{\text{Res}}\ \underset{s_{2}=-s_{1}-\alpha-\beta}{\text{Res}}\ \mathcal{J}\\
+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in A\\
\beta\in B\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{4\pi i}\int_{(-\epsilon-\re(\alpha)-\re(\beta))}\underset{s_{2}=-s_{1}-\alpha-\beta}{\text{Res}}\ \mathcal{J}\,ds_{1}.\end{split}

For brevity, we write this as

(9.1.19)

J_{2}=J_{21}+J_{22}+J_{23}.

Since the residue of
\widetilde{V}(s)
at
s=0
is
1
by (
3.9
), it follows from (
9.1.18
) that

(9.1.20)

\begin{split}J_{21}&=-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in A\\
\beta\in B\end{subarray}}X^{-\alpha-\beta}Q^{2}\widetilde{V}(-\alpha-\beta)\widetilde{W}(2)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha\\
\hat{\beta}\neq\beta\end{subarray}}\zeta(1+\hat{\alpha}-\alpha+\hat{\beta}-\beta)\prod_{\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha}\zeta(1+\hat{\alpha}-\alpha)\prod_{\hat{\beta}\neq\beta}\zeta(1+\hat{\beta}-\beta)\mathcal{K}(0,-\alpha-\beta,2).\end{split}

Similarly, since the residue of
\widetilde{V}(-s_{1}-\alpha-\beta)
at
s_{1}=-\alpha-\beta
is
-1
by (
3.9
), we see from (
9.1.18
) that the term
J_{22}
in (
9.1.19
) equals

(9.1.21)

\begin{split}J_{22}&=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in A\\
\beta\in B\end{subarray}}X^{-\alpha-\beta}Q^{2}\widetilde{V}(-\alpha-\beta)\widetilde{W}(2)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha\\
\hat{\beta}\neq\beta\end{subarray}}\zeta(1+\hat{\alpha}-\alpha+\hat{\beta}-\beta)\prod_{\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha}\zeta(1+\hat{\alpha}-\alpha)\prod_{\hat{\beta}\neq\beta}\zeta(1+\hat{\beta}-\beta)\mathcal{K}(-\alpha-\beta,0,2).\end{split}

To evaluate the integral
J_{23}
in (
9.1.19
), we first prove the following lemma that gives a functional equation for
\mathcal{K}
.

Lemma 9.4
.

Suppose that
\epsilon>0
is arbitrarily small. Let
\alpha\in A
and
\beta\in B
, and let
h
and
k
be positive integers. If
s_{1},s_{2},w
are complex numbers satisfying the conditions (i)–(iii) in Lemma
9.3
,
then

\mathcal{K}(s_{1},s_{2},w;A,B,\alpha,\beta,h,k)=\left(\frac{h}{k}\right)^{s_{1}}\mathcal{K}(0,s_{1}+s_{2},w;A,B,\alpha,\beta,h,k).

Proof.

Lemma
9.3
guarantees that the product (
9.1.2
) defining
\mathcal{K}(s_{1},s_{2},w)
converges absolutely and thus
\mathcal{K}(s_{1},s_{2},w)
is well-defined. Now (
3.3
) implies that

(9.1.22)

\begin{split}&\tau_{A_{s_{1}}\smallsetminus\{\alpha+s_{1}\}\cup\{-\beta-s_{2}\}}(p^{m})\tau_{B_{s_{2}}\smallsetminus\{\beta+s_{2}\}\cup\{-\alpha-s_{1}\}}(p^{m})\\
&=\tau_{A\smallsetminus\{\alpha\}\cup\{-\beta-s_{1}-s_{2}\}}(p^{m})\tau_{B_{s_{1}+s_{2}}\smallsetminus\{\beta+s_{1}+s_{2}\}\cup\{-\alpha\}}(p^{m}).\end{split}

Similarly, if
m+\text{ord}_{p}(h)=n+\text{ord}_{p}(k)
, then (
3.3
) implies

(9.1.23)

\begin{split}\tau_{A_{s_{1}}\smallsetminus\{\alpha+s_{1}\}\cup\{-\beta-s_{2}\}}(p^{m})&\tau_{B_{s_{2}}\smallsetminus\{\beta+s_{2}\}\cup\{-\alpha-s_{1}\}}(p^{n})\\
&=p^{s_{1}(n-m)}\tau_{A\smallsetminus\{\alpha\}\cup\{-\beta-s_{1}-s_{2}\}}(p^{m})\tau_{B_{s_{1}+s_{2}}\smallsetminus\{\beta+s_{1}+s_{2}\}\cup\{-\alpha\}}(p^{n})\\
&=p^{s_{1}(\text{ord}_{p}(h)-\text{ord}_{p}(k))}\tau_{A\smallsetminus\{\alpha\}\cup\{-\beta-s_{1}-s_{2}\}}(p^{m})\tau_{B_{s_{1}+s_{2}}\smallsetminus\{\beta+s_{1}+s_{2}\}\cup\{-\alpha\}}(p^{n}).\end{split}

Also, we have

\gamma+\delta=(\gamma-s_{1})+(\delta+s_{1}).

Lemma
9.4
follows from this, (
9.1.22
), (
9.1.23
), the definition (
9.1.2
) of
\mathcal{K}
, and the fact that

\prod_{p|hk}p^{s_{1}(\text{ord}_{p}(h)-\text{ord}_{p}(k))}=\left(\frac{h}{k}\right)^{s_{1}}.

∎

We now evaluate the integral
J_{23}
in (
9.1.19
). Lemma
9.4
implies for
\re(s_{1})=-\epsilon-\re(\alpha)-\re(\beta)
that

(9.1.24)

\mathcal{K}(s_{1},-s_{1}-\alpha-\beta,2)=\left(\frac{h}{k}\right)^{s_{1}}\mathcal{K}(0,-\alpha-\beta,2).

Moreover, a change of variables
s_{1}\mapsto-s-\alpha-\beta
gives

\int_{(-\epsilon-\re(\alpha)-\re(\beta))}\widetilde{V}(s_{1})\widetilde{V}(-s_{1}-\alpha-\beta)\left(\frac{h}{k}\right)^{s_{1}}\,ds_{1}=\int_{(\epsilon)}\widetilde{V}(-s-\alpha-\beta)\widetilde{V}(s)\left(\frac{h}{k}\right)^{-s-\alpha-\beta}\,ds.

From this, (
9.1.24
), and (
9.1.18
), we deduce that the integral
J_{23}
in (
9.1.19
) equals

(9.1.25)

\begin{split}J_{23}&=-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in A\\
\beta\in B\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{4\pi i}\int_{(\epsilon)}X^{-\alpha-\beta}Q^{2}\widetilde{V}(s)\widetilde{V}(-s-\alpha-\beta)\widetilde{W}(2)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha\\
\hat{\beta}\neq\beta\end{subarray}}\zeta(1+\hat{\alpha}-\alpha+\hat{\beta}-\beta)\prod_{\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha}\zeta(1+\hat{\alpha}-\alpha)\prod_{\hat{\beta}\neq\beta}\zeta(1+\hat{\beta}-\beta)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\left(\frac{h}{k}\right)^{-s-\alpha-\beta}\mathcal{K}(0,-\alpha-\beta,2)\,ds.\end{split}

This, (
9.1.19
), (
9.1.20
), and (
9.1.21
) complete our calculation of
J_{2}
.

Now that we have evaluated
J_{2}
, we next turn our attention to the term
J_{3}
in (
9.1.11
). Recall its definition

(9.1.26)

J_{3}:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in A\\
\beta\in B\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha^{\prime}\neq\alpha\\
\beta^{\prime}\neq\beta\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{4\pi i}\int_{(\epsilon)}\underset{s_{2}=-s_{1}-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime}}{\text{Res}}\ \mathcal{J}\,ds_{1}.

Since the residue of
\zeta(1+\alpha^{\prime}+s_{1}+\beta^{\prime}+s_{2})
at
s_{2}=-s_{1}-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime}
is
1
, it follows from the definition (
9.1.10
) of
\mathcal{J}
that if
\alpha^{\prime}\in A\smallsetminus\{\alpha\}
and
\beta^{\prime}\in B\smallsetminus\{\beta\}
, then

(9.1.27)

\begin{split}\underset{s_{2}=-s_{1}-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime}}{\text{Res}}\ \mathcal{J}=X^{-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime}}Q^{2-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime}}\widetilde{V}(s_{1})\widetilde{V}(-s_{1}-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime})\widetilde{W}(2-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime})\\
\times\mathscr{X}(\tfrac{1}{2}+\alpha+s_{1})\mathscr{X}(\tfrac{1}{2}+\beta-s_{1}-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime})\zeta(1-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime})\\
\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha\\
\hat{\beta}\neq\beta\\
(\hat{\alpha},\hat{\beta})\neq(\alpha^{\prime},\beta^{\prime})\end{subarray}}\zeta(1+\hat{\alpha}+\hat{\beta}-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime})\prod_{\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha}\zeta(1+\hat{\alpha}-\alpha)\prod_{\hat{\beta}\neq\beta}\zeta(1+\hat{\beta}-\beta)\\
\times\mathcal{K}(s_{1},-s_{1}-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime},2-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime}).\end{split}

We move the line of integration in (
9.1.26
) to
\re(s_{1})=-\epsilon
. We find residues from the poles at
s_{1}=0
and
s_{1}=-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime}
due to the factors
\widetilde{V}(s_{1})
and
\widetilde{V}(-s_{1}-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime})
in (
9.1.27
), and thus deduce that

\begin{split}J_{3}&=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in A\\
\beta\in B\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha^{\prime}\neq\alpha\\
\beta^{\prime}\neq\beta\end{subarray}}\underset{s_{1}=0}{\text{Res}}\ \underset{s_{2}=-s_{1}-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime}}{\text{Res}}\ \mathcal{J}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in A\\
\beta\in B\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha^{\prime}\neq\alpha\\
\beta^{\prime}\neq\beta\end{subarray}}\underset{s_{1}=-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime}}{\text{Res}}\ \underset{s_{2}=-s_{1}-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime}}{\text{Res}}\ \mathcal{J}\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in A\\
\beta\in B\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha^{\prime}\neq\alpha\\
\beta^{\prime}\neq\beta\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{4\pi i}\int_{(-\epsilon)}\underset{s_{2}=-s_{1}-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime}}{\text{Res}}\ \mathcal{J}\,ds_{1}.\end{split}

For brevity, write this as

(9.1.28)

J_{3}=J_{31}+J_{32}+J_{33}.

We see from (
3.9
) and (
9.1.27
) that

(9.1.29)

\begin{split}J_{31}&=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in A\\
\beta\in B\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha^{\prime}\neq\alpha\\
\beta^{\prime}\neq\beta\end{subarray}}X^{-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime}}Q^{2-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime}}\widetilde{V}(-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime})\widetilde{W}(2-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime})\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\mathscr{X}(\tfrac{1}{2}+\alpha)\mathscr{X}(\tfrac{1}{2}+\beta-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime})\zeta(1-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime})\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha\\
\hat{\beta}\neq\beta\\
(\hat{\alpha},\hat{\beta})\neq(\alpha^{\prime},\beta^{\prime})\end{subarray}}\zeta(1+\hat{\alpha}+\hat{\beta}-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime})\prod_{\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha}\zeta(1+\hat{\alpha}-\alpha)\prod_{\hat{\beta}\neq\beta}\zeta(1+\hat{\beta}-\beta)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\mathcal{K}(0,-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime},2-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime}).\end{split}

Similarly, since the residue of
\widetilde{V}(-s_{1}-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime})
at
s_{1}=-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime}
is
-1
by (
3.9
), we deduce from (
9.1.27
) that the term
J_{32}
in (
9.1.28
) equals

\displaystyle J_{32}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in A\\
\beta\in B\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha^{\prime}\neq\alpha\\
\beta^{\prime}\neq\beta\end{subarray}}X^{-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime}}Q^{2-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime}}\widetilde{V}(-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime})\widetilde{W}(2-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime})

\displaystyle\hskip 18.06749pt\times\mathscr{X}(\tfrac{1}{2}+\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime})\mathscr{X}(\tfrac{1}{2}+\beta)\zeta(1-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime})

\displaystyle\hskip 18.06749pt\times\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha\\
\hat{\beta}\neq\beta\\
(\hat{\alpha},\hat{\beta})\neq(\alpha^{\prime},\beta^{\prime})\end{subarray}}\zeta(1+\hat{\alpha}+\hat{\beta}-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime})\prod_{\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha}\zeta(1+\hat{\alpha}-\alpha)\prod_{\hat{\beta}\neq\beta}\zeta(1+\hat{\beta}-\beta)

(9.1.30)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.06749pt\times\mathcal{K}(-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime},0,2-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime}).

To simplify the integral
J_{33}
in (
9.1.28
), we apply Lemma
9.4
to deduce that

\mathcal{K}(s_{1},-s_{1}-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime},2-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime})=\left(\frac{h}{k}\right)^{s_{1}}\mathcal{K}(0,-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime},2-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime}).

It follows from this and (
9.1.27
) that the integral
J_{33}
in (
9.1.28
) equals

(9.1.31)

\begin{split}J_{33}&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in A\\
\beta\in B\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha^{\prime}\neq\alpha\\
\beta^{\prime}\neq\beta\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{4\pi i}\int_{(-\epsilon)}X^{-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime}}Q^{2-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime}}\widetilde{V}(s_{1})\widetilde{V}(-s_{1}-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime})\widetilde{W}(2-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime})\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\mathscr{X}(\tfrac{1}{2}+\alpha+s_{1})\mathscr{X}(\tfrac{1}{2}+\beta-s_{1}-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime})\zeta(1-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime})\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha\\
\hat{\beta}\neq\beta\\
(\hat{\alpha},\hat{\beta})\neq(\alpha^{\prime},\beta^{\prime})\end{subarray}}\zeta(1+\hat{\alpha}+\hat{\beta}-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime})\prod_{\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha}\zeta(1+\hat{\alpha}-\alpha)\prod_{\hat{\beta}\neq\beta}\zeta(1+\hat{\beta}-\beta)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\left(\frac{h}{k}\right)^{s_{1}}\mathcal{K}(0,-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime},2-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime})\,ds_{1}.\end{split}

This, (
9.1.28
), (
9.1.29
), and (
9.1.30
) complete our evaluation of
J_{3}
.

Putting together our calculations, we deduce from (
9.1.11
), (
9.1.13
), (
9.1.19
), and (
9.1.28
) that

\begin{split}\mathcal{I}_{1}^{*}(h,k)&=J_{11}+J_{12}+J_{13}+J_{21}+J_{22}+J_{23}+J_{31}+J_{32}+J_{33}\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt+O\big(X^{-\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}Q^{\frac{5}{2}}(hk)^{\varepsilon}\big)+O\big(X^{\varepsilon}Q^{\frac{3}{2}+\varepsilon}(hk)^{\varepsilon}\big).\end{split}

The terms
J_{12}
and
J_{22}
cancel each other by (
9.1.15
) and (
9.1.21
), while
J_{13}
cancels with
J_{32}
by (
9.1.16
) and (
9.1.30
). Therefore

(9.1.32)

\mathcal{I}_{1}^{*}(h,k)=J_{11}+J_{21}+J_{23}+J_{31}+J_{33}+O\big(X^{-\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}Q^{\frac{5}{2}}(hk)^{\varepsilon}\big)+O\big(X^{\varepsilon}Q^{\frac{3}{2}+\varepsilon}(hk)^{\varepsilon}\big),

and we have evaluated
J_{11}
in (
9.1.14
),
J_{21}
in (
9.1.20
),
J_{23}
in (
9.1.25
),
J_{31}
in (
9.1.29
), and
J_{33}
in (
9.1.31
). Our goal for the rest of this section is to show that
\mathcal{U}^{2}(h,k)
is equal to the right-hand side of (
9.1.32
) up to an admissible error term.

9.2.
Analysis of
\mathcal{U}^{2}(h,k)

We now continue our analysis of
\mathcal{U}^{2}(h,k)
. Our goal for this subsection and the next is to show that
\mathcal{U}^{2}(h,k)
is equal to the right-hand side of (
9.1.32
) up to an admissible error term. We multiply the integrand in (
9.15
) by

(9.2.1)

\prod_{\alpha\in A}\zeta(-\tfrac{1}{2}+\alpha+s_{1}+w-z)\prod_{\beta\in B}\zeta(\tfrac{1}{2}+\beta+s_{2}+z)

and divide it by the Euler product of (
9.2.1
). The result is

(9.2.2)

\begin{split}\mathcal{U}^{2}(h,k)&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq c\leq C\\
(c,hk)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(c)}{2(2\pi i)^{4}}\int_{(2)}\int_{(2)}\int_{(1+\epsilon)}\int_{(\epsilon/2)}X^{s_{1}+s_{2}}Q^{w}c^{-w}\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\widetilde{V}(s_{1})\widetilde{V}(s_{2})\widetilde{W}(w)\mathcal{H}(z,w-1)\frac{e^{\delta z}-e^{-\delta z}}{2\delta z}\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\zeta(2-w)\prod_{\alpha\in A}\zeta(-\tfrac{1}{2}+\alpha+s_{1}+w-z)\prod_{\beta\in B}\zeta(\tfrac{1}{2}+\beta+s_{2}+z)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times h^{1-w+z}k^{-z}\mathcal{P}(s_{1},s_{2},w,z)\,dz\,dw\,ds_{2}\,ds_{1}\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt+O\left(X^{\varepsilon}Q^{1+\varepsilon}\frac{(hk)^{\varepsilon}(h,k)}{(hk)^{1/2}}+(XChk)^{\varepsilon}kX^{2}Q^{-97}\right),\end{split}

where
\mathcal{P}(s_{1},s_{2},w,z)
is defined by

(9.2.3)

\begin{split}&\mathcal{P}(s_{1},s_{2},w,z)=\mathcal{P}(s_{1},s_{2},w,z;A,B,h,k,c)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt:=\prod_{p|c}\Bigg\{\left(1+\frac{p^{w-1}-1}{p(p-1)}\right)\prod_{\alpha\in A}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{-\frac{1}{2}+\alpha+s_{1}+w-z}}\right)\prod_{\beta\in B}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{\frac{1}{2}+\beta+s_{2}+z}}\right)\Bigg\}\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}p\nmid c\\
p|hk\end{subarray}}\Bigg\{\prod_{\alpha\in A}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{-\frac{1}{2}+\alpha+s_{1}+w-z}}\right)\prod_{\beta\in B}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{\frac{1}{2}+\beta+s_{2}+z}}\right)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\Bigg(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m,n<\infty\\
m+\text{ord}_{p}(h)=n+\text{ord}_{p}(k)\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})\left(1+\frac{p^{w}}{p^{2}(p-1)}-\frac{1}{p-1}\right)}{p^{m(-\frac{1}{2}+s_{1}+w-z)}p^{n(\frac{1}{2}+s_{2}+z)}p^{(1-w)\min\{m+\text{ord}_{p}(h),n+\text{ord}_{p}(k)\}}}\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m,n<\infty\\
m+\text{ord}_{p}(h)\neq n+\text{ord}_{p}(k)\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})\left(1-\frac{p^{w}}{p^{2}}\right)}{p^{m(-\frac{1}{2}+s_{1}+w-z)}p^{n(\frac{1}{2}+s_{2}+z)}p^{(1-w)\min\{m+\text{ord}_{p}(h),n+\text{ord}_{p}(k)\}}}\Bigg)\Bigg\}\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}p\nmid c\\
p\nmid hk\end{subarray}}\Bigg\{\prod_{\alpha\in A}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{-\frac{1}{2}+\alpha+s_{1}+w-z}}\right)\prod_{\beta\in B}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{\frac{1}{2}+\beta+s_{2}+z}}\right)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\Bigg(1+\frac{p^{w-1}-1}{p(p-1)}+\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{m})\left(1+\frac{p^{w}}{p^{2}(p-1)}-\frac{1}{p-1}\right)}{p^{m(1+s_{1}+s_{2})}}\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt+\sum_{0\leq m<n<\infty}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})\left(1-\frac{p^{w}}{p^{2}}\right)}{p^{m(\frac{1}{2}+s_{1}-z)}p^{n(\frac{1}{2}+s_{2}+z)}}+\sum_{0\leq n<m<\infty}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})\left(1-\frac{p^{w}}{p^{2}}\right)}{p^{m(-\frac{1}{2}+s_{1}+w-z)}p^{n(\frac{3}{2}+s_{2}-w+z)}}\Bigg)\Bigg\}.\end{split}

As in our analysis of
\mathcal{I}_{1}^{*}(h,k)
in the previous subsection, this “factoring out” of the zeta-functions (
9.2.1
) gives us the analytic continuation of the integrand and allows us to evaluate its residues when shifting contours.

To facilitate our estimations, we first prove the following lemma, which will allow us to move some lines of integration and bound integrals that remain after applying the residue theorem.

Lemma 9.5
.

Suppose that
\epsilon>0
is arbitrarily small. Let
h,k,c
be positive integers with
(c,hk)=1
. If
s_{1},s_{2},w
are complex numbers such that

(i)

\re(w)\leq 2-\epsilon
,

(ii)

\re(-\frac{1}{2}+s_{1}+w-z)\geq\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon
,

(iii)

\re(\frac{1}{2}+s_{2}+z)\geq\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon

(iv)

\re(1+s_{1}+s_{2})\geq 1+\epsilon

(v)

\re(\frac{1}{2}+s_{1}-z)\geq\epsilon
, and

(vi)

\re(\frac{3}{2}+s_{2}-w+z)\geq\epsilon
.

then the product (
9.2.3
) defining
\mathcal{P}(s_{1},s_{2},w,z;A,B,h,k,c)
converges absolutely and we have

\mathcal{P}(s_{1},s_{2},w,z;A,B,h,k,c)\ll_{\varepsilon}(chk)^{\varepsilon}(h,k)^{\re(w)-1}.

Proof.

For brevity, in this proof we will refer to the conditions in the hypothesis by their respective labels (i), (ii),
\dots
, (vi). We will also repeatedly apply without mention the bounds
\tau_{A}(m)\ll m^{\varepsilon}
and
\tau_{B}(n)\ll n^{\varepsilon}
, which follow from (
3.2
) and our assumption that
\alpha,\beta\ll 1/\log Q
for all
\alpha\in A
and
\beta\in B
. The condition (i) implies

(9.2.4)

1+\frac{p^{w-1}-1}{p(p-1)}=1+O\bigg(\frac{1}{p^{1+\varepsilon}}\bigg).

From (ii), (iii), and our assumption that
\alpha,\beta\ll 1/\log Q
for all
\alpha\in A
and
\beta\in B
, we see that

\frac{1}{p^{-\frac{1}{2}+\alpha+s_{1}+w-z}}\ll\frac{1}{p^{\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}}

for all
\alpha\in A
and

\frac{1}{p^{\frac{1}{2}+\beta+s_{2}+z}}\ll\frac{1}{p^{\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}}

for all
\beta\in B
. Thus, multiplying out the product and applying the definition (
3.1
) gives

(9.2.5)

\begin{split}&\prod_{\alpha\in A}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{-\frac{1}{2}+\alpha+s_{1}+w-z}}\right)\prod_{\beta\in B}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{\frac{1}{2}+\beta+s_{2}+z}}\right)\\
&=1-\sum_{\alpha\in A}\frac{1}{p^{-\frac{1}{2}+\alpha+s_{1}+w-z}}-\sum_{\beta\in B}\frac{1}{p^{\frac{1}{2}+\beta+s_{2}+z}}+O\bigg(\frac{1}{p^{1+\varepsilon}}\bigg)\\
&=1-\frac{\tau_{A}(p)}{p^{-\frac{1}{2}+s_{1}+w-z}}-\frac{\tau_{B}(p)}{p^{\frac{1}{2}+s_{2}+z}}+O\bigg(\frac{1}{p^{1+\varepsilon}}\bigg).\end{split}

By (i) and (iv), we have

(9.2.6)

\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{m})\left(1+\frac{p^{w}}{p^{2}(p-1)}-\frac{1}{p-1}\right)}{p^{m(1+s_{1}+s_{2})}}\ll\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{p^{m(1+\varepsilon)}}\ll\frac{1}{p^{1+\varepsilon}}.

Next, to estimate the sum

\sum_{0\leq m<n<\infty}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})\left(1-\frac{p^{w}}{p^{2}}\right)}{p^{m(\frac{1}{2}+s_{1}-z)}p^{n(\frac{1}{2}+s_{2}+z)}},

we separate it into three parts: the term with
m=0
and
n=1
, the sum of the terms with
m=0
and
n\geq 2
, and the sum of the terms with
m\geq 1
. The part with
m=0
and
n\geq 2
is at most
O(p^{-1-\varepsilon})
by (i) and (iii). To bound the part with
m\geq 1
, we evaluate the
n
-sum first and use (i), (iii), and (iv) to write

\begin{split}\sum_{1\leq m<n<\infty}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})\left(1-\frac{p^{w}}{p^{2}}\right)}{p^{m(\frac{1}{2}+s_{1}-z)}p^{n(\frac{1}{2}+s_{2}+z)}}&\ll\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\frac{p^{(m+1)\varepsilon}}{p^{m(\re(\frac{1}{2}+s_{1}-z))}p^{(m+1)(\re(\frac{1}{2}+s_{2}+z))}}\\
&=\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\frac{p^{(m+1)\varepsilon}}{p^{m(\re(1+s_{1}+s_{2}))+\re(\frac{1}{2}+s_{2}+z)}}\ll\frac{1}{p^{\frac{3}{2}+\varepsilon}}.\end{split}

We thus arrive at

\sum_{0\leq m<n<\infty}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})\left(1-\frac{p^{w}}{p^{2}}\right)}{p^{m(\frac{1}{2}+s_{1}-z)}p^{n(\frac{1}{2}+s_{2}+z)}}=\frac{\tau_{B}(p)\left(1-\frac{p^{w}}{p^{2}}\right)}{p^{\frac{1}{2}+s_{2}+z}}+O\bigg(\frac{1}{p^{1+\varepsilon}}\bigg).

It follows from this and (vi) that

(9.2.7)

\sum_{0\leq m<n<\infty}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})\left(1-\frac{p^{w}}{p^{2}}\right)}{p^{m(\frac{1}{2}+s_{1}-z)}p^{n(\frac{1}{2}+s_{2}+z)}}=\frac{\tau_{B}(p)}{p^{\frac{1}{2}+s_{2}+z}}+O\bigg(\frac{1}{p^{1+\varepsilon}}\bigg).

A similar argument using (i), (ii), (iv), and (v) leads to

(9.2.8)

\sum_{0\leq n<m<\infty}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})\left(1-\frac{p^{w}}{p^{2}}\right)}{p^{m(-\frac{1}{2}+s_{1}+w-z)}p^{n(\frac{3}{2}+s_{2}-w+z)}}=\frac{\tau_{A}(p)}{p^{-\frac{1}{2}+s_{1}+w-z}}+O\bigg(\frac{1}{p^{1+\varepsilon}}\bigg).

We next bound the sum

(9.2.9)

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m,n<\infty\\
m+\text{ord}_{p}(h)=n+\text{ord}_{p}(k)\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})\left(1+\frac{p^{w}}{p^{2}(p-1)}-\frac{1}{p-1}\right)}{p^{m(-\frac{1}{2}+s_{1}+w-z)}p^{n(\frac{1}{2}+s_{2}+z)}p^{(1-w)\min\{m+\text{ord}_{p}(h),n+\text{ord}_{p}(k)\}}}.

For brevity, we denote
h_{p}:=\text{ord}_{p}(h)
and
k_{p}:=\text{ord}_{p}(k)
for the rest of this proof. We make the change of variable
m\mapsto\nu+k_{p}
in (
9.2.9
), so that
n=\nu+h_{p}
, to write (
9.2.9
) as

\frac{1}{p^{k_{p}(\frac{1}{2}+s_{1}-z)}p^{h_{p}(\frac{3}{2}+s_{2}-w+z)}}\sum_{\nu=-\min\{h_{p},k_{p}\}}^{\infty}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{\nu+k_{p}})\tau_{B}(p^{\nu+h_{p}})\left(1+\frac{p^{w}}{p^{2}(p-1)}-\frac{1}{p-1}\right)}{p^{\nu(1+s_{1}+s_{2})}}.

Hence, by (i) and (iv), we see that
(
9.2.9
)
is at most

\ll\frac{p^{\varepsilon h_{p}+\varepsilon k_{p}+\min\{h_{p},k_{p}\}\re(1+s_{1}+s_{2})}}{p^{k_{p}(\re(\frac{1}{2}+s_{1}-z))}p^{h_{p}(\re(\frac{3}{2}+s_{2}-w+z))}}.

The denominator of this bound is
\geq p^{\min\{h_{p},k_{p}\}\re(\frac{1}{2}+s_{1}-z)}p^{\min\{h_{p},k_{p}\}\re(\frac{3}{2}+s_{2}-w+z)}
by (v) and (vi). It follows that

(9.2.10)

\begin{split}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m,n<\infty\\
