m+\text{ord}_{p}(h)=n+\text{ord}_{p}(k)\end{subarray}}&\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})\left(1+\frac{p^{w}}{p^{2}(p-1)}-\frac{1}{p-1}\right)}{p^{m(-\frac{1}{2}+s_{1}+w-z)}p^{n(\frac{1}{2}+s_{2}+z)}p^{(1-w)\min\{m+\text{ord}_{p}(h),n+\text{ord}_{p}(k)\}}}\\
&\hskip 36.135pt\ll p^{\varepsilon h_{p}+\varepsilon k_{p}+\min\{h_{p},k_{p}\}(\re(w)-1)}.\end{split}

Next, to bound the sum

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m,n<\infty\\
m+\text{ord}_{p}(h)<n+\text{ord}_{p}(k)\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})\left(1-\frac{p^{w}}{p^{2}}\right)}{p^{m(-\frac{1}{2}+s_{1}+w-z)}p^{n(\frac{1}{2}+s_{2}+z)}p^{(1-w)\min\{m+\text{ord}_{p}(h),n+\text{ord}_{p}(k)\}}},

we split it into the part with
m<k_{p}-h_{p}
and the part with
m\geq k_{p}-h_{p}
to deduce that

(9.2.11)

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m,n<\infty\\
m+\text{ord}_{p}(h)<n+\text{ord}_{p}(k)\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})\left(1-\frac{p^{w}}{p^{2}}\right)}{p^{m(-\frac{1}{2}+s_{1}+w-z)}p^{n(\frac{1}{2}+s_{2}+z)}p^{(1-w)\min\{m+\text{ord}_{p}(h),n+\text{ord}_{p}(k)\}}}=\Sigma_{1}+\Sigma_{2},

where

(9.2.12)

\Sigma_{1}:=p^{(w-1)h_{p}}\sum_{m=0}^{k_{p}-h_{p}-1}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})\left(1-\frac{p^{w}}{p^{2}}\right)}{p^{m(\frac{1}{2}+s_{1}-z)}p^{n(\frac{1}{2}+s_{2}+z)}}

and

(9.2.13)

\Sigma_{2}:=p^{(w-1)h_{p}}\sum_{m=\max\{0,k_{p}-h_{p}\}}^{\infty}\sum_{n=m+h_{p}-k_{p}+1}^{\infty}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})\left(1-\frac{p^{w}}{p^{2}}\right)}{p^{m(\frac{1}{2}+s_{1}-z)}p^{n(\frac{1}{2}+s_{2}+z)}}.

We use (i) to bound
p^{w}/p^{2}
and apply (iii) to estimate the
n
-sums in (
9.2.12
) and (
9.2.13
) to see that

(9.2.14)

\Sigma_{1}\ll p^{(\re(w)-1)h_{p}}\sum_{m=0}^{k_{p}-h_{p}-1}\frac{p^{m\varepsilon}}{p^{m(\re(\frac{1}{2}+s_{1}-z))}}

and

(9.2.15)

\Sigma_{2}\ll p^{(\re(w)-1)h_{p}}\sum_{m=\max\{0,k_{p}-h_{p}\}}^{\infty}\frac{1}{p^{m(\re(1+s_{1}+s_{2})-\varepsilon)}p^{(h_{p}-k_{p}+1)(\re(\frac{1}{2}+s_{2}+z)-\varepsilon)}}.

If
h_{p}\geq k_{p}
, then the
m
-sum on the right-hand side of (
9.2.14
) is zero. Otherwise, it is
O(1)
by (v). In either case, we have

(9.2.16)

\Sigma_{1}\ll p^{(\re(w)-1)\min\{h_{p},k_{p}\}}.

If
h_{p}\geq k_{p}
, then the
m
-sum in (
9.2.15
) starts at
m=0
and thus (iii), (iv), and (vi) imply

\Sigma_{2}\ll\frac{p^{(\re(w)-1)h_{p}}}{p^{(h_{p}-k_{p}+1)(\re(\frac{1}{2}+s_{2}+z)-\varepsilon)}}=\frac{p^{(\re(w)-1)k_{p}}}{p^{(h_{p}-k_{p})(\re(\frac{3}{2}+s_{2}-w+z)-\varepsilon)}p^{\re(\frac{1}{2}+s_{2}+z)-\varepsilon}}\ll\frac{p^{(\re(w)-1)k_{p}}}{p^{\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}}

On the other hand, if
h_{p}<k_{p}
, then the
m
-sum in (
9.2.15
) starts at
m=k_{p}-h_{p}
and it follows from (iii), (iv), and (v) that

\begin{split}\Sigma_{2}&\ll\frac{p^{(\re(w)-1)h_{p}}}{p^{(k_{p}-h_{p})(\re(1+s_{1}+s_{2})-\varepsilon)}p^{(h_{p}-k_{p}+1)(\re(\frac{1}{2}+s_{2}+z)-\varepsilon)}}\\
&\leq\frac{p^{(\re(w)-1)h_{p}}p^{(k_{p}-h_{p}+1)\varepsilon}}{p^{(k_{p}-h_{p})(\re(\frac{1}{2}+s_{1}-z))}p^{\re(\frac{1}{2}+s_{2}+z)}}\leq\frac{p^{(\re(w)-1)h_{p}}p^{(k_{p}-h_{p}+1)\varepsilon}.}{p^{\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}}\end{split}

In either case, we have

\Sigma_{2}\ll p^{(\re(w)-1)\min\{h_{p},k_{p}\}+\varepsilon h_{p}+\varepsilon k_{p}+\varepsilon-\frac{1}{2}}.

From this, (
9.2.16
), and (
9.2.11
), we arrive at

(9.2.17)

\begin{split}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m,n<\infty\\
m+\text{ord}_{p}(h)<n+\text{ord}_{p}(k)\end{subarray}}&\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})\left(1-\frac{p^{w}}{p^{2}}\right)}{p^{m(-\frac{1}{2}+s_{1}+w-z)}p^{n(\frac{1}{2}+s_{2}+z)}p^{(1-w)\min\{m+\text{ord}_{p}(h),n+\text{ord}_{p}(k)\}}}\\
&\ll p^{\varepsilon h_{p}+\varepsilon k_{p}+\varepsilon+(\re(w)-1)\min\{h_{p},k_{p}\}}.\end{split}

A similar argument using (i), (ii), (iv), (v), and (vi) gives

(9.2.18)

\begin{split}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m,n<\infty\\
m+\text{ord}_{p}(h)>n+\text{ord}_{p}(k)\end{subarray}}&\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})\left(1-\frac{p^{w}}{p^{2}}\right)}{p^{m(-\frac{1}{2}+s_{1}+w-z)}p^{n(\frac{1}{2}+s_{2}+z)}p^{(1-w)\min\{m+\text{ord}_{p}(h),n+\text{ord}_{p}(k)\}}}\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\ll p^{\varepsilon h_{p}+\varepsilon k_{p}+\varepsilon+(\re(w)-1)\min\{h_{p},k_{p}\}}.\end{split}

From (
9.2.4
), (
9.2.5
), (ii), and (iii), we see that if
p|c
then the local factor in (
9.2.3
) corresponding to
p
is
O(1)
. Moreover, from (
9.2.5
), (ii), (iii), (
9.2.10
), (
9.2.17
), and (
9.2.18
), we deduce that if
p\nmid c
and
p|hk
then the local factor in (
9.2.3
) corresponding to
p
is

\ll p^{(\re(w)-1)\min\{\text{ord}_{p}(h),\text{ord}_{p}(k)\}+\varepsilon\text{ord}_{p}(h)+\varepsilon\text{ord}_{p}(k)+\varepsilon}.

Finally, from (
9.2.4
), (
9.2.5
), (
9.2.6
), (
9.2.7
), and (
9.2.8
), we see that if
p\nmid chk
then the local factor in (
9.2.3
) corresponding to
p
is
1+O(p^{-1-\varepsilon})
. We conclude that the right-hand side of (
9.2.3
) converges absolutely, and is

\ll(chk)^{\varepsilon}(h,k)^{\re(w)-1}

because
c
and
hk
are coprime,
(h,k)=\prod_{p|hk}p^{\min\{\text{ord}_{p}(h),\text{ord}_{p}(k)\}}
, and
\prod_{p|\nu}O(1)\ll{\nu}^{\varepsilon}
for any positive integer
\nu
.
∎

We move the
s_{2}
-line in (
9.2.2
) to
\re(s_{2})=\epsilon
. This leaves a residue from the pole at
s_{2}=\frac{1}{2}-\beta-z
for each
\beta\in B
because of the factors (
9.2.1
). Note that we need to assume the Lindelöf Hypothesis to maintain the absolute convergence of the
z
-integral, as there is an arbitrary number of zeta-functions that depend on
z
and
\mathcal{H}(z,w-1)
only decays slowly by (
3.15
). The result is

(9.2.19)

\mathcal{U}^{2}(h,k)=I_{1}+I_{2}+O\left(X^{\varepsilon}Q^{1+\varepsilon}\frac{(hk)^{\varepsilon}(h,k)}{(hk)^{1/2}}+(XChk)^{\varepsilon}kX^{2}Q^{-97}\right),

where
I_{1}
is the integral of the residues at the poles
s_{2}=\frac{1}{2}-\beta-z
and
I_{2}
is the new integral with
\re(s_{2})=\epsilon
. More precisely,

(9.2.20)

\begin{split}I_{1}&:=\sum_{\beta\in B}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq c\leq C\\
(c,hk)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(c)}{2(2\pi i)^{3}}\int_{(2)}\int_{(1+\epsilon)}\int_{(\epsilon/2)}X^{s_{1}+\frac{1}{2}-\beta-z}Q^{w}c^{-w}\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\widetilde{V}(s_{1})\widetilde{V}(\tfrac{1}{2}-\beta-z)\widetilde{W}(w)\mathcal{H}(z,w-1)\frac{e^{\delta z}-e^{-\delta z}}{2\delta z}\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\zeta(2-w)\prod_{\alpha\in A}\zeta(-\tfrac{1}{2}+\alpha+s_{1}+w-z)\prod_{\hat{\beta}\neq\beta}\zeta(1+\hat{\beta}-\beta)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times h^{1-w+z}k^{-z}\mathcal{P}(s_{1},\tfrac{1}{2}-\beta-z,w,z)\,dz\,dw\,ds_{1}\end{split}

and

(9.2.21)

\begin{split}I_{2}&:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq c\leq C\\
(c,hk)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(c)}{2(2\pi i)^{4}}\int_{(2)}\int_{(\epsilon)}\int_{(1+\epsilon)}\int_{(\epsilon/2)}X^{s_{1}+s_{2}}Q^{w}c^{-w}\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\widetilde{V}(s_{1})\widetilde{V}(s_{2})\widetilde{W}(w)\mathcal{H}(z,w-1)\frac{e^{\delta z}-e^{-\delta z}}{2\delta z}\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\zeta(2-w)\prod_{\alpha\in A}\zeta(-\tfrac{1}{2}+\alpha+s_{1}+w-z)\prod_{\beta\in B}\zeta(\tfrac{1}{2}+\beta+s_{2}+z)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times h^{1-w+z}k^{-z}\mathcal{P}(s_{1},s_{2},w,z)\,dz\,dw\,ds_{2}\,ds_{1}.\end{split}

We first bound
I_{2}
. We move the
s_{1}
-line in (
9.2.21
) to
\re(s_{1})=\epsilon
to deduce that

(9.2.22)

I_{2}=I_{21}+I_{22},

where
I_{21}
is the integral of the residues at the poles
s_{1}=\frac{3}{2}-\alpha-w+z
, where
\alpha
runs through the elements of
A
, and
I_{22}
is the new integral with
\re(s_{1})=\epsilon
. In other words,

(9.2.23)

\begin{split}I_{21}&:=\sum_{\alpha\in A}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq c\leq C\\
(c,hk)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(c)}{2(2\pi i)^{3}}\int_{(\epsilon)}\int_{(1+\epsilon)}\int_{(\epsilon/2)}X^{\frac{3}{2}-\alpha-w+z+s_{2}}Q^{w}c^{-w}\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\widetilde{V}(\tfrac{3}{2}-\alpha-w+z)\widetilde{V}(s_{2})\widetilde{W}(w)\mathcal{H}(z,w-1)\frac{e^{\delta z}-e^{-\delta z}}{2\delta z}\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\zeta(2-w)\prod_{\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha}\zeta(1+\hat{\alpha}-\alpha)\prod_{\beta\in B}\zeta(\tfrac{1}{2}+\beta+s_{2}+z)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times h^{1-w+z}k^{-z}\mathcal{P}(\tfrac{3}{2}-\alpha-w+z,s_{2},w,z)\,dz\,dw\,ds_{2}\end{split}

and

\displaystyle I_{22}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq c\leq C\\
(c,hk)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(c)}{2(2\pi i)^{4}}\int_{(\epsilon)}\int_{(\epsilon)}\int_{(1+\epsilon)}\int_{(\epsilon/2)}X^{s_{1}+s_{2}}Q^{w}c^{-w}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.06749pt\times\widetilde{V}(s_{1})\widetilde{V}(s_{2})\widetilde{W}(w)\mathcal{H}(z,w-1)\frac{e^{\delta z}-e^{-\delta z}}{2\delta z}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.06749pt\times\zeta(2-w)\prod_{\alpha\in A}\zeta(-\tfrac{1}{2}+\alpha+s_{1}+w-z)\prod_{\beta\in B}\zeta(\tfrac{1}{2}+\beta+s_{2}+z)

(9.2.24)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.06749pt\times h^{1-w+z}k^{-z}\mathcal{P}(s_{1},s_{2},w,z)\,dz\,dw\,ds_{2}\,ds_{1}.

Note that we need to assume the Lindelöf Hypothesis to justify (
9.2.22
) like we did to validate (
9.2.19
). To estimate
I_{22}
, we again assume the Lindelöf Hypothesis in order to bound the arbitrary number of zeta-functions in (
9.2.24
) that depend on the variable
z
. We apply (
3.10
), (
3.15
), and Lemma
9.5
, and argue as in (
9.11
) to deduce from (
9.2.24
) and the definition (
3.4
) of
\delta
that

(9.2.25)

I_{22}\ll X^{\varepsilon}Q^{1+\varepsilon}h^{\varepsilon}k^{\varepsilon}.

Next, to bound
I_{21}
, we move the
w
-line in (
9.2.23
) to
\re(w)=\frac{3}{2}-\epsilon
. We traverse no poles in doing so. We then bound the resulting expression by applying (
3.6
), (
3.10
), (
3.15
), and Lemma
9.5
. The result is

I_{21}\ll X^{\varepsilon}Q^{\frac{3}{2}}h^{\varepsilon}k^{\varepsilon}.

From this, (
9.2.25
), and (
9.2.22
), we arrive at

(9.2.26)

I_{2}\ll X^{\varepsilon}Q^{\frac{3}{2}}h^{\varepsilon}k^{\varepsilon}.

Having bounded
I_{2}
, we now turn our attention to the integral
I_{1}
defined by (
9.2.20
). We move the
s_{1}
-line in (
9.2.20
) to
\re(s_{1})=\epsilon
. This leaves a residue from the pole at
s_{1}=\frac{3}{2}-\alpha-w+z
for each
\alpha\in A
, and leads to

(9.2.27)

I_{1}=I_{11}+I_{12},

where

(9.2.28)

\begin{split}I_{11}&:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in A\\
\beta\in B\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq c\leq C\\
(c,hk)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(c)}{2(2\pi i)^{2}}\int_{(1+\epsilon)}\int_{(\epsilon/2)}X^{2-\alpha-\beta-w}Q^{w}c^{-w}\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\widetilde{V}(\tfrac{3}{2}-\alpha-w+z)\widetilde{V}(\tfrac{1}{2}-\beta-z)\widetilde{W}(w)\mathcal{H}(z,w-1)\frac{e^{\delta z}-e^{-\delta z}}{2\delta z}\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\zeta(2-w)\prod_{\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha}\zeta(1+\hat{\alpha}-\alpha)\prod_{\hat{\beta}\neq\beta}\zeta(1+\hat{\beta}-\beta)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times h^{1-w+z}k^{-z}\mathcal{P}(\tfrac{3}{2}-\alpha-w+z,\tfrac{1}{2}-\beta-z,w,z)\,dz\,dw\end{split}

and

(9.2.29)

\begin{split}I_{12}&:=\sum_{\beta\in B}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq c\leq C\\
(c,hk)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(c)}{2(2\pi i)^{3}}\int_{(\epsilon)}\int_{(1+\epsilon)}\int_{(\epsilon/2)}X^{s_{1}+\frac{1}{2}-\beta-z}Q^{w}c^{-w}\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\widetilde{V}(s_{1})\widetilde{V}(\tfrac{1}{2}-\beta-z)\widetilde{W}(w)\mathcal{H}(z,w-1)\frac{e^{\delta z}-e^{-\delta z}}{2\delta z}\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\zeta(2-w)\prod_{\alpha\in A}\zeta(-\tfrac{1}{2}+\alpha+s_{1}+w-z)\prod_{\hat{\beta}\neq\beta}\zeta(1+\hat{\beta}-\beta)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times h^{1-w+z}k^{-z}\mathcal{P}(s_{1},\tfrac{1}{2}-\beta-z,w,z)\,dz\,dw\,ds_{1}.\end{split}

To bound
I_{12}
, we move the
w
-line in (
9.2.29
) to the right by a distance of at most
\epsilon/2
, and then move the
z
-line to the right by a distance of at most
\epsilon/2
. We do this in such a way as to maintain the inequality
1+\frac{\epsilon}{2}\leq\re(w-z)\leq 1+\epsilon
, so as to not traverse any pole of
\mathcal{H}(z,w-1)
. We repeat this process until the
w
-line is at
\re(w)=\frac{3}{2}-\epsilon
and the
z
-line is at
\frac{1}{2}-\frac{3\epsilon}{2}
. This leaves no residues because we do not cross any poles of the integrand. We then bound the resulting integral by applying (
3.6
), (
3.10
), (
3.15
), and Lemma
9.5
. We arrive at

(9.2.30)

I_{12}\ll X^{\varepsilon}Q^{\frac{3}{2}}h^{\varepsilon}k^{\varepsilon}.

To estimate the integral
I_{11}
defined by (
9.2.28
), our first task is to extend the
c
-sum in (
9.2.28
) to infinity. To do this, we need to bound the sum

(9.2.31)

\begin{split}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in A\\
\beta\in B\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}c>C\\
(c,hk)=1\end{subarray}}&\frac{\mu(c)}{2(2\pi i)^{2}}\int_{(1+\epsilon)}\int_{(\epsilon/2)}X^{2-\alpha-\beta-w}Q^{w}c^{-w}\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\widetilde{V}(\tfrac{3}{2}-\alpha-w+z)\widetilde{V}(\tfrac{1}{2}-\beta-z)\widetilde{W}(w)\mathcal{H}(z,w-1)\frac{e^{\delta z}-e^{-\delta z}}{2\delta z}\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\zeta(2-w)\prod_{\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha}\zeta(1+\hat{\alpha}-\alpha)\prod_{\hat{\beta}\neq\beta}\zeta(1+\hat{\beta}-\beta)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times h^{1-w+z}k^{-z}\mathcal{P}(\tfrac{3}{2}-\alpha-w+z,\tfrac{1}{2}-\beta-z,w,z)\,dz\,dw.\end{split}

We first move the
w
-line in (
9.2.31
) to
\re(w)=\frac{3}{2}
, crossing no poles. Then, we move the
z
-line to
\re(z)=\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon
, again traversing no poles. Afterward, we further move the
w
-line to
\re(w)=2-2\epsilon
. This does not cross any poles since now
\re(z)=\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon
. We bound the new integral that has
\re(w)=2-2\epsilon
and
\re(z)=\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon
using (
3.6
), (
3.10
), (
3.15
), and Lemma
9.5
, and deduce that (
9.2.31
) is at most

\ll\frac{(XChk)^{\varepsilon}Q^{2}(h,k)}{C\sqrt{hk}}.

It follows from this and (
9.2.28
) that

(9.2.32)

I_{11}=R_{0}+O\bigg(\frac{(XChk)^{\varepsilon}Q^{2}(h,k)}{C\sqrt{hk}}\bigg),

where
R_{0}
is defined by

(9.2.33)

\begin{split}R_{0}&:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in A\\
\beta\in B\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}c\geq 1\\
(c,hk)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(c)}{2(2\pi i)^{2}}\int_{(1+\epsilon)}\int_{(\epsilon/2)}X^{2-\alpha-\beta-w}Q^{w}c^{-w}\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\widetilde{V}(\tfrac{3}{2}-\alpha-w+z)\widetilde{V}(\tfrac{1}{2}-\beta-z)\widetilde{W}(w)\mathcal{H}(z,w-1)\frac{e^{\delta z}-e^{-\delta z}}{2\delta z}\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\zeta(2-w)\prod_{\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha}\zeta(1+\hat{\alpha}-\alpha)\prod_{\hat{\beta}\neq\beta}\zeta(1+\hat{\beta}-\beta)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times h^{1-w+z}k^{-z}\mathcal{P}(\tfrac{3}{2}-\alpha-w+z,\tfrac{1}{2}-\beta-z,w,z)\,dz\,dw.\end{split}

We may evaluate the sum of
\mu(c)c^{-w}\mathcal{P}(\tfrac{3}{2}-\alpha-w+z,\tfrac{1}{2}-\beta-z,w,z)
over all
c\geq 1
with
(c,hk)=1
by using the definition (
9.2.3
) of
\mathcal{P}
and Lemma
3.2
, with absolute convergence ensured by Lemma
9.5
. This and (
9.2.33
) lead to

(9.2.34)

\begin{split}R_{0}&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in A\\
\beta\in B\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{2(2\pi i)^{2}}\int_{(1+\epsilon)}\int_{(\epsilon/2)}X^{2-\alpha-\beta-w}Q^{w}\widetilde{V}(\tfrac{3}{2}-\alpha-w+z)\widetilde{V}(\tfrac{1}{2}-\beta-z)\widetilde{W}(w)\mathcal{H}(z,w-1)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\frac{e^{\delta z}-e^{-\delta z}}{2\delta z}\zeta(2-w)\prod_{\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha}\zeta(1+\hat{\alpha}-\alpha)\prod_{\hat{\beta}\neq\beta}\zeta(1+\hat{\beta}-\beta)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times h^{1-w+z}k^{-z}\prod_{p|hk}\Bigg\{\prod_{\hat{\alpha}\in A}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+\hat{\alpha}-\alpha}}\right)\prod_{\hat{\beta}\in B}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+\hat{\beta}-\beta}}\right)\\
&\hskip 36.135pt\times\Bigg(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m,n<\infty\\
m+\text{ord}_{p}(h)=n+\text{ord}_{p}(k)\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})\left(1+\frac{p^{w}}{p^{2}(p-1)}-\frac{1}{p-1}\right)}{p^{m(1-\alpha)}p^{n(1-\beta)}p^{(1-w)\min\{m+\text{ord}_{p}(h),n+\text{ord}_{p}(k)\}}}\\
&\hskip 36.135pt+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m,n<\infty\\
m+\text{ord}_{p}(h)\neq n+\text{ord}_{p}(k)\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})\left(1-\frac{p^{w}}{p^{2}}\right)}{p^{m(1-\alpha)}p^{n(1-\beta)}p^{(1-w)\min\{m+\text{ord}_{p}(h),n+\text{ord}_{p}(k)\}}}\Bigg)\Bigg\}\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\prod_{p\nmid hk}\Bigg\{\prod_{\hat{\alpha}\in A}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+\hat{\alpha}-\alpha}}\right)\prod_{\hat{\beta}\in B}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+\hat{\beta}-\beta}}\right)\\
&\hskip 36.135pt\times\Bigg(\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{w}}\right)\left(1+\frac{p^{w-1}-1}{p(p-1)}\right)+\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{m})\left(1+\frac{p^{w}}{p^{2}(p-1)}-\frac{1}{p-1}\right)}{p^{m(3-\alpha-\beta-w)}}\\
&\hskip 36.135pt+\sum_{0\leq m<n<\infty}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})\left(1-\frac{p^{w}}{p^{2}}\right)}{p^{m(2-\alpha-w)}p^{n(1-\beta)}}+\sum_{0\leq n<m<\infty}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})\left(1-\frac{p^{w}}{p^{2}}\right)}{p^{m(1-\alpha)}p^{n(2-\beta-w)}}\Bigg)\Bigg\}\,dz\,dw.\end{split}

From (
9.2.27
), (
9.2.30
), (
9.2.32
), we deduce that

(9.2.35)

I_{1}=R_{0}+O\bigg(\frac{(XChk)^{\varepsilon}Q^{2}(h,k)}{C\sqrt{hk}}\bigg)+O\Big(X^{\varepsilon}Q^{\frac{3}{2}}h^{\varepsilon}k^{\varepsilon}\Big),

where
R_{0}
is expressed as a finite sum of contour integrals in (
9.2.34
).

To be able to shift the contours and evaluate residues, we analytically continue the integrand in (
9.2.34
) by multiplying it by

(9.2.36)

\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha\\
\hat{\beta}\neq\beta\end{subarray}}\zeta(3+\hat{\alpha}+\hat{\beta}-\alpha-\beta-w)

and dividing it by the Euler product of (
9.2.36
). The result is

\displaystyle R_{0}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in A\\
\beta\in B\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{2(2\pi i)^{2}}\int_{(1+\epsilon)}\int_{(\epsilon/2)}X^{2-\alpha-\beta-w}Q^{w}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.06749pt\times\widetilde{V}(\tfrac{3}{2}-\alpha-w+z)\widetilde{V}(\tfrac{1}{2}-\beta-z)\widetilde{W}(w)\mathcal{H}(z,w-1)\frac{e^{\delta z}-e^{-\delta z}}{2\delta z}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.06749pt\times\zeta(2-w)\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha\\
\hat{\beta}\neq\beta\end{subarray}}\zeta(3+\hat{\alpha}+\hat{\beta}-\alpha-\beta-w)\prod_{\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha}\zeta(1+\hat{\alpha}-\alpha)\prod_{\hat{\beta}\neq\beta}\zeta(1+\hat{\beta}-\beta)

(9.2.37)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.06749pt\times h^{1-w+z}k^{-z}\mathcal{G}(w,\alpha,\beta)\,dz\,dw,

where
\mathcal{G}(w,\alpha,\beta)
is defined by

(9.2.38)

\begin{split}\mathcal{G}(w&,\alpha,\beta)=\mathcal{G}(w,\alpha,\beta;A,B,h,k)\\
&:=\prod_{p|hk}\Bigg\{\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha\\
\hat{\beta}\neq\beta\end{subarray}}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{3+\hat{\alpha}+\hat{\beta}-\alpha-\beta-w}}\right)\prod_{\hat{\alpha}\in A}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+\hat{\alpha}-\alpha}}\right)\prod_{\hat{\beta}\in B}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+\hat{\beta}-\beta}}\right)\\
&\hskip 54.2025pt\times\Bigg(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m,n<\infty\\
m+\text{ord}_{p}(h)=n+\text{ord}_{p}(k)\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})\left(1+\frac{p^{w}}{p^{2}(p-1)}-\frac{1}{p-1}\right)}{p^{m(1-\alpha)}p^{n(1-\beta)}p^{(1-w)\min\{m+\text{ord}_{p}(h),n+\text{ord}_{p}(k)\}}}\\
&\hskip 54.2025pt+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m,n<\infty\\
m+\text{ord}_{p}(h)\neq n+\text{ord}_{p}(k)\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})\left(1-\frac{p^{w}}{p^{2}}\right)}{p^{m(1-\alpha)}p^{n(1-\beta)}p^{(1-w)\min\{m+\text{ord}_{p}(h),n+\text{ord}_{p}(k)\}}}\Bigg)\Bigg\}\\
&\hskip 36.135pt\times\prod_{p\nmid hk}\Bigg\{\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha\\
\hat{\beta}\neq\beta\end{subarray}}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{3+\hat{\alpha}+\hat{\beta}-\alpha-\beta-w}}\right)\prod_{\hat{\alpha}\in A}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+\hat{\alpha}-\alpha}}\right)\prod_{\hat{\beta}\in B}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+\hat{\beta}-\beta}}\right)\\
&\hskip 54.2025pt\times\Bigg(\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{w}}\right)\left(1+\frac{p^{w-1}-1}{p(p-1)}\right)+\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{m})\left(1+\frac{p^{w}}{p^{2}(p-1)}-\frac{1}{p-1}\right)}{p^{m(3-\alpha-\beta-w)}}\\
&\hskip 54.2025pt+\sum_{0\leq m<n<\infty}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})\left(1-\frac{p^{w}}{p^{2}}\right)}{p^{m(2-\alpha-w)}p^{n(1-\beta)}}+\sum_{0\leq n<m<\infty}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})\left(1-\frac{p^{w}}{p^{2}}\right)}{p^{m(1-\alpha)}p^{n(2-\beta-w)}}\Bigg)\Bigg\}.\end{split}

We next prove the following lemma, which we will use to justify moving the lines of integration and bound some of the integrals that remain after applying the residue theorem.

Lemma 9.6
.

Suppose that
\epsilon>0
is arbitrarily small. Let
\alpha\in A
and
\beta\in B
, and let
h
and
k
be positive integers. If
w
is a complex number such that

1+\epsilon\leq\re(w)\leq\frac{5}{2}-\epsilon,

then the product (
9.2.38
) defining
\mathcal{G}(w,\alpha,\beta;A,B,h,k)
converges absolutely and we have

\mathcal{G}(w,\alpha,\beta;A,B,h,k)\ll_{\varepsilon}h^{\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}k^{\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}(h,k)^{\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}.

Proof.

In this proof, we will repeatedly apply without mention the bounds
\tau_{A}(m)\ll m^{\varepsilon}
, and
\tau_{B}(n)\ll n^{\varepsilon}
, which follow from (
3.2
) and the assumption that
\alpha,\beta\ll 1/\log Q
for all
\alpha\in A
and
\beta\in B
. Since
\re(w)\leq\frac{5}{2}-\epsilon
, we have

\frac{1}{p^{3+\hat{\alpha}+\hat{\beta}-\alpha-\beta-w}}\ll\frac{1}{p^{\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}}

for all
\hat{\alpha}\in A
and
\hat{\beta}\in B
. Also, it holds that

\frac{1}{p^{1+\hat{\alpha}-\alpha}}\ll\frac{1}{p^{1-\varepsilon}}

and

\frac{1}{p^{1+\hat{\beta}-\beta}}\ll\frac{1}{p^{1-\varepsilon}}

for all
\hat{\alpha}\in A
and
\hat{\beta}\in B
. Hence, multiplying out the product and applying the definition (
3.1
) gives

(9.2.39)

\begin{split}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha\\
\hat{\beta}\neq\beta\end{subarray}}&\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{3+\hat{\alpha}+\hat{\beta}-\alpha-\beta-w}}\right)\prod_{\hat{\alpha}\in A}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+\hat{\alpha}-\alpha}}\right)\prod_{\hat{\beta}\in B}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+\hat{\beta}-\beta}}\right)\\
&=1-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha\\
\hat{\beta}\neq\beta\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{p^{3+\hat{\alpha}+\hat{\beta}-\alpha-\beta-w}}-\sum_{\hat{\alpha}\in A}\frac{1}{p^{1+\hat{\alpha}-\alpha}}-\sum_{\hat{\beta}\in B}\frac{1}{p^{1+\hat{\beta}-\beta}}+O\left(\frac{1}{p^{1+\varepsilon}}\right)\\
&=1-\frac{\tau_{A}(p)\tau_{B}(p)}{p^{3-\alpha-\beta-w}}+\frac{\tau_{A}(p)}{p^{3-\alpha-w}}+\frac{\tau_{B}(p)}{p^{3-\beta-w}}-\frac{1}{p^{3-w}}-\frac{\tau_{A}(p)}{p^{1-\alpha}}-\frac{\tau_{B}(p)}{p^{1-\beta}}+O\left(\frac{1}{p^{1+\varepsilon}}\right).\end{split}

Since
1+\epsilon\leq\re(w)\leq\frac{5}{2}-\epsilon
, we have

(9.2.40)

\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{w}}\right)\left(1+\frac{p^{w-1}-1}{p(p-1)}\right)=1+\frac{1}{p^{3-w}}+O\left(\frac{1}{p^{1+\varepsilon}}\right).

The assumption
\re(w)\leq\frac{5}{2}-\epsilon
also implies
\re(3-\alpha-\beta-w)\geq\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon
and thus

(9.2.41)

\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{m})\left(1+\frac{p^{w}}{p^{2}(p-1)}-\frac{1}{p-1}\right)}{p^{m(3-\alpha-\beta-w)}}=\frac{\tau_{A}(p)\tau_{B}(p)}{p^{3-\alpha-\beta-w}}+O\left(\frac{1}{p^{1+\varepsilon}}\right).

Next, since
p^{w}/p^{2}\ll p^{\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon}
, the terms with
m\geq 1
in the sum

\sum_{0\leq m<n<\infty}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})\left(1-\frac{p^{w}}{p^{2}}\right)}{p^{m(2-\alpha-w)}p^{n(1-\beta)}}

add up to at most

\ll\sum_{1\leq m<n<\infty}\frac{p^{\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon}}{p^{m(-\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon+\epsilon)}p^{n(1-\varepsilon)}}\ll\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\frac{p^{\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon}}{p^{m(-\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon+\epsilon)}p^{(m+1)(1-\varepsilon)}}\ll\frac{1}{p^{1+\varepsilon}},

while the terms with
m=0
and
n\geq 2
add up to at most

\ll\sum_{n=2}^{\infty}\frac{p^{\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon}}{p^{n(1-\varepsilon)}}\ll\frac{1}{p^{\frac{3}{2}+\varepsilon}}.

Hence

(9.2.42)

\sum_{0\leq m<n<\infty}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})\left(1-\frac{p^{w}}{p^{2}}\right)}{p^{m(2-\alpha-w)}p^{n(1-\beta)}}=\frac{\tau_{B}(p)}{p^{1-\beta}}-\frac{\tau_{B}(p)}{p^{3-\beta-w}}+O\left(\frac{1}{p^{1+\varepsilon}}\right).

Similarly, or by symmetry, we have

(9.2.43)

\sum_{0\leq n<m<\infty}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})\left(1-\frac{p^{w}}{p^{2}}\right)}{p^{m(1-\alpha)}p^{n(2-\beta-w)}}=\frac{\tau_{A}(p)}{p^{1-\alpha}}-\frac{\tau_{A}(p)}{p^{3-\alpha-w}}+O\left(\frac{1}{p^{1+\varepsilon}}\right).

We next bound the sum

(9.2.44)

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m,n<\infty\\
m+\text{ord}_{p}(h)=n+\text{ord}_{p}(k)\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})\left(1+\frac{p^{w}}{p^{2}(p-1)}-\frac{1}{p-1}\right)}{p^{m(1-\alpha)}p^{n(1-\beta)}p^{(1-w)\min\{m+\text{ord}_{p}(h),n+\text{ord}_{p}(k)\}}}.

For brevity, we denote
h_{p}:=\text{ord}_{p}(h)
and
k_{p}:=\text{ord}_{p}(k)
for the rest of this proof. We make the change of variable
m\mapsto\nu+k_{p}
in (
9.2.44
), so that
n=\nu+h_{p}
, to see that (
9.2.44
) equals

p^{k_{p}(w+\alpha-2)+h_{p}(w+\beta-2)}\sum_{\nu=-\min\{h_{p},k_{p}\}}^{\infty}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{\nu+k_{p}})\tau_{B}(p^{\nu+h_{p}})\left(1+\frac{p^{w}}{p^{2}(p-1)}-\frac{1}{p-1}\right)}{p^{\nu(3-\alpha-\beta-w)}}.

This and the inequality
(k_{p}+h_{p}-\min\{h_{p},k_{p}\})\re(w)\leq\frac{5}{2}(k_{p}+h_{p}-\min\{h_{p},k_{p}\})
imply

(9.2.45)

\begin{split}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m,n<\infty\\
m+\text{ord}_{p}(h)=n+\text{ord}_{p}(k)\end{subarray}}&\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})\left(1+\frac{p^{w}}{p^{2}(p-1)}-\frac{1}{p-1}\right)}{p^{m(1-\alpha)}p^{n(1-\beta)}p^{(1-w)\min\{m+\text{ord}_{p}(h),n+\text{ord}_{p}(k)\}}}\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\ll p^{k_{p}(\re(w)-2+\varepsilon)+h_{p}(\re(w)-2+\varepsilon)+\min\{h_{p},k_{p}\}\re(3-w)}\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\ll p^{(\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon)(h_{p}+k_{p}+\min\{h_{p},k_{p}\})}.\end{split}

Next, to bound the sum

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m,n<\infty\\
m+\text{ord}_{p}(h)<n+\text{ord}_{p}(k)\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})\left(1-\frac{p^{w}}{p^{2}}\right)}{p^{m(1-\alpha)}p^{n(1-\beta)}p^{(1-w)\min\{m+\text{ord}_{p}(h),n+\text{ord}_{p}(k)\}}},

we split it into the part with
m<k_{p}-h_{p}
and the part with
m\geq k_{p}-h_{p}
to deduce that

(9.2.46)

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m,n<\infty\\
m+\text{ord}_{p}(h)<n+\text{ord}_{p}(k)\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})\left(1-\frac{p^{w}}{p^{2}}\right)}{p^{m(1-\alpha)}p^{n(1-\beta)}p^{(1-w)\min\{m+\text{ord}_{p}(h),n+\text{ord}_{p}(k)\}}}=\Sigma_{1}+\Sigma_{2},

where

(9.2.47)

\Sigma_{1}:=p^{(w-1)h_{p}}\sum_{m=0}^{k_{p}-h_{p}-1}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})\left(1-\frac{p^{w}}{p^{2}}\right)}{p^{m(2-\alpha-w)}p^{n(1-\beta)}}

and

(9.2.48)

\Sigma_{2}:=p^{(w-1)h_{p}}\sum_{m=\max\{0,k_{p}-h_{p}\}}^{\infty}\sum_{n=m+h_{p}-k_{p}+1}^{\infty}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})\left(1-\frac{p^{w}}{p^{2}}\right)}{p^{m(2-\alpha-w)}p^{n(1-\beta)}}.

We apply
\re(w)\leq\frac{5}{2}-\epsilon
and bound the
n
-sums in (
9.2.47
) and (
9.2.48
) to deduce that

(9.2.49)

\Sigma_{1}\ll p^{(\frac{3}{2}-\epsilon)h_{p}}\sum_{m=0}^{k_{p}-h_{p}-1}\frac{p^{\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon}}{p^{m(-\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon+\epsilon)}}

and

(9.2.50)

\Sigma_{2}\ll p^{(\frac{3}{2}-\epsilon)h_{p}}\sum_{m=\max\{0,k_{p}-h_{p}\}}^{\infty}\frac{p^{\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon}}{p^{m(\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon+\epsilon)}p^{(h_{p}-k_{p}+1)(1-\varepsilon)}}.

The right-hand side of (
9.2.49
) is zero if
h_{p}\geq k_{p}
, and otherwise it is
\ll p^{(\frac{3}{2}-\epsilon)h_{p}+(k_{p}-h_{p})(\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon)}
. In either case, we have

(9.2.51)

\Sigma_{1}\ll p^{(\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon)(h_{p}+k_{p}+\min\{h_{p},k_{p}\})}.

If
h_{p}\geq k_{p}
, then the
m
-sum in (
9.2.50
) starts at
m=0
and thus

\Sigma_{2}\ll\frac{p^{(\frac{3}{2}-\epsilon)h_{p}+\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon}}{p^{(h_{p}-k_{p}+1)(1-\varepsilon)}}\ll p^{(\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon)h_{p}+k_{p}-\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}.

On the other hand, if
h_{p}<k_{p}
, then the
m
-sum in (
9.2.50
) starts at
m=k_{p}-h_{p}
and hence

\Sigma_{2}\ll\frac{p^{(\frac{3}{2}-\epsilon)h_{p}+\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon}}{p^{(k_{p}-h_{p})(\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon+\epsilon)}p^{(h_{p}-k_{p}+1)(1-\varepsilon)}}\ll p^{(1+\varepsilon)h_{p}+(\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon)k_{p}-\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}.

In either case, we have

\Sigma_{2}\ll p^{(\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon)(h_{p}+k_{p}+\min\{h_{p},k_{p}\})-\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}.

From this, (
9.2.51
), and (
9.2.46
), we arrive at

(9.2.52)

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m,n<\infty\\
m+\text{ord}_{p}(h)<n+\text{ord}_{p}(k)\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})\left(1-\frac{p^{w}}{p^{2}}\right)}{p^{m(1-\alpha)}p^{n(1-\beta)}p^{(1-w)\min\{m+\text{ord}_{p}(h),n+\text{ord}_{p}(k)\}}}\ll p^{(\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon)(h_{p}+k_{p}+\min\{h_{p},k_{p}\})}.

Similarly, or by symmetry, it holds that

(9.2.53)

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m,n<\infty\\
m+\text{ord}_{p}(h)>n+\text{ord}_{p}(k)\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})\left(1-\frac{p^{w}}{p^{2}}\right)}{p^{m(1-\alpha)}p^{n(1-\beta)}p^{(1-w)\min\{m+\text{ord}_{p}(h),n+\text{ord}_{p}(k)\}}}\ll p^{(\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon)(h_{p}+k_{p}+\min\{h_{p},k_{p}\})}.

From (
9.2.39
), (
9.2.40
), (
9.2.41
), (
9.2.42
), and (
9.2.43
), we deduce that if
p\nmid hk
then the local factor in (
9.2.38
) corresponding to
p
is
1+O(p^{-1-\varepsilon})
. To bound the local factors corresponding to the primes
p|hk
, observe that (
9.2.39
) is
O(1)
because
\re(w)\leq\frac{5}{2}-\epsilon
. This, (
9.2.45
), (
9.2.52
), and (
9.2.53
) imply that if
p|hk
then the local factor corresponding to
p
is

\ll p^{(\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon)(h_{p}+k_{p}+\min\{h_{p},k_{p}\})}.

We conclude that the right-hand side of (
9.2.38
) converges absolutely, and is

\ll h^{\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}k^{\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}(h,k)^{\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}

because
hk(h,k)=\prod_{p|hk}p^{h_{p}+k_{p}+\min\{h_{p},k_{p}\}}
and
\prod_{p|\nu}O(1)\ll{\nu}^{\varepsilon}
for any positive integer
\nu
.
∎

We now move the
w
-line in (
9.2.37
) rightward to
\re(w)=\frac{5}{2}-\epsilon
to deduce that

(9.2.54)

R_{0}=R_{1}+R_{2}+R_{3}+R_{4},

where
R_{1}
is the integral of the residue at
w=2
,
R_{2}
is the integral of the residue at
w=\frac{3}{2}-\alpha+z
,
R_{3}
is the integral of the residues at the poles of (
9.2.36
), and
R_{4}
is the new integral with
\re(w)=\frac{5}{2}-\epsilon
.

We first bound
R_{4}
, which is defined by

\begin{split}R_{4}&:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in A\\
\beta\in B\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{2(2\pi i)^{2}}\int_{(\frac{5}{2}-\epsilon)}\int_{(\epsilon/2)}X^{2-\alpha-\beta-w}Q^{w}\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\widetilde{V}(\tfrac{3}{2}-\alpha-w+z)\widetilde{V}(\tfrac{1}{2}-\beta-z)\widetilde{W}(w)\mathcal{H}(z,w-1)\frac{e^{\delta z}-e^{-\delta z}}{2\delta z}\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\zeta(2-w)\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha\\
\hat{\beta}\neq\beta\end{subarray}}\zeta(3+\hat{\alpha}+\hat{\beta}-\alpha-\beta-w)\prod_{\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha}\zeta(1+\hat{\alpha}-\alpha)\prod_{\hat{\beta}\neq\beta}\zeta(1+\hat{\beta}-\beta)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times h^{1-w+z}k^{-z}\mathcal{G}(w,\alpha,\beta)\,dz\,dw.\end{split}

We move the
z
-line to
\re(z)=\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon
, traversing no poles in the process. We then bound the resulting integral using (
3.6
), (
3.10
), (
3.15
), and Lemma
9.6
. The result is

(9.2.55)

R_{4}\ll X^{-\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}Q^{\frac{5}{2}}(hk)^{\varepsilon}.

We next evaluate the integral
R_{1}
defined in (
9.2.54
). To do this, observe that the winding number in the application of the residue theorem in (
9.2.54
) is
-1
. Also, the definition (
3.13
) implies that

\underset{w=2}{\text{Res}}\ \mathcal{H}(z,w-1)=-2

because Res
{}_{s=0}\Gamma(s)=1
and
\Gamma(1/2)=\sqrt{\pi}
. Furthermore,
\zeta(0)=-1/2
. Hence

(9.2.56)

\begin{split}R_{1}&=-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in A\\
\beta\in B\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{4\pi i}\int_{(\epsilon/2)}X^{-\alpha-\beta}Q^{2}\widetilde{V}(-\tfrac{1}{2}-\alpha+z)\widetilde{V}(\tfrac{1}{2}-\beta-z)\widetilde{W}(2)\frac{e^{\delta z}-e^{-\delta z}}{2\delta z}\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha\\
\hat{\beta}\neq\beta\end{subarray}}\zeta(1+\hat{\alpha}+\hat{\beta}-\alpha-\beta)\prod_{\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha}\zeta(1+\hat{\alpha}-\alpha)\prod_{\hat{\beta}\neq\beta}\zeta(1+\hat{\beta}-\beta)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times h^{-1+z}k^{-z}\mathcal{G}(2,\alpha,\beta)\,dz.\end{split}

Some factors here do not depend on
z
, and we only need to evaluate

\int_{(\epsilon/2)}\widetilde{V}(-\tfrac{1}{2}-\alpha+z)\widetilde{V}(\tfrac{1}{2}-\beta-z)\frac{e^{\delta z}-e^{-\delta z}}{2\delta z}h^{z}k^{-z}\,dz.

The part of this with
|\text{Im}z|\geq 1/\delta
is negligible because of (
3.10
) and the definition (
3.4
) of
\delta
. In the complementary part with
|\text{Im}z|\leq 1/\delta
, we have

(9.2.57)

\frac{e^{\delta z}-e^{-\delta z}}{2\delta z}=1+O(\delta|z|).

Thus

\begin{split}\int_{(\epsilon/2)}\widetilde{V}(-\tfrac{1}{2}-\alpha+z)&\widetilde{V}(\tfrac{1}{2}-\beta-z)\frac{e^{\delta z}-e^{-\delta z}}{2\delta z}h^{z}k^{-z}\,dz\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt=\int_{\frac{\epsilon}{2}-\frac{i}{\delta}}^{\frac{\epsilon}{2}+\frac{i}{\delta}}\widetilde{V}(-\tfrac{1}{2}-\alpha+z)\widetilde{V}(\tfrac{1}{2}-\beta-z)h^{z}k^{-z}\,dz+O\big((hk)^{\varepsilon}\delta\big).\end{split}

By (
3.4
) and (
3.10
), we may extend the range of Im
(z)
in the latter integral to
(-\infty,\infty)
by adding a negligible quantity. We then make the change of variables
s\mapsto\frac{1}{2}-\beta-z
, and afterward move the line of integration to
\re(s)=\epsilon
. We traverse no poles in doing so, and we arrive at

\begin{split}\int_{(\epsilon/2)}\widetilde{V}(-\tfrac{1}{2}-\alpha+z)&\widetilde{V}(\tfrac{1}{2}-\beta-z)\frac{e^{\delta z}-e^{-\delta z}}{2\delta z}h^{z}k^{-z}\,dz\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt=\int_{(\epsilon)}\widetilde{V}(-\alpha-\beta-s)\widetilde{V}(s)\left(\frac{h}{k}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}-\beta-s}\,ds+O\big((hk)^{\varepsilon}\delta\big).\end{split}

From this and (
9.2.56
), we deduce that

(9.2.58)

\begin{split}R_{1}&=-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in A\\
\beta\in B\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{4\pi i}\int_{(\epsilon)}X^{-\alpha-\beta}Q^{2}\widetilde{V}(-\alpha-\beta-s)\widetilde{V}(s)\widetilde{W}(2)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha\\
\hat{\beta}\neq\beta\end{subarray}}\zeta(1+\hat{\alpha}+\hat{\beta}-\alpha-\beta)\prod_{\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha}\zeta(1+\hat{\alpha}-\alpha)\prod_{\hat{\beta}\neq\beta}\zeta(1+\hat{\beta}-\beta)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times h^{-\frac{1}{2}-\beta-s}k^{-\frac{1}{2}+\beta+s}\mathcal{G}(2,\alpha,\beta)\,ds+O\big((Xhk)^{\varepsilon}k^{1/2}Q^{-96}\big),\end{split}

where we have applied (
3.4
), (
3.6
), and Lemma
9.6
to bound the error term.

Having evaluated
R_{1}
, we next turn our attention to the integral
R_{2}
defined in (
9.2.54
). By (
3.9
), the residue of
\widetilde{V}(\frac{3}{2}-\alpha-w+z)
at
w=\frac{3}{2}-\alpha+z
is
-1
. From this and the fact that the winding number in the application of the residue theorem in (
9.2.54
) is
-1
, we deduce that

\begin{split}R_{2}&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in A\\
\beta\in B\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{4\pi i}\int_{(\epsilon/2)}X^{\frac{1}{2}-\beta-z}Q^{\frac{3}{2}-\alpha+z}\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\widetilde{V}(\tfrac{1}{2}-\beta-z)\widetilde{W}(\tfrac{3}{2}-\alpha+z)\mathcal{H}(z,\tfrac{1}{2}-\alpha+z)\frac{e^{\delta z}-e^{-\delta z}}{2\delta z}\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\zeta(\tfrac{1}{2}+\alpha-z)\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha\\
\hat{\beta}\neq\beta\end{subarray}}\zeta(\tfrac{3}{2}+\hat{\alpha}+\hat{\beta}-\beta-z)\prod_{\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha}\zeta(1+\hat{\alpha}-\alpha)\prod_{\hat{\beta}\neq\beta}\zeta(1+\hat{\beta}-\beta)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times h^{-\frac{1}{2}+\alpha}k^{-z}\mathcal{G}(\tfrac{3}{2}-\alpha+z,\alpha,\beta)\,dz.\end{split}

We move the line of integration to
\re(z)=1-2\epsilon
to deduce that

(9.2.59)

R_{2}=R_{21}+R_{22}+R_{23}+R_{24},

where
R_{21}
is the residue at
z=\frac{1}{2}-\beta
,
R_{22}
is the residue at
z=\frac{1}{2}+\alpha
,
R_{23}
is the sum of the residues at the poles
z=\frac{1}{2}+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime}-\beta
, where
\alpha^{\prime}
runs through the elements of
A\smallsetminus\{\alpha\}
and
\beta^{\prime}
runs through the elements of
B\smallsetminus\{\beta\}
, and
R_{24}
is the new integral with
\re(z)=1-2\epsilon
. To bound
R_{24}
, we apply (
3.6
), (
3.10
), (
3.15
), and Lemma
9.6
. The result is

(9.2.60)

R_{24}\ll X^{-\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}Q^{\frac{5}{2}}(hk)^{\varepsilon}.

We next estimate the residue
R_{21}
defined in (
9.2.59
). By (
3.9
), the residue of
\widetilde{V}(\frac{1}{2}-\beta-z)
at
z=\frac{1}{2}-\beta
is
-1
. From this and the fact that the winding number in the application of the residue theorem in (
9.2.59
) is
-1
, we have

(9.2.61)

\begin{split}R_{21}&=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in A\\
\beta\in B\end{subarray}}Q^{2-\alpha-\beta}\widetilde{W}(2-\alpha-\beta)\mathcal{H}(\tfrac{1}{2}-\beta,1-\alpha-\beta)\frac{e^{\delta(\frac{1}{2}-\beta)}-e^{-\delta(\frac{1}{2}-\beta)}}{\delta(1-2\beta)}\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\zeta(\alpha+\beta)\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha\\
\hat{\beta}\neq\beta\end{subarray}}\zeta(1+\hat{\alpha}+\hat{\beta})\prod_{\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha}\zeta(1+\hat{\alpha}-\alpha)\prod_{\hat{\beta}\neq\beta}\zeta(1+\hat{\beta}-\beta)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times h^{-\frac{1}{2}+\alpha}k^{-\frac{1}{2}+\beta}\mathcal{G}(2-\alpha-\beta,\alpha,\beta).\end{split}

Now (
3.14
) gives

\mathcal{H}(\tfrac{1}{2}-\beta,1-\alpha-\beta)=\mathscr{X}(1-\alpha-\beta)\mathscr{X}(\tfrac{1}{2}+\beta)\mathscr{X}(\tfrac{1}{2}+\alpha),

and thus the functional equation of
\zeta(s)
implies

\zeta(\alpha+\beta)\mathcal{H}(\tfrac{1}{2}-\beta,1-\alpha-\beta)=\zeta(1-\alpha-\beta)\mathscr{X}(\tfrac{1}{2}+\beta)\mathscr{X}(\tfrac{1}{2}+\alpha).

It follows from this and (
9.2.61
) that

(9.2.62)

\begin{split}R_{21}&=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in A\\
\beta\in B\end{subarray}}Q^{2-\alpha-\beta}\widetilde{W}(2-\alpha-\beta)\mathscr{X}(\tfrac{1}{2}+\alpha)\mathscr{X}(\tfrac{1}{2}+\beta)\frac{e^{\delta(\frac{1}{2}-\beta)}-e^{-\delta(\frac{1}{2}-\beta)}}{\delta(1-2\beta)}\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\zeta(1-\alpha-\beta)\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha\\
\hat{\beta}\neq\beta\end{subarray}}\zeta(1+\hat{\alpha}+\hat{\beta})\prod_{\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha}\zeta(1+\hat{\alpha}-\alpha)\prod_{\hat{\beta}\neq\beta}\zeta(1+\hat{\beta}-\beta)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times h^{-\frac{1}{2}+\alpha}k^{-\frac{1}{2}+\beta}\mathcal{G}(2-\alpha-\beta,\alpha,\beta).\end{split}

By (
3.4
) and the assumption that
\alpha,\beta\ll 1/\log Q
for all
\alpha\in A
and
\beta\in B
, we have

\frac{e^{\delta(\frac{1}{2}-\beta)}-e^{-\delta(\frac{1}{2}-\beta)}}{\delta(1-2\beta)}=1+O\big(Q^{-99}\big).

We insert this into (
9.2.62
) and apply Lemma
9.6
and (
3.6
) to bound the contribution of the error term. The result is

(9.2.63)

\begin{split}R_{21}&=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in A\\
\beta\in B\end{subarray}}Q^{2-\alpha-\beta}\widetilde{W}(2-\alpha-\beta)\mathscr{X}(\tfrac{1}{2}+\alpha)\mathscr{X}(\tfrac{1}{2}+\beta)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\zeta(1-\alpha-\beta)\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha\\
\hat{\beta}\neq\beta\end{subarray}}\zeta(1+\hat{\alpha}+\hat{\beta})\prod_{\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha}\zeta(1+\hat{\alpha}-\alpha)\prod_{\hat{\beta}\neq\beta}\zeta(1+\hat{\beta}-\beta)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times h^{-\frac{1}{2}+\alpha}k^{-\frac{1}{2}+\beta}\mathcal{G}(2-\alpha-\beta,\alpha,\beta)+O\big((hk)^{\varepsilon}(h,k)^{1/2}Q^{-96}\big).\end{split}

Our next task is to evaluate the residue
R_{22}
defined in (
9.2.59
). To do this, observe that the winding number in the application of the residue theorem in (
9.2.59
) is
-1
. Moreover, the definition (
3.13
) implies that

\underset{z=\frac{1}{2}+\alpha}{\text{Res}}\ \mathcal{H}(z,\tfrac{1}{2}-\alpha+z)=-2

because Res
{}_{s=0}\Gamma(s)=1
and
\Gamma(1/2)=\sqrt{\pi}
. Furthermore,
\zeta(0)=-1/2
. Hence

(9.2.64)

\begin{split}R_{22}&=-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in A\\
\beta\in B\end{subarray}}X^{-\alpha-\beta}Q^{2}\widetilde{V}(-\alpha-\beta)\widetilde{W}(2)\frac{e^{\delta(\frac{1}{2}+\alpha)}-e^{-\delta(\frac{1}{2}+\alpha)}}{\delta(1+2\alpha)}\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha\\
\hat{\beta}\neq\beta\end{subarray}}\zeta(1+\hat{\alpha}+\hat{\beta}-\alpha-\beta)\prod_{\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha}\zeta(1+\hat{\alpha}-\alpha)\prod_{\hat{\beta}\neq\beta}\zeta(1+\hat{\beta}-\beta)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times h^{-\frac{1}{2}+\alpha}k^{-\frac{1}{2}-\alpha}\mathcal{G}(2,\alpha,\beta).\end{split}

By (
3.4
) and the assumption that
\alpha,\beta\ll 1/\log Q
for all
\alpha\in A
and
\beta\in B
, we have

\frac{e^{\delta(\frac{1}{2}+\alpha)}-e^{-\delta(\frac{1}{2}+\alpha)}}{\delta(1+2\alpha)}=1+O\big(Q^{-99}\big).

We insert this into (
9.2.64
) and apply Lemma
9.6
and (
3.6
) to bound the contribution of the error term. The result is

(9.2.65)

\begin{split}R_{22}&=-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in A\\
\beta\in B\end{subarray}}X^{-\alpha-\beta}Q^{2}\widetilde{V}(-\alpha-\beta)\widetilde{W}(2)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha\\
\hat{\beta}\neq\beta\end{subarray}}\zeta(1+\hat{\alpha}+\hat{\beta}-\alpha-\beta)\prod_{\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha}\zeta(1+\hat{\alpha}-\alpha)\prod_{\hat{\beta}\neq\beta}\zeta(1+\hat{\beta}-\beta)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times h^{-\frac{1}{2}+\alpha}k^{-\frac{1}{2}-\alpha}\mathcal{G}(2,\alpha,\beta)+O\big((Xhk)^{\varepsilon}(h,k)^{1/2}Q^{-96}\big).\end{split}

We next estimate the sum
R_{23}
defined in (
9.2.59
). Since the winding number in the application of the residue theorem in (
9.2.59
) is
-1
and

\underset{z=\frac{1}{2}+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime}-\beta}{\text{Res}}\ \zeta(\tfrac{3}{2}+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime}-\beta-z)=-1,

it follows that

\displaystyle R_{23}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in A\\
\beta\in B\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha^{\prime}\neq\alpha\\
\beta^{\prime}\neq\beta\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{2}X^{-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime}}Q^{2-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.06749pt\times\widetilde{V}(-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime})\widetilde{W}(2-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime})\mathcal{H}(\tfrac{1}{2}+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime}-\beta,1-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime})

\displaystyle\hskip 18.06749pt\times\frac{e^{\delta(\frac{1}{2}+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime}-\beta)}-e^{-\delta(\frac{1}{2}+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime}-\beta)}}{2\delta(\frac{1}{2}+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime}-\beta)}\zeta(\alpha+\beta-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime})

\displaystyle\hskip 18.06749pt\times\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha\\
\hat{\beta}\neq\beta\\
(\hat{\alpha},\hat{\beta})\neq(\alpha^{\prime},\beta^{\prime})\end{subarray}}\zeta(1+\hat{\alpha}+\hat{\beta}-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime})\prod_{\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha}\zeta(1+\hat{\alpha}-\alpha)\prod_{\hat{\beta}\neq\beta}\zeta(1+\hat{\beta}-\beta)

(9.2.66)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.06749pt\times h^{-\frac{1}{2}+\alpha}k^{-\frac{1}{2}-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime}+\beta}\mathcal{G}(2-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime},\alpha,\beta).

Now (
3.14
) gives

\mathcal{H}(\tfrac{1}{2}+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime}-\beta,1-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime})=\mathscr{X}(1-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime})\mathscr{X}(\tfrac{1}{2}+\beta-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime})\mathscr{X}(\tfrac{1}{2}+\alpha),

and thus the functional equation of
\zeta(s)
implies

\begin{split}&\zeta(\alpha+\beta-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime})\mathcal{H}(\tfrac{1}{2}+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime}-\beta,1-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime})\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt=\zeta(1-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime})\mathscr{X}(\tfrac{1}{2}+\beta-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime})\mathscr{X}(\tfrac{1}{2}+\alpha).\end{split}

It follows from this and (
9.2.66
) that

(9.2.67)

\begin{split}R_{23}&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in A\\
\beta\in B\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha^{\prime}\neq\alpha\\
\beta^{\prime}\neq\beta\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{2}X^{-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime}}Q^{2-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime}}\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\widetilde{V}(-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime})\widetilde{W}(2-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime})\mathscr{X}(\tfrac{1}{2}+\beta-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime})\mathscr{X}(\tfrac{1}{2}+\alpha)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\frac{e^{\delta(\frac{1}{2}+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime}-\beta)}-e^{-\delta(\frac{1}{2}+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime}-\beta)}}{2\delta(\frac{1}{2}+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime}-\beta)}\zeta(1-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime})\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha\\
\hat{\beta}\neq\beta\\
(\hat{\alpha},\hat{\beta})\neq(\alpha^{\prime},\beta^{\prime})\end{subarray}}\zeta(1+\hat{\alpha}+\hat{\beta}-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime})\prod_{\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha}\zeta(1+\hat{\alpha}-\alpha)\prod_{\hat{\beta}\neq\beta}\zeta(1+\hat{\beta}-\beta)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times h^{-\frac{1}{2}+\alpha}k^{-\frac{1}{2}-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime}+\beta}\mathcal{G}(2-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime},\alpha,\beta).\end{split}

By (
3.4
) and the assumption that
\alpha,\beta\ll 1/\log Q
for all
\alpha\in A
and
\beta\in B
, we have

\frac{e^{\delta(\frac{1}{2}+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime}-\beta)}-e^{-\delta(\frac{1}{2}+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime}-\beta)}}{2\delta(\frac{1}{2}+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime}-\beta)}=1+O\big(Q^{-99}\big).

We insert this into (
9.2.67
) and apply Lemma
9.6
and (
3.6
) to bound the contribution of the error term. The result is

(9.2.68)

\begin{split}R_{23}&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in A\\
\beta\in B\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha^{\prime}\neq\alpha\\
\beta^{\prime}\neq\beta\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{2}X^{-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime}}Q^{2-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime}}\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\widetilde{V}(-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime})\widetilde{W}(2-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime})\mathscr{X}(\tfrac{1}{2}+\beta-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime})\mathscr{X}(\tfrac{1}{2}+\alpha)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\zeta(1-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime})\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha\\
\hat{\beta}\neq\beta\\
(\hat{\alpha},\hat{\beta})\neq(\alpha^{\prime},\beta^{\prime})\end{subarray}}\zeta(1+\hat{\alpha}+\hat{\beta}-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime})\prod_{\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha}\zeta(1+\hat{\alpha}-\alpha)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\prod_{\hat{\beta}\neq\beta}\zeta(1+\hat{\beta}-\beta)h^{-\frac{1}{2}+\alpha}k^{-\frac{1}{2}-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime}+\beta}\mathcal{G}(2-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime},\alpha,\beta)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt+O\big((Xhk)^{\varepsilon}(h,k)^{1/2}Q^{-96}\big).\end{split}

This, (
9.2.59
), (
9.2.60
), (
9.2.63
), and (
9.2.65
) complete our evaluation of
R_{2}
.

Having estimated
R_{2}
, we next turn our attention to the integral
R_{3}
defined in (
9.2.54
). Since

\underset{w=2-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime}}{\text{Res}}\ \zeta(3+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime}-\alpha-\beta-w)=-1

and the winding number in the application of the residue theorem in (
9.2.54
) is
-1
, we may write

(9.2.69)

\begin{split}R_{3}&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in A\\
\beta\in B\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha^{\prime}\neq\alpha\\
\beta^{\prime}\neq\beta\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{4\pi i}\int_{(\epsilon/2)}X^{-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime}}Q^{2-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime}}\widetilde{V}(-\tfrac{1}{2}+\beta-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime}+z)\widetilde{V}(\tfrac{1}{2}-\beta-z)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\widetilde{W}(2-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime})\mathcal{H}(z,1-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime})\frac{e^{\delta z}-e^{-\delta z}}{2\delta z}\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\zeta(\alpha+\beta-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime})\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha\\
\hat{\beta}\neq\beta\\
(\hat{\alpha},\hat{\beta})\neq(\alpha^{\prime},\beta^{\prime})\end{subarray}}\zeta(1+\hat{\alpha}+\hat{\beta}-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime})\prod_{\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha}\zeta(1+\hat{\alpha}-\alpha)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\prod_{\hat{\beta}\neq\beta}\zeta(1+\hat{\beta}-\beta)h^{-1+\alpha+\beta-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime}+z}k^{-z}\mathcal{G}(2-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime},\alpha,\beta)\,dz.\end{split}

By (
3.14
), we have

\mathcal{H}(z,1-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime})=\mathscr{X}(1-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime})\mathscr{X}(1-z)\mathscr{X}(\alpha+\beta-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime}+z),

and thus the functional equation of
\zeta(s)
implies

\zeta(\alpha+\beta-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime})\mathcal{H}(z,1-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime})=\zeta(1-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime})\mathscr{X}(1-z)\mathscr{X}(\alpha+\beta-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime}+z).

It follows from this and (
9.2.69
) that

(9.2.70)

\begin{split}R_{3}&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in A\\
\beta\in B\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha^{\prime}\neq\alpha\\
\beta^{\prime}\neq\beta\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{4\pi i}\int_{(\epsilon/2)}X^{-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime}}Q^{2-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime}}\widetilde{V}(-\tfrac{1}{2}+\beta-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime}+z)\widetilde{V}(\tfrac{1}{2}-\beta-z)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\widetilde{W}(2-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime})\mathscr{X}(1-z)\mathscr{X}(\alpha+\beta-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime}+z)\frac{e^{\delta z}-e^{-\delta z}}{2\delta z}\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\zeta(1-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime})\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha\\
\hat{\beta}\neq\beta\\
(\hat{\alpha},\hat{\beta})\neq(\alpha^{\prime},\beta^{\prime})\end{subarray}}\zeta(1+\hat{\alpha}+\hat{\beta}-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime})\prod_{\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha}\zeta(1+\hat{\alpha}-\alpha)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\prod_{\hat{\beta}\neq\beta}\zeta(1+\hat{\beta}-\beta)h^{-1+\alpha+\beta-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime}+z}k^{-z}\mathcal{G}(2-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime},\alpha,\beta)\,dz.\end{split}

Some factors here do not depend on
z
, and we only need to evaluate

\int_{(\epsilon/2)}\widetilde{V}(-\tfrac{1}{2}+\beta-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime}+z)\widetilde{V}(\tfrac{1}{2}-\beta-z)\mathscr{X}(1-z)\mathscr{X}(\alpha+\beta-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime}+z)\frac{e^{\delta z}-e^{-\delta z}}{2\delta z}h^{z}k^{-z}\,dz.

The part of this with
|\text{Im}z|\geq 1/\delta
is negligible because of (
3.10
), (
3.12
), and the definition (
3.4
) of
\delta
. In the complementary part with
|\text{Im}z|\leq 1/\delta
, we have (
9.2.57
) and thus

\begin{split}\int_{(\epsilon/2)}&\widetilde{V}(-\tfrac{1}{2}+\beta-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime}+z)\widetilde{V}(\tfrac{1}{2}-\beta-z)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\mathscr{X}(1-z)\mathscr{X}(\alpha+\beta-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime}+z)\frac{e^{\delta z}-e^{-\delta z}}{2\delta z}h^{z}k^{-z}\,dz\\
&=\int_{\frac{\epsilon}{2}-\frac{i}{\delta}}^{\frac{\epsilon}{2}+\frac{i}{\delta}}\widetilde{V}(-\tfrac{1}{2}+\beta-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime}+z)\widetilde{V}(\tfrac{1}{2}-\beta-z)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\mathscr{X}(1-z)\mathscr{X}(\alpha+\beta-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime}+z)h^{z}k^{-z}\,dz+O\big((hk)^{\varepsilon}\delta\big).\end{split}

By (
3.4
) and (
3.10
), we may extend the range of Im
(z)
in the latter integral to
(-\infty,\infty)
by adding a negligible quantity. We then make the change of variables

s\longmapsto-\frac{1}{2}+\beta-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime}+z,

and afterward move the line of integration to
\re(s)=-\epsilon
. We traverse no poles in doing so, and we arrive at

\begin{split}\int_{(\epsilon/2)}&\widetilde{V}(-\tfrac{1}{2}+\beta-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime}+z)\widetilde{V}(\tfrac{1}{2}-\beta-z)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\mathscr{X}(1-z)\mathscr{X}(\alpha+\beta-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime}+z)\frac{e^{\delta z}-e^{-\delta z}}{2\delta z}h^{z}k^{-z}\,dz\\
&=\int_{(-\epsilon)}\widetilde{V}(s)\widetilde{V}(-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime}-s)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\mathscr{X}(\tfrac{1}{2}+\beta-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime}-s)\mathscr{X}(\tfrac{1}{2}+\alpha+s)\left(\frac{h}{k}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}-\beta+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime}+s}\,ds+O\big((hk)^{\varepsilon}\delta\big).\end{split}

From this and (
9.2.70
), we deduce that

(9.2.71)

\begin{split}R_{3}&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in A\\
\beta\in B\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha^{\prime}\neq\alpha\\
\beta^{\prime}\neq\beta\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{4\pi i}\int_{(-\epsilon)}X^{-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime}}Q^{2-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime}}\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\widetilde{V}(s)\widetilde{V}(-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime}-s)\widetilde{W}(2-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime})\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\mathscr{X}(\tfrac{1}{2}+\beta-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime}-s)\mathscr{X}(\tfrac{1}{2}+\alpha+s)\zeta(1-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime})\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha\\
\hat{\beta}\neq\beta\\
(\hat{\alpha},\hat{\beta})\neq(\alpha^{\prime},\beta^{\prime})\end{subarray}}\zeta(1+\hat{\alpha}+\hat{\beta}-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime})\prod_{\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha}\zeta(1+\hat{\alpha}-\alpha)\prod_{\hat{\beta}\neq\beta}\zeta(1+\hat{\beta}-\beta)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times h^{-\frac{1}{2}+\alpha+s}k^{-\frac{1}{2}+\beta-\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime}-s}\mathcal{G}(2-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime},\alpha,\beta)\,ds\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt+O\big((Xhk)^{\varepsilon}k^{1/2}Q^{-96}\big),\end{split}

where we have applied (
3.4
), (
3.6
), (
3.10
), (
3.15
), and Lemma
9.6
to bound the error term.

Putting together our calculations, we see from (
9.2.19
), (
9.2.26
), and (
9.2.35
) that

\mathcal{U}^{2}(h,k)=R_{0}+O\bigg(\bigg(Q^{1+\varepsilon}+\frac{Q^{2}}{C^{1-\varepsilon}}\bigg)\frac{(Xhk)^{\varepsilon}(h,k)}{\sqrt{hk}}\bigg)+O\Big(X^{\varepsilon}Q^{\frac{3}{2}}h^{\varepsilon}k^{\varepsilon}+(XChk)^{\varepsilon}kX^{2}Q^{-97}\Big).

From this, (
9.2.54
), (
9.2.55
), (
9.2.59
), and (
9.2.60
), we arrive at

(9.2.72)

\begin{split}\mathcal{U}^{2}(h,k)&=R_{1}+R_{21}+R_{22}+R_{23}+R_{3}+O\bigg(\bigg(Q^{1+\varepsilon}+\frac{Q^{2}}{C^{1-\varepsilon}}\bigg)\frac{(Xhk)^{\varepsilon}(h,k)}{\sqrt{hk}}\bigg)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt+O\Big(X^{\varepsilon}Q^{\frac{3}{2}}h^{\varepsilon}k^{\varepsilon}+X^{-\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}Q^{\frac{5}{2}}(hk)^{\varepsilon}+(XChk)^{\varepsilon}kX^{2}Q^{-97}\Big),\end{split}

where we have evaluated the residue
R_{1}
in (
9.2.58
),
R_{21}
in (
9.2.63
),
R_{22}
in (
9.2.65
),
R_{23}
in (
9.2.68
), and
R_{3}
in (
9.2.71
). In the next subsection, we will match these five residues with the five residues on the right-hand side of (
9.1.32
) in such a way that corresponding residues are equal, thus showing that
\mathcal{U}^{2}(h,k)
is equal to
\mathcal{I}_{1}^{*}(h,k)
up to an admissible error term.

9.3.
Matching the residues: Euler product evaluations

To be able to show that each of the residues on the right-hand side of (
9.2.72
) is equal to some term on the right-hand side of in (
9.1.32
), we will prove the following identity involving the Euler products
\mathcal{G}
and
\mathcal{K}
.

Lemma 9.7
.

Let
\alpha\in A
and
\beta\in B
. Suppose that
h
and
k
are positive integers. If
\mathcal{G}
is defined by (
9.2.38
) and
\mathcal{K}
by (
9.1.2
), then

(9.3.1)

h^{-\frac{1}{2}+\alpha}k^{-\frac{1}{2}+\beta}\mathcal{G}(2-\alpha-\beta,\alpha,\beta;A,B,h,k)=\mathcal{K}(0,0,2-\alpha-\beta;A,B,\alpha,\beta,h,k).

Our proof of Lemma
9.7
will depend on the following three lemmas. The first is a slight generalization of an identity due to Conrey and Keating
[
CK15b
]

Lemma 9.8
.

Let
\alpha\in A
and
\beta\in B
. Suppose that
j
and
\ell
are nonnegative integers and
p
is a prime. Then

\begin{split}\tau_{A\smallsetminus\{\alpha\}\cup\{-\beta\}}(p^{j})\tau_{B\smallsetminus\{\beta\}}(p^{\ell})+\tau_{A\smallsetminus\{\alpha\}}(p^{j})\tau_{B\smallsetminus\{\beta\}\cup\{-\alpha\}}(p^{\ell})-\tau_{A\smallsetminus\{\alpha\}}(p^{j})\tau_{B\smallsetminus\{\beta\}}(p^{\ell})\\
=\tau_{A\smallsetminus\{\alpha\}\cup\{-\beta\}}(p^{j})\tau_{B\smallsetminus\{\beta\}\cup\{-\alpha\}}(p^{\ell})-p^{\alpha+\beta}\tau_{A\smallsetminus\{\alpha\}\cup\{-\beta\}}(p^{j-1})\tau_{B\smallsetminus\{\beta\}\cup\{-\alpha\}}(p^{\ell-1}),\end{split}

where
\tau_{E}(p^{-1})
is defined to be zero for any multiset
E
.

Proof.

We argue as in
[
CK15b
]
. Observe that the definition (
3.1
) implies that if
m
is any nonnegative integer,
E
is any finite multiset, and
\gamma\in E
, then

(9.3.2)

\tau_{E}(p^{m})=\tau_{E\smallsetminus\{\gamma\}}(p^{m})+p^{-\gamma}\tau_{E}(p^{m-1}).

We apply this, multiply out the resulting products, and then cancel one
\tau_{A\smallsetminus\{\alpha\}}(p^{j})\tau_{B\smallsetminus\{\beta\}}(p^{\ell})
with its negative to deduce that

\begin{split}&\tau_{A\smallsetminus\{\alpha\}\cup\{-\beta\}}(p^{j})\tau_{B\smallsetminus\{\beta\}}(p^{\ell})+\tau_{A\smallsetminus\{\alpha\}}(p^{j})\tau_{B\smallsetminus\{\beta\}\cup\{-\alpha\}}(p^{\ell})-\tau_{A\smallsetminus\{\alpha\}}(p^{j})\tau_{B\smallsetminus\{\beta\}}(p^{\ell})\\
&=\Big(\tau_{A\smallsetminus\{\alpha\}}(p^{j})+p^{\beta}\tau_{A\smallsetminus\{\alpha\}\cup\{-\beta\}}(p^{j-1})\Big)\tau_{B\smallsetminus\{\beta\}}(p^{\ell})\\
&\hskip 36.135pt+\tau_{A\smallsetminus\{\alpha\}}(p^{j})\Big(\tau_{B\smallsetminus\{\beta\}}(p^{\ell})+p^{\alpha}\tau_{B\smallsetminus\{\beta\}\cup\{-\alpha\}}(p^{\ell-1})\Big)-\tau_{A\smallsetminus\{\alpha\}}(p^{j})\tau_{B\smallsetminus\{\beta\}}(p^{\ell})\\
&=\tau_{A\smallsetminus\{\alpha\}}(p^{j})\tau_{B\smallsetminus\{\beta\}}(p^{\ell})+p^{\beta}\tau_{A\smallsetminus\{\alpha\}\cup\{-\beta\}}(p^{j-1})\tau_{B\smallsetminus\{\beta\}}(p^{\ell})+p^{\alpha}\tau_{A\smallsetminus\{\alpha\}}(p^{j})\tau_{B\smallsetminus\{\beta\}\cup\{-\alpha\}}(p^{\ell-1}).\end{split}

We add and subtract
p^{\alpha+\beta}\tau_{A\smallsetminus\{\alpha\}\cup\{-\beta\}}(p^{j-1})\tau_{B\smallsetminus\{\beta\}\cup\{-\alpha\}}(p^{\ell-1})
, and then factor part of the resulting expression to arrive at

\begin{split}\tau_{A\smallsetminus\{\alpha\}\cup\{-\beta\}}(p^{j})\tau_{B\smallsetminus\{\beta\}}(p^{\ell})+\tau_{A\smallsetminus\{\alpha\}}(p^{j})\tau_{B\smallsetminus\{\beta\}\cup\{-\alpha\}}(p^{\ell})-\tau_{A\smallsetminus\{\alpha\}}(p^{j})\tau_{B\smallsetminus\{\beta\}}(p^{\ell})\\
=\Big(\tau_{A\smallsetminus\{\alpha\}}(p^{j})+p^{\beta}\tau_{A\smallsetminus\{\alpha\}\cup\{-\beta\}}(p^{j-1})\Big)\Big(\tau_{B\smallsetminus\{\beta\}}(p^{\ell})+p^{\alpha}\tau_{B\smallsetminus\{\beta\}\cup\{-\alpha\}}(p^{\ell-1})\Big)\\
-p^{\alpha+\beta}\tau_{A\smallsetminus\{\alpha\}\cup\{-\beta\}}(p^{j-1})\tau_{B\smallsetminus\{\beta\}\cup\{-\alpha\}}(p^{\ell-1}).\end{split}

The lemma now follows from this and (
9.3.2
).
∎

Lemma 9.9
.

Let
\alpha\in A
and
\beta\in B
. Suppose that
j
and
\ell
are nonnegative integers and
p
is a prime. Then

(9.3.3)

\begin{split}\tau_{A\smallsetminus\{\alpha\}\cup\{-\beta\}}(p^{j})\tau_{B\smallsetminus\{\beta\}}(p^{\ell})&=(1-p^{-\alpha-\beta})\tau_{A\cup\{-\beta\}}(p^{j})\tau_{B}(p^{\ell})+p^{-\alpha-\beta}\tau_{A}(p^{j})\tau_{B}(p^{\ell})\\
&-p^{-\beta}\tau_{A}(p^{j})\tau_{B}(p^{\ell-1})-(1-p^{-\alpha-\beta})\tau_{A\cup\{-\beta\}}(p^{j-1})\tau_{B}(p^{\ell-1})\end{split}

where
\tau_{E}(p^{-1})
is defined to be zero for any multiset
E
.

Proof.

We apply (
9.3.2
) and multiply out the resulting expression to deduce that

\begin{split}(1-p^{-\alpha-\beta})\tau_{A\cup\{-\beta\}}(p^{j})\tau_{B}(p^{\ell})&=(1-p^{-\alpha-\beta})\Big(\tau_{A}(p^{j})+p^{\beta}\tau_{A\cup\{-\beta\}}(p^{j-1})\Big)\tau_{B}(p^{\ell})\\
&=\tau_{A}(p^{j})\tau_{B}(p^{\ell})-p^{-\alpha-\beta}\tau_{A}(p^{j})\tau_{B}(p^{\ell})\\
&\hskip 36.135pt+(p^{\beta}-p^{-\alpha})\tau_{A\cup\{-\beta\}}(p^{j-1})\tau_{B}(p^{\ell})\end{split}

The term
-p^{-\alpha-\beta}\tau_{A}(p^{j})\tau_{B}(p^{\ell})
cancels with its negative on the left-hand side of (
9.3.3
), and it follows that

\begin{split}(1-p^{-\alpha-\beta})\tau_{A\cup\{-\beta\}}(p^{j})\tau_{B}(p^{\ell})&+p^{-\alpha-\beta}\tau_{A}(p^{j})\tau_{B}(p^{\ell})\\
&-p^{-\beta}\tau_{A}(p^{j})\tau_{B}(p^{\ell-1})-(1-p^{-\alpha-\beta})\tau_{A\cup\{-\beta\}}(p^{j-1})\tau_{B}(p^{\ell-1})\\
&\hskip-36.135pt=\tau_{A}(p^{j})\tau_{B}(p^{\ell})+(p^{\beta}-p^{-\alpha})\tau_{A\cup\{-\beta\}}(p^{j-1})\tau_{B}(p^{\ell})\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt-p^{-\beta}\tau_{A}(p^{j})\tau_{B}(p^{\ell-1})-(1-p^{-\alpha-\beta})\tau_{A\cup\{-\beta\}}(p^{j-1})\tau_{B}(p^{\ell-1}).\end{split}

The right-hand side factors as

\begin{split}\Big(\tau_{A}(p^{j})+(p^{\beta}-p^{-\alpha})\tau_{A\cup\{-\beta\}}(p^{j-1})\Big)\Big(\tau_{B}(p^{\ell})-p^{-\beta}\tau_{B}(p^{\ell-1})\Big),\end{split}

which, by (
9.3.2
), equals
\tau_{A\smallsetminus\{\alpha\}\cup\{-\beta\}}(p^{j})\tau_{B\smallsetminus\{\beta\}}(p^{\ell})
.
∎

Lemma 9.10
.

Let
\beta\in B
. Suppose that
j
and
\ell
are nonnegative integers and
p
is a prime. Then

p^{(\frac{1}{2}-\beta)(j-\ell)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m,n<\infty\\
m+j<n+\ell\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})}{p^{m\beta}p^{n(1-\beta)}}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m,n<\infty\\
m+j=n+\ell\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A\cup\{-\beta\}}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})-\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})}{p^{\frac{m}{2}+\frac{n}{2}}}

Proof.

The definition (
3.1
) of
\tau_{E}
implies that if
D
and
E
are finite multisets, then the Dirichlet convolution
\tau_{D}*\tau_{E}
of
\tau_{D}
and
\tau_{E}
is
\tau_{D\cup E}
. It follows from this and the definition of Dirichlet convolution that, for each nonnegative integer
m
,

\tau_{A\cup\{-\beta\}}(p^{m-1})=(\tau_{A}*\tau_{\{-\beta\}})(p^{m-1})=\sum_{\nu=0}^{m-1}\tau_{A}(p^{\nu})\tau_{\{-\beta\}}(p^{m-1-\nu})=\sum_{\nu=0}^{m-1}\tau_{A}(p^{\nu})p^{\beta(m-1-\nu)}.

This and the identity (
9.3.2
) imply

\tau_{A\cup\{-\beta\}}(p^{m})-\tau_{A}(p^{m})=p^{\beta}\tau_{A\cup\{-\beta\}}(p^{m-1})=\sum_{\nu=0}^{m-1}\tau_{A}(p^{\nu})p^{\beta(m-\nu)}.

Therefore

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m,n<\infty\\
m+j=n+\ell\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A\cup\{-\beta\}}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})-\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})}{p^{\frac{m}{2}+\frac{n}{2}}}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m,n<\infty\\
m+j=n+\ell\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{B}(p^{n})}{p^{\frac{m}{2}+\frac{n}{2}}}\sum_{\nu=0}^{m-1}\tau_{A}(p^{\nu})p^{\beta(m-\nu)}.

In the latter sum, we may replace
m
with
n+\ell-j
to write the sum as

\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{\tau_{B}(p^{n})}{p^{n+\frac{1}{2}(\ell-j)}}\sum_{\nu=0}^{n+\ell-j-1}\tau_{A}(p^{\nu})p^{\beta(n+\ell-j-\nu)}=p^{(\frac{1}{2}-\beta)(j-\ell)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq\nu,n<\infty\\
\nu+j<n+\ell\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{\nu})\tau_{B}(p^{n})}{p^{\nu\beta}p^{n(1-\beta)}}.

∎

Proof of Lemma
9.7
.

We may write each side of (
9.3.1
) as an Euler product by the definitions (
9.1.2
) of
\mathcal{K}
and (
9.2.38
) of
\mathcal{G}
. The Euler products converge absolutely by Lemmas
9.3
and
9.6
. To prove Lemma
9.7
, it suffices to show for each
p
that the local factors corresponding to
p
in these Euler products agree.

We first examine the local factors corresponding to a given prime
p\nmid hk
. For brevity, let
\mathfrak{F}_{p}
denote the local factor corresponding to this
p
in the Euler product expression for the left-hand side of (
9.3.1
). Thus, from the definition (
9.2.38
) of
\mathcal{G}
, we see that
\mathfrak{F}_{p}
is defined by

(9.3.4)

\begin{split}\mathfrak{F}_{p}&:=\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha\\
\hat{\beta}\neq\beta\end{subarray}}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+\hat{\alpha}+\hat{\beta}}}\right)\prod_{\hat{\alpha}\in A}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+\hat{\alpha}-\alpha}}\right)\prod_{\hat{\beta}\in B}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+\hat{\beta}-\beta}}\right)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\Bigg(\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{2-\alpha-\beta}}\right)\left(1+\frac{p^{1-\alpha-\beta}-1}{p(p-1)}\right)+\left(1+\frac{p^{-\alpha-\beta}}{p-1}-\frac{1}{p-1}\right)\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{m})}{p^{m}}\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt+(1-p^{-\alpha-\beta})\sum_{0\leq m<n<\infty}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})}{p^{m\beta}p^{n(1-\beta)}}+(1-p^{-\alpha-\beta})\sum_{0\leq n<m<\infty}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})}{p^{m(1-\alpha)}p^{n\alpha}}\Bigg).\end{split}

Lemma
9.10
with
j=\ell=0
implies

(9.3.5)

\sum_{0\leq m<n<\infty}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})}{p^{m\beta}p^{n(1-\beta)}}=\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\frac{\tau_{A\cup\{-\beta\}}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{m})}{p^{m}}-\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{m})}{p^{m}}.

Similarly, Lemma
9.10
with
A
and
B
interchanged,
\beta
replaced by
\alpha
, and
j=\ell=0
implies

(9.3.6)

\sum_{0\leq n<m<\infty}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})}{p^{m(1-\alpha)}p^{n\alpha}}=\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B\cup\{-\alpha\}}(p^{m})}{p^{m}}-\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{m})}{p^{m}}.

We complete the first
m
-sum in (
9.3.4
) by adding and subtracting its
m=0
term, and then insert (
9.3.5
) and (
9.3.6
) to deduce that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{F}_{p}

\displaystyle=\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha\\
\hat{\beta}\neq\beta\end{subarray}}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+\hat{\alpha}+\hat{\beta}}}\right)\prod_{\hat{\alpha}\in A}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+\hat{\alpha}-\alpha}}\right)\prod_{\hat{\beta}\in B}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+\hat{\beta}-\beta}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.06749pt\times\Bigg(\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{2-\alpha-\beta}}\right)\left(1+\frac{p^{1-\alpha-\beta}-1}{p(p-1)}\right)-\left(1+\frac{p^{-\alpha-\beta}}{p-1}-\frac{1}{p-1}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 36.135pt+\left(2p^{-\alpha-\beta}-1+\frac{p^{-\alpha-\beta}}{p-1}-\frac{1}{p-1}\right)\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{m})}{p^{m}}

(9.3.7)

\displaystyle\hskip 36.135pt+(1-p^{-\alpha-\beta})\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\frac{\tau_{A\cup\{-\beta\}}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{m})}{p^{m}}+(1-p^{-\alpha-\beta})\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B\cup\{-\alpha\}}(p^{m})}{p^{m}}\Bigg).

Observe that there is the factor
(1-1/p)^{2}
in (
9.3.7
). This factor is the product of the factor corresponding to
\hat{\alpha}=\alpha
in the product over
\hat{\alpha}\in A
and the factor corresponding to
\hat{\beta}=\beta
in the product over
\hat{\beta}\in B
. We distribute
(1-1/p)
among the terms in (
9.3.7
) and arrive at

(9.3.8)

\begin{split}\mathfrak{F}_{p}&=\left(1-\frac{1}{p}\right)\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha\\
\hat{\beta}\neq\beta\end{subarray}}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+\hat{\alpha}+\hat{\beta}}}\right)\prod_{\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+\hat{\alpha}-\alpha}}\right)\prod_{\hat{\beta}\neq\beta}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+\hat{\beta}-\beta}}\right)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\Bigg(\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{2-\alpha-\beta}}\right)\left(1-\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{1+\alpha+\beta}}-\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)-\left(1-\frac{2}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{1+\alpha+\beta}}\right)+\Sigma_{0}\Bigg),\end{split}

where
\Sigma_{0}
is defined by

\begin{split}\Sigma_{0}:=&\left(2p^{-\alpha-\beta}-1-\frac{p^{-\alpha-\beta}}{p}\right)\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{m})}{p^{m}}\\
&+\bigg(1-p^{-\alpha-\beta}-\frac{1}{p}+\frac{p^{-\alpha-\beta}}{p}\bigg)\Bigg(\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\frac{\tau_{A\cup\{-\beta\}}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{m})}{p^{m}}+\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B\cup\{-\alpha\}}(p^{m})}{p^{m}}\Bigg).\end{split}

Multiply out the products in the latter expression and rearrange the terms to write

\begin{split}\Sigma_{0}&=\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\frac{(1-p^{-\alpha-\beta})\tau_{A\cup\{-\beta\}}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{m})+p^{-\alpha-\beta}\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{m})}{p^{m}}\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt+\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\frac{(1-p^{-\alpha-\beta})\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B\cup\{-\alpha\}}(p^{m})+p^{-\alpha-\beta}\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{m})-\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{m})}{p^{m}}\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt-\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\frac{(1-p^{-\alpha-\beta})\big(\tau_{A\cup\{-\beta\}}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{m})+\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B\cup\{-\alpha\}}(p^{m})\big)+p^{-\alpha-\beta}\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{m})}{p^{m+1}}.\end{split}

We make a change of variables in the last
m
-sum on the right-hand side by replacing each instance of
m
with
m-1
. To the resulting expression for
\Sigma_{0}
, we add

\begin{split}0&=\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\frac{p^{-\alpha}\tau_{A}(p^{m-1})\tau_{B}(p^{m})+p^{-\beta}\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{m-1})}{p^{m}}\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt-\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\frac{p^{-\alpha}\tau_{A}(p^{m-1})\tau_{B}(p^{m})+p^{-\beta}\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{m-1})}{p^{m}}\end{split}

and rearrange the terms to deduce that

(9.3.9)

\Sigma_{0}=\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\Big(D_{1,m}+D_{2,m}+D_{3,m}\Big)\frac{1}{p^{m}},

where
D_{1,m}
,
D_{2,m}
, and
D_{3,m}
are defined by

\begin{split}D_{1,m}&:=(1-p^{-\alpha-\beta})\tau_{A\cup\{-\beta\}}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{m})+p^{-\alpha-\beta}\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{m})\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt-p^{-\beta}\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{m-1})-(1-p^{-\alpha-\beta})\tau_{A\cup\{-\beta\}}(p^{m-1})\tau_{B}(p^{m-1}),\end{split}

\begin{split}D_{2,m}&:=(1-p^{-\alpha-\beta})\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B\cup\{-\alpha\}}(p^{m})+p^{-\alpha-\beta}\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{m})\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt-p^{-\alpha}\tau_{A}(p^{m-1})\tau_{B}(p^{m})-(1-p^{-\alpha-\beta})\tau_{A}(p^{m-1})\tau_{B\cup\{-\alpha\}}(p^{m-1}),\end{split}

and

\begin{split}D_{3,m}&:=-\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{m})+p^{-\alpha}\tau_{A}(p^{m-1})\tau_{B}(p^{m})\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt+p^{-\beta}\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{m-1})-p^{-\alpha-\beta}\tau_{A}(p^{m-1})\tau_{B}(p^{m-1}),\end{split}

where we recall that
\tau_{E}(p^{-1})
is defined to be zero for any multiset
E
. Now Lemma
9.9
with
j=\ell=m
implies

(9.3.10)

D_{1,m}=\tau_{A\smallsetminus\{\alpha\}\cup\{-\beta\}}(p^{m})\tau_{B\smallsetminus\{\beta\}}(p^{m}).

Moreover, Lemma
9.9
with
A
and
B
interchanged and
j=\ell=m
implies

(9.3.11)

D_{2,m}=\tau_{A\smallsetminus\{\alpha\}}(p^{m})\tau_{B\smallsetminus\{\beta\}\cup\{-\alpha\}}(p^{m}).

As for
D_{3,m}
, we may factor it and apply (
9.3.2
) to deduce that

\begin{split}D_{3,m}&=-\big(\tau_{A}(p^{m})-p^{-\alpha}\tau_{A}(p^{m-1})\big)\big(\tau_{B}(p^{m})-p^{-\beta}\tau_{B}(p^{m-1})\big)\\
&=-\tau_{A\smallsetminus\{\alpha\}}(p^{m})\tau_{B\smallsetminus\{\beta\}}(p^{m}).\end{split}

From this, (
9.3.10
), (
9.3.11
), and Lemma
9.8
with
j=\ell=m
, we arrive at

\begin{split}D_{1,m}&+D_{2,m}+D_{3,m}\\
&=\tau_{A\smallsetminus\{\alpha\}\cup\{-\beta\}}(p^{m})\tau_{B\smallsetminus\{\beta\}\cup\{-\alpha\}}(p^{m})-p^{\alpha+\beta}\tau_{A\smallsetminus\{\alpha\}\cup\{-\beta\}}(p^{m-1})\tau_{B\smallsetminus\{\beta\}\cup\{-\alpha\}}(p^{m-1}).\end{split}

This and (
9.3.9
) imply

\begin{split}\Sigma_{0}&=\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\frac{\tau_{A\smallsetminus\{\alpha\}\cup\{-\beta\}}(p^{m})\tau_{B\smallsetminus\{\beta\}\cup\{-\alpha\}}(p^{m})}{p^{m}}\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt-p^{\alpha+\beta}\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\frac{\tau_{A\smallsetminus\{\alpha\}\cup\{-\beta\}}(p^{m-1})\tau_{B\smallsetminus\{\beta\}\cup\{-\alpha\}}(p^{m-1})}{p^{m}}.\end{split}

We make a change of variables in the latter
m
-sum by replacing each instance of
m
with
m+1
. The result is

\begin{split}\Sigma_{0}=\bigg(1-\frac{1}{p^{1-\alpha-\beta}}\bigg)\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\frac{\tau_{A\smallsetminus\{\alpha\}\cup\{-\beta\}}(p^{m})\tau_{B\smallsetminus\{\beta\}\cup\{-\alpha\}}(p^{m})}{p^{m}}.\end{split}

We insert this into (
9.3.8
) and arrive at

(9.3.12)

\begin{split}\mathfrak{F}_{p}&=\left(1-\frac{1}{p}\right)\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha\\
\hat{\beta}\neq\beta\end{subarray}}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+\hat{\alpha}+\hat{\beta}}}\right)\prod_{\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+\hat{\alpha}-\alpha}}\right)\prod_{\hat{\beta}\neq\beta}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+\hat{\beta}-\beta}}\right)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\Bigg(\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{2-\alpha-\beta}}\right)\left(1-\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{1+\alpha+\beta}}-\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)-\left(1-\frac{2}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{1+\alpha+\beta}}\right)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt+\bigg(1-\frac{1}{p^{1-\alpha-\beta}}\bigg)+\bigg(1-\frac{1}{p^{1-\alpha-\beta}}\bigg)\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\frac{\tau_{A\smallsetminus\{\alpha\}\cup\{-\beta\}}(p^{m})\tau_{B\smallsetminus\{\beta\}\cup\{-\alpha\}}(p^{m})}{p^{m}}\Bigg),\end{split}

where we have separated the
m=0
term from the
m
-sum. A direct calculation gives

\begin{split}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{2-\alpha-\beta}}\right)\left(1-\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{1+\alpha+\beta}}-\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)-\left(1-\frac{2}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{1+\alpha+\beta}}\right)+\bigg(1-\frac{1}{p^{1-\alpha-\beta}}\bigg)\\
=\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1-\alpha-\beta}}\right)\left(1+\frac{1}{p}\right)\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right).\end{split}

We insert this into (
9.3.12
) and then factor out
(1-p^{-1+\alpha+\beta})
to deduce that

\begin{split}\mathfrak{F}_{p}=\left(1-\frac{1}{p}\right)\bigg(1-\frac{1}{p^{1-\alpha-\beta}}\bigg)\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha\\
\hat{\beta}\neq\beta\end{subarray}}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+\hat{\alpha}+\hat{\beta}}}\right)\prod_{\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+\hat{\alpha}-\alpha}}\right)\prod_{\hat{\beta}\neq\beta}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+\hat{\beta}-\beta}}\right)\\
\times\Bigg(\left(1+\frac{1}{p}\right)\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)+\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\frac{\tau_{A\smallsetminus\{\alpha\}\cup\{-\beta\}}(p^{m})\tau_{B\smallsetminus\{\beta\}\cup\{-\alpha\}}(p^{m})}{p^{m}}\Bigg).\end{split}

The right-hand side is exactly the local factor corresponding to
p
in the Euler product expression for
\mathcal{K}(0,0,2-\alpha-\beta)
by the definition (
9.1.2
), because we are assuming that
p\nmid hk
.

We have now shown for each
p\nmid hk
that the local factors corresponding to
p
in the Euler product expressions of both sides of (
9.3.1
) agree. Our next task is to do the same for each
p|hk
. To this end, let
p|hk
be given, and let
\mathfrak{G}_{p}
denote the local factor corresponding to this
p
in the Euler product expression for the left-hand side of (
9.3.1
). Also, for brevity, for the rest of this proof we denote
h_{p}:=\text{ord}_{p}(h)
and
k_{p}:=\text{ord}_{p}(k)
. With these notations, we see from the definition (
9.2.38
) of
\mathcal{G}
that
\mathfrak{G}_{p}
is defined by

(9.3.13)

\begin{split}\mathfrak{G}_{p}:=p^{-(\frac{1}{2}-\alpha)h_{p}-(\frac{1}{2}-\beta)k_{p}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha\\
\hat{\beta}\neq\beta\end{subarray}}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+\hat{\alpha}+\hat{\beta}}}\right)\prod_{\hat{\alpha}\in A}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+\hat{\alpha}-\alpha}}\right)\prod_{\hat{\beta}\in B}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+\hat{\beta}-\beta}}\right)\\
\times\Bigg(\left(1+\frac{p^{-\alpha-\beta}}{p-1}-\frac{1}{p-1}\right)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m,n<\infty\\
m+h_{p}=n+k_{p}\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})}{p^{m(1-\alpha)}p^{n(1-\beta)}p^{(-1+\alpha+\beta)\min\{m+h_{p},n+k_{p}\}}}\\
+\left(1-p^{-\alpha-\beta}\right)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m,n<\infty\\
m+h_{p}\neq n+k_{p}\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})}{p^{m(1-\alpha)}p^{n(1-\beta)}p^{(-1+\alpha+\beta)\min\{m+h_{p},n+k_{p}\}}}\Bigg).\end{split}

If
m+h_{p}=n+k_{p}
, then

(-1+\alpha+\beta)\min\{m+h_{p},n+k_{p}\}=\left(-\frac{1}{2}+\alpha\right)(m+h_{p})+\left(-\frac{1}{2}+\beta\right)(n+k_{p}),

and so

(9.3.14)

\begin{split}p^{-(\frac{1}{2}-\alpha)h_{p}-(\frac{1}{2}-\beta)k_{p}}&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m,n<\infty\\
m+h_{p}=n+k_{p}\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})}{p^{m(1-\alpha)}p^{n(1-\beta)}p^{(-1+\alpha+\beta)\min\{m+h_{p},n+k_{p}\}}}\\
&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m,n<\infty\\
m+h_{p}=n+k_{p}\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})}{p^{\frac{m}{2}+\frac{n}{2}}}.\end{split}

If
m+h_{p}<n+k_{p}
, then
\min\{m+h_{p},n+k_{p}\}=m+h_{p}
and it follows from Lemma
9.10
with
j=h_{p}
and
\ell=k_{p}
that

(9.3.15)

\begin{split}p^{-(\frac{1}{2}-\alpha)h_{p}-(\frac{1}{2}-\beta)k_{p}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m,n<\infty\\
m+h_{p}<n+k_{p}\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})}{p^{m(1-\alpha)}p^{n(1-\beta)}p^{(-1+\alpha+\beta)\min\{m+h_{p},n+k_{p}\}}}\\
=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m,n<\infty\\
m+h_{p}=n+k_{p}\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A\cup\{-\beta\}}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})-\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})}{p^{\frac{m}{2}+\frac{n}{2}}}.\end{split}

Similarly, Lemma
9.10
with
A
and
B
interchanged,
\beta
replaced by
\alpha
,
j=k_{p}
, and
\ell=h_{p}
implies

\begin{split}p^{-(\frac{1}{2}-\alpha)h_{p}-(\frac{1}{2}-\beta)k_{p}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m,n<\infty\\
m+h_{p}>n+k_{p}\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})}{p^{m(1-\alpha)}p^{n(1-\beta)}p^{(-1+\alpha+\beta)\min\{m+h_{p},n+k_{p}\}}}\\
=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m,n<\infty\\
m+h_{p}=n+k_{p}\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B\cup\{-\alpha\}}(p^{n})-\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})}{p^{\frac{m}{2}+\frac{n}{2}}}.\end{split}

It follows from this, (
9.3.13
), (
9.3.14
), and (
9.3.15
) that

(9.3.16)

\begin{split}\mathfrak{G}_{p}&=\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha\\
\hat{\beta}\neq\beta\end{subarray}}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+\hat{\alpha}+\hat{\beta}}}\right)\prod_{\hat{\alpha}\in A}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+\hat{\alpha}-\alpha}}\right)\prod_{\hat{\beta}\in B}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+\hat{\beta}-\beta}}\right)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\Bigg(\left(2p^{-\alpha-\beta}-1+\frac{p^{-\alpha-\beta}}{p-1}-\frac{1}{p-1}\right)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m,n<\infty\\
m+h_{p}=n+k_{p}\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})}{p^{\frac{m}{2}+\frac{n}{2}}}\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt+\left(1-p^{-\alpha-\beta}\right)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m,n<\infty\\
m+h_{p}=n+k_{p}\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A\cup\{-\beta\}}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})+\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B\cup\{-\alpha\}}(p^{n})}{p^{\frac{m}{2}+\frac{n}{2}}}\Bigg).\end{split}

There is the factor
(1-1/p)^{2}
in (
9.3.16
) by the same reason mentioned below (
9.3.7
). We distribute
(1-1/p)
among the terms in (
9.3.16
) and deduce that

(9.3.17)

\mathfrak{G}_{p}=\Sigma_{1}\times\left(1-\frac{1}{p}\right)\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha\\
\hat{\beta}\neq\beta\end{subarray}}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+\hat{\alpha}+\hat{\beta}}}\right)\prod_{\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+\hat{\alpha}-\alpha}}\right)\prod_{\hat{\beta}\neq\beta}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+\hat{\beta}-\beta}}\right),

where
\Sigma_{1}
is defined by

\begin{split}\Sigma_{1}&:=\left(2p^{-\alpha-\beta}-1-\frac{p^{-\alpha-\beta}}{p}\right)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m,n<\infty\\
m+h_{p}=n+k_{p}\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})}{p^{\frac{m}{2}+\frac{n}{2}}}\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt+\left(1-p^{-\alpha-\beta}-\frac{1}{p}+\frac{p^{-\alpha-\beta}}{p}\right)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m,n<\infty\\
m+h_{p}=n+k_{p}\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A\cup\{-\beta\}}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})+\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B\cup\{-\alpha\}}(p^{n})}{p^{\frac{m}{2}+\frac{n}{2}}}.\end{split}

Multiply out the products and rearrange the terms to write
\Sigma_{1}
as

\begin{split}&\Sigma_{1}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m,n<\infty\\
m+h_{p}=n+k_{p}\end{subarray}}\frac{(1-p^{-\alpha-\beta})\tau_{A\cup\{-\beta\}}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})+p^{-\alpha-\beta}\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})}{p^{\frac{m}{2}+\frac{n}{2}}}\\
&+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m,n<\infty\\
m+h_{p}=n+k_{p}\end{subarray}}\frac{(1-p^{-\alpha-\beta})\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B\cup\{-\alpha\}}(p^{n})+p^{-\alpha-\beta}\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})-\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})}{p^{\frac{m}{2}+\frac{n}{2}}}\\
&-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m,n<\infty\\
m+h_{p}=n+k_{p}\end{subarray}}\frac{(1-p^{-\alpha-\beta})\big(\tau_{A\cup\{-\beta\}}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})+\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B\cup\{-\alpha\}}(p^{n})\big)+p^{-\alpha-\beta}\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})}{p^{1+\frac{m}{2}+\frac{n}{2}}}.\end{split}

We make changes of variables in the last
m,n
-sum on the right-hand side by replacing each instance of
m
with
m-1
and each instance of
n
with
n-1
. To the resulting expression for
\Sigma_{1}
, we add

\begin{split}0&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m,n<\infty\\
m+h_{p}=n+k_{p}\end{subarray}}\frac{p^{-\alpha}\tau_{A}(p^{m-1})\tau_{B}(p^{n})+p^{-\beta}\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n-1})}{p^{\frac{m}{2}+\frac{n}{2}}}\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m,n<\infty\\
