m+h_{p}=n+k_{p}\end{subarray}}\frac{p^{-\alpha}\tau_{A}(p^{m-1})\tau_{B}(p^{n})+p^{-\beta}\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n-1})}{p^{\frac{m}{2}+\frac{n}{2}}}\end{split}

and rearrange the terms to deduce that

(9.3.18)

\Sigma_{1}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m,n<\infty\\
m+h_{p}=n+k_{p}\end{subarray}}\Big(D_{1,m,n}+D_{2,m,n}+D_{3,m,n}\Big)\frac{1}{p^{\frac{m}{2}+\frac{n}{2}}},

where
D_{1,m,n}
,
D_{2,m,n}
, and
D_{3,m,n}
are defined by

\begin{split}D_{1,m,n}&:=(1-p^{-\alpha-\beta})\tau_{A\cup\{-\beta\}}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})+p^{-\alpha-\beta}\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt-p^{-\beta}\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n-1})-(1-p^{-\alpha-\beta})\tau_{A\cup\{-\beta\}}(p^{m-1})\tau_{B}(p^{n-1}),\end{split}

\begin{split}D_{2,m,n}&:=(1-p^{-\alpha-\beta})\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B\cup\{-\alpha\}}(p^{n})+p^{-\alpha-\beta}\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt-p^{-\alpha}\tau_{A}(p^{m-1})\tau_{B}(p^{n})-(1-p^{-\alpha-\beta})\tau_{A}(p^{m-1})\tau_{B\cup\{-\alpha\}}(p^{n-1}),\end{split}

and

\begin{split}D_{3,m,n}&:=-\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})+p^{-\alpha}\tau_{A}(p^{m-1})\tau_{B}(p^{n})\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt+p^{-\beta}\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n-1})-p^{-\alpha-\beta}\tau_{A}(p^{m-1})\tau_{B}(p^{n-1}),\end{split}

where we recall that
\tau_{E}(p^{-1})
is defined to be zero for any multiset
E
. Now Lemma
9.9
with
j=m
and
\ell=n
implies

(9.3.19)

D_{1,m,n}=\tau_{A\smallsetminus\{\alpha\}\cup\{-\beta\}}(p^{m})\tau_{B\smallsetminus\{\beta\}}(p^{n}).

Moreover, Lemma
9.9
with
A
and
B
interchanged,
j=n
, and
\ell=m
implies

(9.3.20)

D_{2,m,n}=\tau_{A\smallsetminus\{\alpha\}}(p^{m})\tau_{B\smallsetminus\{\beta\}\cup\{-\alpha\}}(p^{n}).

As for
D_{3,m,n}
, we may factor it and apply (
9.3.2
) to deduce that

\begin{split}D_{3,m,n}&=-\big(\tau_{A}(p^{m})-p^{-\alpha}\tau_{A}(p^{m-1})\big)\big(\tau_{B}(p^{n})-p^{-\beta}\tau_{B}(p^{n-1})\big)\\
&=-\tau_{A\smallsetminus\{\alpha\}}(p^{m})\tau_{B\smallsetminus\{\beta\}}(p^{n}).\end{split}

From this, (
9.3.19
), (
9.3.20
), and Lemma
9.8
with
j=m
and
\ell=n
, we arrive at

\begin{split}D_{1,m,n}&+D_{2,m,n}+D_{3,m,n}\\
&=\tau_{A\smallsetminus\{\alpha\}\cup\{-\beta\}}(p^{m})\tau_{B\smallsetminus\{\beta\}\cup\{-\alpha\}}(p^{n})-p^{\alpha+\beta}\tau_{A\smallsetminus\{\alpha\}\cup\{-\beta\}}(p^{m-1})\tau_{B\smallsetminus\{\beta\}\cup\{-\alpha\}}(p^{n-1}).\end{split}

This and (
9.3.18
) imply

\begin{split}\Sigma_{1}&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m,n<\infty\\
m+h_{p}=n+k_{p}\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A\smallsetminus\{\alpha\}\cup\{-\beta\}}(p^{m})\tau_{B\smallsetminus\{\beta\}\cup\{-\alpha\}}(p^{n})}{p^{\frac{m}{2}+\frac{n}{2}}}\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt-p^{\alpha+\beta}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m,n<\infty\\
m+h_{p}=n+k_{p}\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A\smallsetminus\{\alpha\}\cup\{-\beta\}}(p^{m-1})\tau_{B\smallsetminus\{\beta\}\cup\{-\alpha\}}(p^{n-1})}{p^{\frac{m}{2}+\frac{n}{2}}}.\end{split}

We make a change of variables in the latter
m,n
-sum by replacing each instance of
m
with
m+1
and each instance of
n
with
n+1
. The result is

\Sigma_{1}=\bigg(1-\frac{1}{p^{1-\alpha-\beta}}\bigg)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m,n<\infty\\
m+h_{p}=n+k_{p}\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A\smallsetminus\{\alpha\}\cup\{-\beta\}}(p^{m})\tau_{B\smallsetminus\{\beta\}\cup\{-\alpha\}}(p^{n})}{p^{\frac{m}{2}+\frac{n}{2}}}.

We insert this into (
9.3.17
) and arrive at

\begin{split}\mathfrak{G}_{p}&=\left(1-\frac{1}{p}\right)\bigg(1-\frac{1}{p^{1-\alpha-\beta}}\bigg)\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha\\
\hat{\beta}\neq\beta\end{subarray}}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+\hat{\alpha}+\hat{\beta}}}\right)\prod_{\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+\hat{\alpha}-\alpha}}\right)\prod_{\hat{\beta}\neq\beta}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+\hat{\beta}-\beta}}\right)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m,n<\infty\\
m+h_{p}=n+k_{p}\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A\smallsetminus\{\alpha\}\cup\{-\beta\}}(p^{m})\tau_{B\smallsetminus\{\beta\}\cup\{-\alpha\}}(p^{n})}{p^{\frac{m}{2}+\frac{n}{2}}}.\end{split}

The right-hand side is exactly the local factor corresponding to
p
in the Euler product expression for
\mathcal{K}(0,0,2-\alpha-\beta)
by the definition (
9.1.2
), because we are assuming that
p|hk
.

We have now shown for each
p
that the local factors corresponding to
p
in the Euler product expressions of both sides of (
9.3.1
) agree. This completes the proof of Lemma
9.7
.
∎

We will also use the following variant and consequence of Lemma
9.7
.

Lemma 9.11
.

Let
\alpha,\alpha^{*}\in A
and
\beta,\beta^{*}\in B
. Suppose that
h
and
k
are positive integers. If
\mathcal{G}
is defined by (
9.2.38
) and
\mathcal{K}
by (
9.1.2
), then

\begin{split}h^{-\frac{1}{2}+\alpha}k^{-\frac{1}{2}+\beta-\alpha^{*}-\beta^{*}}&\mathcal{G}(2-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{*}+\beta^{*},\alpha,\beta;A,B,h,k)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt=\mathcal{K}(0,-\alpha^{*}-\beta^{*},2-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{*}+\beta^{*};A,B,\alpha,\beta,h,k).\end{split}

Proof.

The definition (
9.2.38
) implies

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}(2

\displaystyle-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{*}+\beta^{*},\alpha,\beta;A,B,h,k)

\displaystyle=\prod_{p|hk}\Bigg\{\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha\\
\hat{\beta}\neq\beta\end{subarray}}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+\hat{\alpha}+\hat{\beta}-\alpha^{*}-\beta^{*}}}\right)\prod_{\hat{\alpha}\in A}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+\hat{\alpha}-\alpha}}\right)\prod_{\hat{\beta}\in B}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+\hat{\beta}-\beta}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.06749pt\times\Bigg(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m,n<\infty\\
m+\text{ord}_{p}(h)=n+\text{ord}_{p}(k)\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})\left(1+\frac{p^{-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{*}+\beta^{*}}}{p-1}-\frac{1}{p-1}\right)}{p^{m(1-\alpha)}p^{n(1-\beta)}p^{(-1+\alpha+\beta-\alpha^{*}-\beta^{*})\min\{m+\text{ord}_{p}(h),n+\text{ord}_{p}(k)\}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.06749pt+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m,n<\infty\\
m+\text{ord}_{p}(h)\neq n+\text{ord}_{p}(k)\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})\big(1-p^{-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{*}+\beta^{*}}\big)}{p^{m(1-\alpha)}p^{n(1-\beta)}p^{(-1+\alpha+\beta-\alpha^{*}-\beta^{*})\min\{m+\text{ord}_{p}(h),n+\text{ord}_{p}(k)\}}}\Bigg)\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{p\nmid hk}\Bigg\{\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha\\
\hat{\beta}\neq\beta\end{subarray}}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+\hat{\alpha}+\hat{\beta}-\alpha^{*}-\beta^{*}}}\right)\prod_{\hat{\alpha}\in A}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+\hat{\alpha}-\alpha}}\right)\prod_{\hat{\beta}\in B}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+\hat{\beta}-\beta}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.06749pt\times\Bigg(\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{2-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{*}+\beta^{*}}}\right)\left(1+\frac{p^{1-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{*}+\beta^{*}}-1}{(p-1)}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 36.135pt+\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{m})\left(1+\frac{p^{-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{*}+\beta^{*}}}{p-1}-\frac{1}{p-1}\right)}{p^{m(1-\alpha^{*}-\beta^{*})}}

\displaystyle\hskip 36.135pt+\sum_{0\leq m<n<\infty}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})\big(1-p^{-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{*}+\beta^{*}}\big)}{p^{m(\beta-\alpha^{*}-\beta^{*})}p^{n(1-\beta)}}

(9.3.21)

\displaystyle\hskip 36.135pt+\sum_{0\leq n<m<\infty}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})\big(1-p^{-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{*}+\beta^{*}}\big)}{p^{m(1-\alpha)}p^{n(\alpha-\alpha^{*}-\beta^{*})}}\Bigg)\Bigg\},

with the product absolutely convergent by Lemma
9.6
. Now

\prod_{\hat{\beta}\in B}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+\hat{\beta}-\beta}}\right)=\prod_{\gamma\in B_{-\alpha^{*}-\beta^{*}}}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+\gamma-\beta+\alpha^{*}+\beta^{*}}}\right),

while (
3.3
) implies

\frac{\tau_{B}(p^{n})}{p^{n(1-\beta)}}=\frac{\tau_{B_{-\alpha^{*}-\beta^{*}}}(p^{n})}{p^{n(1-\beta+\alpha^{*}+\beta^{*})}}

and

\frac{\tau_{B}(p^{m})}{p^{m(1-\alpha^{*}-\beta^{*})}}=\frac{\tau_{B_{-\alpha^{*}-\beta^{*}}}(p^{m})}{p^{m}}.

It follows from these, (
9.3.21
), and the definition (
9.2.38
) of
\mathcal{G}
that

(9.3.22)

\begin{split}\mathcal{G}&(2-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{*}+\beta^{*},\alpha,\beta;A,B,h,k)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt=\mathcal{G}(2-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{*}+\beta^{*},\alpha,\beta-\alpha^{*}-\beta^{*};A,B_{-\alpha^{*}-\beta^{*}},h,k).\end{split}

Lemma
9.7
with
B
replaced by
B_{-\alpha^{*}-\beta^{*}}
and
\beta
replaced by
\beta-\alpha^{*}-\beta^{*}
implies

(9.3.23)

\begin{split}h^{-\frac{1}{2}+\alpha}k^{-\frac{1}{2}+\beta-\alpha^{*}-\beta^{*}}\mathcal{G}(2-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{*}+\beta^{*},\alpha,\beta-\alpha^{*}-\beta^{*};A,B_{-\alpha^{*}-\beta^{*}},h,k)\\
=\mathcal{K}(0,0,2-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{*}+\beta^{*};A,B_{-\alpha^{*}-\beta^{*}},\alpha,\beta-\alpha^{*}-\beta^{*},h,k).\end{split}

To see that the right-hand side is the same as

\mathcal{K}(0,-\alpha^{*}-\beta^{*},2-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{*}+\beta^{*};A,B,\alpha,\beta,h,k),

we make the following observations. If
w=2-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{*}+\beta^{*}
and
s_{1}=s_{2}=0
, then

w-1+\alpha+s_{1}+(\beta-\alpha^{*}-\beta^{*})+s_{2}=1,

A_{s_{1}}\smallsetminus\{\alpha+s_{1}\}\cup\{-\beta+\alpha^{*}+\beta^{*}-s_{2}\}=A\smallsetminus\{\alpha\}\cup\{-\beta+\alpha^{*}+\beta^{*}\},

and

\big(B_{-\alpha^{*}-\beta^{*}}\big)_{s_{2}}\smallsetminus\{\beta-\alpha^{*}-\beta^{*}+s_{2}\}\cup\{-\alpha-s_{1}\}=B_{-\alpha^{*}-\beta^{*}}\smallsetminus\{\beta-\alpha^{*}-\beta^{*}\}\cup\{-\alpha\}.

On the other hand, if
w=2-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{*}+\beta^{*}
,
s_{1}=0
, and
s_{2}=-\alpha^{*}-\beta^{*}
, then

w-1+\alpha+s_{1}+\beta+s_{2}=1,

A_{s_{1}}\smallsetminus\{\alpha+s_{1}\}\cup\{-\beta-s_{2}\}=A\smallsetminus\{\alpha\}\cup\{-\beta+\alpha^{*}+\beta^{*}\},

and

B_{s_{2}}\smallsetminus\{\beta+s_{2}\}\cup\{-\alpha-s_{1}\}=B_{-\alpha^{*}-\beta^{*}}\smallsetminus\{\beta-\alpha^{*}-\beta^{*}\}\cup\{-\alpha\}.

These observations and the definition (
9.1.2
) of
\mathcal{K}
imply that

\begin{split}\mathcal{K}(0,0,2-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{*}+\beta^{*};A,B_{-\alpha^{*}-\beta^{*}},\alpha,\beta-\alpha^{*}-\beta^{*},h,k)\\
=\mathcal{K}(0,-\alpha^{*}-\beta^{*},2-\alpha-\beta+\alpha^{*}+\beta^{*};A,B,\alpha,\beta,h,k).\end{split}

From this, (
9.3.22
), and (
9.3.23
), we arrive at Lemma
9.11
.
∎

The special case of Lemma
9.11
with
\alpha^{*}=\alpha
and
\beta^{*}=\beta
implies that

(9.3.24)

\begin{split}h^{-\frac{1}{2}+\alpha}k^{-\frac{1}{2}-\alpha}\mathcal{G}(2,\alpha,\beta)=\mathcal{K}(0,-\alpha-\beta,2).\end{split}

As a side note, we mention that (
9.3.24
) may be proved directly from the definitions (
9.2.38
) of
\mathcal{G}
and (
9.1.2
) of
\mathcal{K}
by using the identity

\frac{\tau_{B}(p^{n})}{p^{n(-\alpha-\beta)}}=\tau_{B_{-\alpha-\beta}}(p^{n}),

which follows from (
3.3
), and observing that if
m+\text{ord}_{p}(h)=n+\text{ord}_{p}(k)
then

\begin{split}p^{m(1-\alpha)}p^{n(1-\beta)}p^{-\min\{m+\text{ord}_{p}(h),n+\text{ord}_{p}(k)\}}&=p^{m(1-\alpha)}p^{n(1-\beta)}p^{-\frac{1}{2}(m+\text{ord}_{p}(h))-\frac{1}{2}(n+\text{ord}_{p}(k))}\\
&=p^{-(\frac{1}{2}-\alpha)\text{ord}_{p}(h)-(\frac{1}{2}+\alpha)\text{ord}_{p}(k)+\frac{m}{2}+n(\frac{1}{2}-\alpha-\beta)}\end{split}

because
\alpha(\text{ord}_{p}(h)-\text{ord}_{p}(k))=\alpha(n-m)
.

We are now ready to match each residue on the right-hand side of (
9.2.72
) with a residue on the right-hand side of (
9.1.32
) in such a way that corresponding residues are equal. The identity (
9.3.24
) implies that

\begin{split}h^{-\frac{1}{2}-\beta-s}k^{-\frac{1}{2}+\beta+s}\mathcal{G}(2,\alpha,\beta)&=\left(\frac{h}{k}\right)^{-s-\alpha-\beta}h^{-\frac{1}{2}+\alpha}k^{-\frac{1}{2}-\alpha}\mathcal{G}(2,\alpha,\beta)\\
&=\left(\frac{h}{k}\right)^{-s-\alpha-\beta}\mathcal{K}(0,-\alpha-\beta,2).\end{split}

From this, (
9.1.25
), and (
9.2.58
), we deduce that

(9.3.25)

R_{1}=J_{23}+O\big((Xhk)^{\varepsilon}k^{1/2}Q^{-96}\big).

Now from (
9.1.14
), (
9.2.63
), and Lemma
9.7
, we immediately see that

(9.3.26)

R_{21}=J_{11}+O\big((hk)^{\varepsilon}(h,k)^{1/2}Q^{-96}\big).

Next, (
9.1.20
), (
9.2.65
), and (
9.3.24
) imply

(9.3.27)

R_{22}=J_{21}+O\big((Xhk)^{\varepsilon}(h,k)^{1/2}Q^{-96}\big).

From (
9.1.29
), (
9.2.68
), and Lemma
9.11
with
\alpha^{*}=\alpha^{\prime}
and
\beta^{*}=\beta^{\prime}
, we deduce that

(9.3.28)

R_{23}=J_{31}+O\big((Xhk)^{\varepsilon}(h,k)^{1/2}Q^{-96}\big).

Finally, (
9.1.31
), (
9.2.71
), and Lemma
9.11
with
\alpha^{*}=\alpha^{\prime}
and
\beta^{*}=\beta^{\prime}
imply

R_{3}=J_{33}+O\big((Xhk)^{\varepsilon}k^{1/2}Q^{-96}\big).

From this, (
9.3.25
), (
9.3.26
), (
9.3.27
), (
9.3.28
), and (
9.2.72
), we arrive at

\begin{split}\mathcal{U}^{2}(h,k)&=J_{23}+J_{11}+J_{21}+J_{31}+J_{33}+O\bigg(\bigg(Q^{1+\varepsilon}+\frac{Q^{2}}{C^{1-\varepsilon}}\bigg)\frac{(Xhk)^{\varepsilon}(h,k)}{\sqrt{hk}}\bigg)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt+O\Big(X^{\varepsilon}Q^{\frac{3}{2}}h^{\varepsilon}k^{\varepsilon}+X^{-\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}Q^{\frac{5}{2}}(hk)^{\varepsilon}+(XChk)^{\varepsilon}hkX^{2}Q^{-96}\Big).\end{split}

From this and (
9.1.32
), we conclude that

(9.3.29)

\begin{split}\mathcal{U}^{2}(h,k)&=\mathcal{I}_{1}^{*}(h,k)+O\bigg(\bigg(Q^{1+\varepsilon}+\frac{Q^{2}}{C^{1-\varepsilon}}\bigg)\frac{(Xhk)^{\varepsilon}(h,k)}{\sqrt{hk}}\bigg)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt+O\big(X^{-\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}Q^{\frac{5}{2}}(hk)^{\varepsilon}+X^{\varepsilon}Q^{\frac{3}{2}+\varepsilon}(hk)^{\varepsilon}+(XChk)^{\varepsilon}hkX^{2}Q^{-96}\big).\end{split}

10.
The error term
\mathcal{U}^{r}(h,k)

Recall that
\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2},\dots
are arbitrary complex numbers such that
\lambda_{h}\ll_{\varepsilon}h^{\varepsilon}
for all
\varepsilon>0
. In this section, we bound the sum

\sum_{h,k\leq Q^{\vartheta}}\frac{\lambda_{h}\overline{\lambda_{k}}}{\sqrt{hk}}\mathcal{U}^{r}(h,k),

where
\mathcal{U}^{r}(h,k)
is defined by (
8.1.7
). The majority of the work that follows consists of preparing the above sum for an eventual application of the large sieve.

We begin by showing that the terms in (
8.1.7
) that have sufficiently large
a\ell
are zero. Since the support of
W
is compact and contained in
(0,\infty)
, the summand in the definition (
8.1.7
) of
\mathcal{U}^{r}(h,k)
is zero unless
|mh\pm nk|\asymp g\ell Q/c
, which implies that either
mh\gg g\ell Q/c
or
nk\gg g\ell Q/c
. Since
a|g
,
c\leq C
, and
h,k\leq Q^{\vartheta}
, this means that the summand in (
8.1.7
) is zero unless

m\gg\frac{Qg\ell}{hc}\geq\frac{Qa\ell}{CQ^{\vartheta}}\ \ \ \ \text{or}\ \ \ \ n\gg\frac{Qg\ell}{kc}\geq\frac{Qa\ell}{CQ^{\vartheta}}.

Now
V(m/X)V(n/X)=0
except if
m,n\ll X
. Thus the summand in (
8.1.7
) is zero unless

(10.1)

X\gg\frac{Qa\ell}{CQ^{\vartheta}}.

In other words, the terms in the definition (
8.1.7
) of
\mathcal{U}^{r}(h,k)
are zero unless
a\ell\ll XCQ^{\vartheta-1}
.

We next show that the terms in (
8.1.7
) that have sufficiently large
ae\ell
are negligible. We first consider the terms that have
mh/{g}\equiv\mp{nk}/{g}
(mod
ae\ell
). In this case,
|mh\pm nk|/g
is a multiple of
ae\ell
that is not zero because
mh\neq nk
. Thus
|mh\pm nk|/g\geq ae\ell
, and the triangle inequality implies that either
mh/g\geq ae\ell/2
or
nk/g\geq ae\ell/2
. Since
h,k\leq Q^{\vartheta}
and
g=(mh,nk)\geq 1
, these lower bounds imply that either
ae\ell\ll mQ^{\vartheta}
or
ae\ell\ll nQ^{\vartheta}
. Hence, using the support of
V
in the same way we deduced (
10.1
), we see that the terms in (
8.1.7
) that have
mh/{g}\equiv\mp{nk}/{g}
(mod
ae\ell
) are zero unless

ae\ell\ll XQ^{\vartheta}
.

Next, we consider the terms in (
8.1.7
) that have
mh/{g}\not\equiv\mp{nk}/{g}
(mod
ae\ell
) and
ae\ell\gg Y
, where
Y
is a large parameter that we will choose later (in Section
11
). For these terms, the orthogonality of Dirichlet characters implies that the
\psi
-sum in (
8.1.7
) is
O(1)
. Moreover, we have shown that these terms are zero unless (
10.1
) holds, and thus we may assume that
e\gg YQ^{\vartheta-1}/(XC)
. It follows from these and (
3.2
) that the sum of the terms in (
8.1.7
) that have
mh/{g}\not\equiv\mp{nk}/{g}
(mod
ae\ell
) and
ae\ell\gg Y
is bounded by

(10.2)

\ll\sum_{1\leq c\leq C}\sum_{1\leq m,n\ll X}\frac{(mn)^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{mn}}\sum_{\frac{YQ^{1-\vartheta}}{XC}\ll e<\infty}\frac{1}{e}\sum_{a|g}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq\ell<\infty\\
a\ell\ll XCQ^{\vartheta-1}\end{subarray}}\frac{(ae\ell)^{\varepsilon}}{ae\ell}\cdot\frac{|mh\pm nk|}{g\ell}W\left(\frac{c|mh\pm nk|}{g\ell Q}\right).

Since the support of
W
is compact and contained in
(0,\infty)
, we have
|mh\pm nk|/(g\ell)\ll Q/c
in (
10.2
), and so (
10.2
) is

\displaystyle\ll

\displaystyle(XCQ)^{\varepsilon}Q\sum_{1\leq c\leq C}\frac{1}{c}\sum_{1\leq m,n\ll X}\frac{(mn)^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{mn}}\left(\frac{YQ^{1-\vartheta}}{XC}\right)^{-1+\varepsilon}\ll(XCQY)^{\varepsilon}\frac{X^{2}CQ^{\vartheta}}{Y}.

This bound is small if
Y
is, say, a large power of
Q
. We have thus shown that the terms in (
8.1.7
) that have
mh/{g}\not\equiv\mp{nk}/{g}
(mod
ae\ell
) and
ae\ell\gg Y
are negligible for large enough
Y
.

From all these observations, we deduce for
h,k\leq Q^{\vartheta}
and
Y\geq XQ^{\vartheta}
that the total contribution of the terms in the definition (
8.1.7
) of
\mathcal{U}^{r}(h,k)
that have
a\ell\gg XCQ^{\vartheta-1}
or
ae\ell\gg Y
is

\ll(XCQY)^{\varepsilon}\frac{X^{2}CQ^{\vartheta}}{Y}.

Thus

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}^{r}(h,k)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq c\leq C\\
(c,hk)=1\end{subarray}}\mu(c)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq m,n<\infty\\
(mn,c)=1\\
mh\neq nk\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A}(m)\tau_{B}(n)}{\sqrt{mn}}V\left(\frac{m}{X}\right)V\left(\frac{n}{X}\right)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq e<\infty\\
(e,g)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(e)}{e}

\displaystyle\hskip 36.135pt\times\sum_{a|g}\mu(a)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq\ell<\infty\\
(ea\ell,\frac{mh}{g}\cdot\frac{nk}{g})=1\\
a\ell\ll XCQ^{\vartheta-1}\\
ae\ell\ll Y\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{\phi(ea\ell)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\psi\bmod ae\ell\\
\psi\neq\psi_{0}\end{subarray}}\psi\left(\frac{mh}{g}\right)\overline{\psi}\left(\mp\frac{nk}{g}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 54.2025pt\times\frac{|mh\pm nk|}{g\ell}W\left(\frac{c|mh\pm nk|}{g\ell Q}\right)\ +\ O\left((XCQY)^{\varepsilon}\frac{X^{2}CQ^{\vartheta}}{Y}\right).

We multiply both sides by
\lambda_{h}\overline{\lambda_{k}}(hk)^{-1/2}
and then sum over all
h,k\leq Q^{\vartheta}
to arrive at

(10.3)

\begin{split}&\sum_{h,k\leq Q^{\vartheta}}\frac{\lambda_{h}\overline{\lambda_{k}}}{\sqrt{hk}}\mathcal{U}^{r}(h,k)=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{1\leq c\leq C}\mu(c)\sum_{1\leq e<\infty}\frac{\mu(e)}{e}\sum_{1\leq\ell<\infty}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq a<\infty\\
a\ell\ll XCQ^{\vartheta-1}\\
ae\ell\ll Y\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(a)}{\phi(ae\ell)\ell}\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\psi\bmod ae\ell\\
\psi\neq\psi_{0}\end{subarray}}\Big\{\mathcal{U}^{+}(c,a,e,\ell,\psi)+\mathcal{U}^{-}(c,a,e,\ell,\psi)\Big\}\ +\ O\left((XCQY)^{\varepsilon}\frac{X^{2}CQ^{2\vartheta}}{Y}\right),\end{split}

where
\mathcal{U}^{\pm}(c,a,e,\ell,\psi)
is defined by

(10.4)

\begin{split}\mathcal{U}^{\pm}(c,a,e,\ell,\psi)&:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}h,k\leq Q^{\vartheta}\\
(c,hk)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\lambda_{h}\overline{\lambda_{k}}}{\sqrt{hk}}\sideset{}{{}^{\prime}}{\sum}_{m,n}\frac{\tau_{A}(m)\tau_{B}(n)}{\sqrt{mn}}V\left(\frac{m}{X}\right)V\left(\frac{n}{X}\right)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\psi\left(\frac{mh}{g}\right)\overline{\psi}\left(\mp\frac{nk}{g}\right)\frac{|mh\pm nk|}{g}W\left(\frac{c|mh\pm nk|}{g\ell Q}\right),\end{split}

with the symbol
\sum^{\prime}
denoting summation over all positive integers
m,n
such that
(mn,c)=1
,
mh\neq nk
,
(e,g)=1
,
a|g
, and
(ea\ell,mhnk/g^{2})=1
, where
g=(mh,nk)
. We split the
a,e,\ell
-sum in (
10.3
) into dyadic blocks and deduce that

(10.5)

\begin{split}&\sum_{h,k\leq Q^{\vartheta}}\frac{\lambda_{h}\overline{\lambda_{k}}}{\sqrt{hk}}\mathcal{U}^{r}(h,k)\ll\sum_{1\leq c\leq C}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}A,E,L\\
AL\ll XCQ^{\vartheta-1}\\
AEL\ll Y\end{subarray}}\sum_{A<a\leq 2A}\sum_{E<e\leq 2E}\sum_{L<\ell\leq 2L}\frac{(ae\ell)^{\varepsilon}}{ae^{2}\ell^{2}}\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\psi\bmod ae\ell\\
\psi\neq\psi_{0}\end{subarray}}\Big\{|\mathcal{U}^{+}(c,a,e,\ell,\psi)|+|\mathcal{U}^{-}(c,a,e,\ell,\psi)|\Big\}\ +\ (XCQY)^{\varepsilon}\frac{X^{2}CQ^{2\vartheta}}{Y},\end{split}

where each of the summation variables
A,E,L
runs through the set
\{2^{\nu}:\nu\in\mathbb{Z},\nu\geq-1\}
. Note that we are abusing notation here and using the symbol
A
to denote both the summation variable in (
10.5
) and the set in
\tau_{A}
in (
10.4
). However, this will not cause confusion.

To remove the interdependence of the summation variables in (
10.4
), we let
g_{1}=(h,k)
,
g_{2}=(m,n)
,
g_{3}=(m/g_{2},k/g_{1})
, and
g_{4}=(n/g_{2},h/g_{1})
, and make the change of variables
h=g_{1}g_{4}H
,
k=g_{1}g_{3}K
,
m=g_{2}g_{3}M
, and
n=g_{2}g_{4}N
. Recalling the definition
g=(mh,nk)
, we note that
g=g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}g_{4}
. By their definitions, the new variables satisfy the coprimality conditions
(g_{3},g_{4})=1
,
(H,g_{3})=1
,
(K,g_{4})=1
,
(H,K)=1
,
(M,g_{4})=1
,
(N,g_{3})=1
,
(M,N)=1
,
(M,K)=1
, and
(N,H)=1
. Furthermore, the properties of
m,h,n,k
in (
10.4
) are equivalent to
(c,MNHKg_{1}g_{2}g_{3}g_{4})=1
,
MH\neq NK
,
(e,g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}g_{4})=1
,
a|g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}g_{4}
,
(ea\ell,MNHK)=1
,
g_{1}g_{4}H\leq Q^{\vartheta}
,
g_{1}g_{3}K\leq Q^{\vartheta}
, and
1\leq M,N<\infty
. Since
V
has compact support, we may assume that
m,n\ll X
in (
10.4
) and hence
g_{2}\ll X
. Thus, the result of this change of variables is

(10.6)

\begin{split}\mathcal{U}^{\pm}(c,a,e,\ell,\psi)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}g_{1},g_{2},g_{3},g_{4}\\
M,N,H,K\end{subarray}}^{*}\frac{\lambda_{g_{1}g_{4}H}\overline{\lambda_{g_{1}g_{3}K}}}{g_{1}\sqrt{g_{3}g_{4}HK}}\frac{\tau_{A}(g_{2}g_{3}M)\tau_{B}(g_{2}g_{4}N)}{g_{2}\sqrt{g_{3}g_{4}MN}}V\left(\frac{g_{2}g_{3}M}{X}\right)V\left(\frac{g_{2}g_{4}N}{X}\right)\\
\times\psi(MH)\overline{\psi}(\mp NK)|MH\pm NK|W\left(\frac{c|MH\pm NK|}{\ell Q}\right),\end{split}

where
*
denotes the conditions for
g_{1},g_{2},g_{3},g_{4},M,N,H,K
listed above.

Our next task is to write (
10.6
) in terms of an Euler product. To this end, we apply Mellin inversion twice to write

(10.7)

\begin{split}V\left(\frac{g_{2}g_{3}x}{X}\right)&V\left(\frac{g_{2}g_{4}y}{X}\right)|xH\pm yK|W\left(\frac{c|xH\pm yK|}{\ell Q}\right)\\
&=\frac{1}{(2\pi i)^{2}}\int_{(\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon)}\int_{(\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon)}(xH)^{-s_{1}}(yK)^{-s_{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}u^{s_{1}-1}v^{s_{2}-1}\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times V\left(\frac{g_{2}g_{3}u}{HX}\right)V\left(\frac{g_{2}g_{4}v}{KX}\right)|u\pm v|W\left(\frac{c|u\pm v|}{\ell Q}\right)\,dv\,du\,ds_{2}\,ds_{1}.\end{split}

We have chosen the lines of integration to be at
\re(s_{1})=\re(s_{2})=\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon
to facilitate later estimations. We let
\Psi:[0,\infty)\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be a smooth nonnegative function of compact support such that
\Psi(\xi)=1
for all
\xi
in the support of
V
. Then

\displaystyle V\left(\frac{g_{2}g_{3}u}{HX}\right)=\Psi\Big(\frac{u}{XQ^{\vartheta}}\Big)V\left(\frac{g_{2}g_{3}u}{HX}\right)

for all
u\geq 0
, and applying Mellin inversion on the right-hand side gives

\displaystyle V\left(\frac{g_{2}g_{3}u}{HX}\right)=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\Psi\Big(\frac{u}{XQ^{\vartheta}}\Big)\int_{(\varepsilon)}\left(\frac{XH}{g_{2}g_{3}u}\right)^{s_{3}}\widetilde{V}(s_{3})\,ds_{3}.

Similarly,

\displaystyle V\left(\frac{g_{2}g_{4}v}{KX}\right)=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\Psi\Big(\frac{v}{XQ^{\vartheta}}\Big)\int_{(\varepsilon)}\left(\frac{XK}{g_{2}g_{4}v}\right)^{s_{4}}\widetilde{V}(s_{4})\,ds_{4}.

It follows from these and (
10.7
) that

\displaystyle V\left(\frac{g_{2}g_{3}x}{X}\right)

\displaystyle V\left(\frac{g_{2}g_{4}y}{X}\right)|xH\pm yK|W\left(\frac{c|xH\pm yK|}{\ell Q}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(2\pi i)^{4}}\int_{(\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon)}\int_{(\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon)}\int_{(\varepsilon)}\int_{(\varepsilon)}H^{-s_{1}+s_{3}}K^{-s_{2}+s_{4}}\left(\frac{X}{g_{2}g_{3}}\right)^{s_{3}}\left(\frac{X}{g_{2}g_{4}}\right)^{s_{4}}x^{-s_{1}}y^{-s_{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.06749pt\times\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}u^{s_{1}-s_{3}-1}v^{s_{2}-s_{4}-1}\Psi\Big(\frac{u}{XQ^{\vartheta}}\Big)\Psi\Big(\frac{v}{XQ^{\vartheta}}\Big)\widetilde{V}(s_{3})\widetilde{V}(s_{4})|u\pm v|

\displaystyle\hskip 36.135pt\times W\left(\frac{c|u\pm v|}{\ell Q}\right)\,dv\,du\,ds_{4}\,ds_{3}\,ds_{2}\,ds_{1}.

Now take
x=M
and
y=N
, and insert the result into (
10.6
) to deduce that

(10.8)

\begin{split}\mathcal{U}^{\pm}(c,a,e,\ell,\psi)&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}g_{1},g_{2},g_{3},g_{4}\\
M,N,H,K\end{subarray}}^{*}\frac{\lambda_{g_{1}g_{4}H}\overline{\lambda_{g_{1}g_{3}K}}}{g_{1}\sqrt{g_{3}g_{4}HK}}\frac{\tau_{A}(g_{2}g_{3}M)\tau_{B}(g_{2}g_{4}N)}{g_{2}\sqrt{g_{3}g_{4}MN}}\psi(MH)\overline{\psi}(\mp NK)\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\frac{1}{(2\pi i)^{4}}\int_{(\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon)}\int_{(\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon)}\int_{(\epsilon)}\int_{(\epsilon)}H^{-s_{1}+s_{3}}K^{-s_{2}+s_{4}}\left(\frac{X}{g_{2}g_{3}}\right)^{s_{3}}\left(\frac{X}{g_{2}g_{4}}\right)^{s_{4}}\\
&\hskip 36.135pt\times M^{-s_{1}}N^{-s_{2}}\mathcal{V}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3},s_{4})\,ds_{4}\,ds_{3}\,ds_{2}\,ds_{1},\end{split}

where
\mathcal{V}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3},s_{4})
is defined by

(10.9)

\begin{split}\mathcal{V}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3},s_{4})&=\mathcal{V}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3},s_{4};X,Q,\vartheta,c,\ell)\\
&:=\widetilde{V}(s_{3})\widetilde{V}(s_{4})\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}u^{s_{1}-s_{3}-1}v^{s_{2}-s_{4}-1}\Psi\Big(\frac{u}{XQ^{\vartheta}}\Big)\Psi\Big(\frac{v}{XQ^{\vartheta}}\Big)\\
&\hskip 36.135pt\times|u\pm v|W\left(\frac{c|u\pm v|}{\ell Q}\right)\,dv\,du.\end{split}

The following lemma gives a bound for
\mathcal{V}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3},s_{4})
, and is analogous to (
3.10
).

Lemma 10.1
.

If
j_{1},j_{2}
are nonnegative integers and
s_{1},s_{2},s_{3},s_{4}
are complex numbers such that
j_{1}+j_{2}\geq 1
,
\re(s_{1}-s_{3})>0
, and
\re(s_{2}-s_{4})>0
, then

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}u^{s_{1}-s_{3}-1}v^{s_{2}-s_{4}-1}\Psi\Big(\frac{u}{XQ^{\vartheta}}\Big)\Psi\Big(\frac{v}{XQ^{\vartheta}}\Big)|u\pm v|W\left(\frac{c|u\pm v|}{\ell Q}\right)\,dv\,du

\displaystyle\ll\frac{(XQ^{\vartheta})^{\re(s_{1}+s_{2}-s_{3}-s_{4})}}{|s_{1}-s_{3}|^{j_{1}}|s_{2}-s_{4}|^{j_{2}}}\left(\frac{\ell Q}{c}\right)\bigg(1+\frac{XcQ^{\vartheta-1}}{\ell}\bigg)^{j_{1}+j_{2}-1},

where the implied constant may depend only on
\Psi
,
W
,
\re(s_{1}-s_{3})
,
\re(s_{2}-s_{4})
,
j_{1}
, or
j_{2}
.

Proof.

For brevity, let
\mathcal{D}
denote the double integral in question, and let
W_{0}
denote the function
W_{0}(\xi):=\xi W(\xi)
. Make the change of variables
u\mapsto{u\ell Q}/{c}
and
v\mapsto{v\ell Q}/{c}
, then integrate by parts with respect to
u

j_{1}
times and with respect to
v

j_{2}
times to deduce that

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{j_{1}+j_{2}}\left(\frac{\ell Q}{c}\right)^{s_{1}+s_{2}-s_{3}-s_{4}+1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{u^{s_{1}-s_{3}+j_{1}-1}}{(s_{1}-s_{3})\cdots(s_{1}-s_{3}+j_{1}-1)}

\displaystyle\times\frac{v^{s_{2}-s_{4}+j_{2}-1}}{(s_{2}-s_{4})\cdots(s_{2}-s_{4}+j_{2}-1)}\frac{\partial^{j_{1}}}{\partial u^{j_{1}}}\frac{\partial^{j_{2}}}{\partial v^{j_{2}}}\bigg\{\Psi\Big(\frac{u\ell}{XcQ^{\vartheta-1}}\Big)\Psi\Big(\frac{v\ell}{XcQ^{\vartheta-1}}\Big)W_{0}(|u\pm v|)\bigg\}\,dv\,du.

We may use the product rule and chain rule to bound the derivatives in the integrand. We also observe that the integrand is zero unless
u,v\ll XcQ^{\vartheta-1}/\ell
and
|u\pm v|\asymp 1
, because
\Psi
is supported on a compact subset of
[0,\infty)
and
W
is supported on a compact subset of
(0,\infty)
. Thus

(10.10)

\begin{split}\mathcal{D}\ll\left(\frac{\ell Q}{c}\right)^{\re(s_{1}+s_{2}-s_{3}-s_{4})+1}\bigg(1+\frac{\ell}{XcQ^{\vartheta-1}}\bigg)^{j_{1}+j_{2}}\frac{1}{|s_{1}-s_{3}|^{j_{1}}|s_{2}-s_{4}|^{j_{2}}}\\
\times\mathop{\iint}_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq u,v\ll{XcQ^{\vartheta-1}}/{\ell}\\
|u\pm v|\asymp 1\end{subarray}}u^{\re(s_{1}-s_{3})+j_{1}-1}v^{\re(s_{2}-s_{4})+j_{2}-1}\,dv\,du.\end{split}

Since
j_{1},j_{2}
are nonnegative integers with
j_{1}+j_{2}\geq 1
, it holds that either
j_{1}\geq 1
or
j_{2}\geq 1
. By renaming the variables
u
and
v
if necessary, we may suppose, without loss of generality, that
j_{2}\geq 1
. Then
v^{j_{2}-1}\ll(XcQ^{\vartheta-1}/{\ell})^{j_{2}-1}
. Moreover, for each
u
, the
v
-integral is over an interval of length
\ll\min\{1,XcQ^{\vartheta-1}/{\ell}\}
. Hence the
u,v
-integral in (
10.10
) is at most

\ll\bigg(\frac{XcQ^{\vartheta-1}}{\ell}\bigg)^{\re(s_{1}+s_{2}-s_{3}-s_{4})+j_{1}+j_{2}-1}\min\left\{1,\frac{XcQ^{\vartheta-1}}{\ell}\right\}.

Since
\min\{1,1/x\}\asymp 1/(1+x)
for
x>0
, this proves the lemma.
∎

Now (
3.10
), (
10.9
), and Lemma
10.1
imply that

(10.11)

\begin{split}\mathcal{V}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3},s_{4})&\ll_{\varepsilon,j_{1},j_{2},j_{3},j_{4}}\frac{(XQ^{\vartheta})^{\re(s_{1}+s_{2}-s_{3}-s_{4})}}{|s_{1}-s_{3}|^{j_{1}}|s_{2}-s_{4}|^{j_{2}}|s_{3}|^{j_{3}}|s_{4}|^{j_{4}}}\left(\frac{\ell Q}{c}\right)\bigg(1+\frac{XcQ^{\vartheta-1}}{\ell}\bigg)^{j_{1}+j_{2}-1}\end{split}

for any nonnegative integers
j_{1},j_{2},j_{3},j_{4}
with
j_{1}+j_{2}\geq 1
and any complex numbers
s_{1},s_{2},s_{3},s_{4}
such that each of Re
(s_{1}-s_{3})
, Re
(s_{2}-s_{4})
, Re
(s_{3})
, and Re
(s_{4})
is
\geq\varepsilon
. It follows that (
10.8
) is absolutely convergent, and we may interchange the order of summation to deduce that, recalling the conditions indicated by
*
and listed before (
10.6
), we have

(10.12)

\begin{split}\mathcal{U}^{\pm}(c,a,e,\ell,\psi)=&\frac{1}{(2\pi i)^{4}}\int_{(\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon)}\int_{(\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon)}\int_{(\epsilon)}\int_{(\epsilon)}X^{s_{3}+s_{4}}\\
&\times\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq g_{1},g_{2},g_{3},g_{4},H,K\ll\max\{Q^{\vartheta},X\}\\
g_{1}g_{4}H\leq Q^{\vartheta},\ g_{1}g_{3}K\leq Q^{\vartheta},\ g_{2}\ll X\\
(g_{3},g_{4})=(H,g_{3})=(K,g_{4})=(H,K)=1\\
(ec,g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}g_{4})=(cae\ell,HK)=1\\
a|g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}g_{4}\end{subarray}}\frac{\lambda_{g_{1}g_{4}H}\overline{\lambda_{g_{1}g_{3}K}}\,\psi(H)\overline{\psi}(\mp K)}{g_{1}g_{2}^{1+s_{3}+s_{4}}g_{3}^{1+s_{3}}g_{4}^{1+s_{4}}H^{\frac{1}{2}+s_{1}-s_{3}}K^{\frac{1}{2}+s_{2}-s_{4}}}\\
&\times\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq M,N<\infty\\
(M,g_{4})=(N,g_{3})=(M,K)=(N,H)=1\\
(M,N)=(MN,cae\ell)=1\\
MH\neq NK\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A}(g_{2}g_{3}M)\tau_{B}(g_{2}g_{4}N)\psi(M)\overline{\psi}(N)}{M^{\frac{1}{2}+s_{1}}N^{\frac{1}{2}+s_{2}}}\\
&\times\mathcal{V}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3},s_{4})\,ds_{4}\,ds_{3}\,ds_{2}\,ds_{1}.\end{split}

We next write the
M,N
-sum in terms of an Euler product. To do this, we first add and subtract the terms with
MH=NK
and write

(10.13)

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq M,N<\infty\\
(M,g_{4})=(N,g_{3})=(M,K)=(N,H)=1\\
(M,N)=(MN,cae\ell)=1\\
MH\neq NK\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A}(g_{2}g_{3}M)\tau_{B}(g_{2}g_{4}N)\psi(M)\overline{\psi}(N)}{M^{\frac{1}{2}+s_{1}}N^{\frac{1}{2}+s_{2}}}=\mathscr{P}_{1}-\mathscr{P}_{2},

where
\mathscr{P}_{1}
is the sum on the left-hand side, except without the condition
MH\neq NK
, and
\mathscr{P}_{2}
is the sum with the condition
MH=NK
instead of
MH\neq NK
. To evaluate
\mathscr{P}_{2}
, observe that the conditions
(H,K)=1
and
(M,N)=1
imply that
MH=NK
if and only if
M=K
and
N=H
. Since
(M,K)=(N,H)=1
, this is only possible if
M=N=H=K=1
. Thus

(10.14)

\mathscr{P}_{2}=\tau_{A}(g_{2}g_{3})\tau_{B}(g_{2}g_{4}).

Next, we express the sum
\mathscr{P}_{1}
defined in (
10.13
) as an Euler product and write

(10.15)

\mathscr{P}_{1}=\prod_{\alpha\in A}L(\tfrac{1}{2}+s_{1}+\alpha,\psi)\prod_{\beta\in B}L(\tfrac{1}{2}+s_{2}+\beta,\overline{\psi})\mathcal{R}(s_{1},s_{2}),

where
\mathcal{R}(s_{1},s_{2})
is defined by

(10.16)

\begin{split}\mathcal{R}(s_{1},s_{2})=&\mathcal{R}(s_{1},s_{2};g_{2},g_{3},g_{4},H,K,cae\ell)\\
:=&\prod_{p}\Bigg\{\prod_{\alpha\in A}\left(1-\frac{\psi(p)}{p^{\frac{1}{2}+s_{1}+\alpha}}\right)\prod_{\beta\in B}\left(1-\frac{\overline{\psi}(p)}{p^{\frac{1}{2}+s_{2}+\beta}}\right)\\
&\times\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m,n<\infty\\
\min\{m,\text{ord}_{p}(g_{4}K)\}=\min\{n,\text{ord}_{p}(g_{3}H)\}=0\\
\min\{m,n\}=\min\{mn,\text{ord}_{p}(cae\ell)\}=0\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m+\text{ord}_{p}(g_{2}g_{3})})\tau_{B}(p^{n+\text{ord}_{p}(g_{2}g_{4})})\psi(p^{m})\overline{\psi}(p^{n})}{p^{m(\frac{1}{2}+s_{1})+n(\frac{1}{2}+s_{2})}}\Bigg\}.\end{split}

If
\re(s_{1}),\re(s_{2})\geq\varepsilon
and
p|g_{2}g_{3}g_{4}HKcae\ell
, then the local factor in (
10.16
) corresponding to
p
is
O(p^{\varepsilon\text{ord}_{p}(g_{2}g_{3}g_{4})})
by (
3.2
). Moreover, if
\re(s_{1}),\re(s_{2})\geq\varepsilon
and
p\nmid g_{2}g_{3}g_{4}HKcae\ell
, then it follows from (
3.1
) and (
3.2
) that the local factor in (
10.16
) corresponding to
p
is

\displaystyle\prod_{\alpha\in A}

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{\psi(p)}{p^{\frac{1}{2}+s_{1}+\alpha}}\right)\prod_{\beta\in B}\left(1-\frac{\overline{\psi}(p)}{p^{\frac{1}{2}+s_{2}+\beta}}\right)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m,n<\infty\\
\min\{m,n\}=0\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau_{A}(p^{m})\tau_{B}(p^{n})\psi(p^{m})\overline{\psi}(p^{n})}{p^{m(\frac{1}{2}+s_{1})+n(\frac{1}{2}+s_{2})}}

\displaystyle=\left(1-\frac{\tau_{A}(p)\psi(p)}{p^{\frac{1}{2}+s_{1}}}+O\left(\frac{1}{p^{1+\varepsilon}}\right)\right)\left(1-\frac{\tau_{B}(p)\overline{\psi}(p)}{p^{\frac{1}{2}+s_{2}}}+O\left(\frac{1}{p^{1+\varepsilon}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.06749pt\times\left(1+\frac{\tau_{A}(p)\psi(p)}{p^{\frac{1}{2}+s_{1}}}+\frac{\tau_{B}(p)\overline{\psi}(p)}{p^{\frac{1}{2}+s_{2}}}+O\left(\frac{1}{p^{1+\varepsilon}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=1+O\left(\frac{1}{p^{1+\varepsilon}}\right).

Thus, if
\re(s_{1}),\re(s_{2})\geq\varepsilon
, then the product in (
10.16
) converges absolutely and we have

(10.17)

\mathcal{R}(s_{1},s_{2})\ll(g_{2}g_{3}g_{4}HKcae\ell)^{\varepsilon}

because
\prod_{p|\nu}O(1)\ll\nu^{\varepsilon}
for any positive integer
\nu
. Hence, (
10.13
) with (
10.14
) and (
10.15
) gives an analytic continuation of the
M,N
-sum in (
10.12
) to the region with
\re(s_{1}),\re(s_{2})\geq\varepsilon
. If
\psi
is non-principal, then this analytic continuation has no poles in the region, and (
3.2
) and (
10.17
) imply that it is bounded by

\ll(g_{2}g_{3}g_{4}HKcae\ell)^{\varepsilon}\Bigg\{1+\prod_{\alpha\in A}|L(\tfrac{1}{2}+s_{1}+\alpha,\psi)|\prod_{\beta\in B}|L(\tfrac{1}{2}+s_{2}+\beta,\overline{\psi})|\Bigg\}

for
\re(s_{1}),\re(s_{2})\geq\varepsilon
. This fact together with (
10.11
) implies that if
\psi
is non-principal, then we may move the
s_{1}
- and
s_{2}
-lines in (
10.12
) to
\re(s_{1})=\re(s_{2})=2\epsilon
and deduce that

(10.18)

\begin{split}\mathcal{U}^{\pm}(c,a,e,\ell,\psi)\ll&(XQcae\ell)^{\varepsilon}\int_{(2\epsilon)}\int_{(2\epsilon)}\int_{(\epsilon)}\int_{(\epsilon)}\\
&\times\Bigg|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq g_{1},g_{2},g_{3},g_{4},H,K\ll\max\{Q^{\vartheta},X\}\\
g_{1}g_{4}H\leq Q^{\vartheta},\ g_{1}g_{3}K\leq Q^{\vartheta},\ g_{2}\ll X\\
(g_{3},g_{4})=(H,g_{3})=(K,g_{4})=(H,K)=1\\
(ec,g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}g_{4})=(cae\ell,HK)=1\\
a|g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}g_{4}\end{subarray}}\frac{\lambda_{g_{1}g_{4}H}\overline{\lambda_{g_{1}g_{3}K}}\,\psi(H)\overline{\psi}(\mp K)}{g_{1}g_{2}^{1+s_{3}+s_{4}}g_{3}^{1+s_{3}}g_{4}^{1+s_{4}}H^{\frac{1}{2}+s_{1}-s_{3}}K^{\frac{1}{2}+s_{2}-s_{4}}}\Bigg|\\
&\times\Bigg\{1+\prod_{\alpha\in A}|L(\tfrac{1}{2}+s_{1}+\alpha,\psi)|\prod_{\beta\in B}|L(\tfrac{1}{2}+s_{2}+\beta,\overline{\psi})|\Bigg\}\\
&\times|\mathcal{V}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3},s_{4})|\,|ds_{4}\,ds_{3}\,ds_{2}\,ds_{1}|.\end{split}

We apply Möbius inversion to remove the interdependence of the variables
H
and
K
and write

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq H,K\ll\max\{Q^{\vartheta},X\}\\
g_{1}g_{4}H\leq Q^{\vartheta},\ g_{1}g_{3}K\leq Q^{\vartheta}\\
(H,g_{3})=(K,g_{4})=(H,K)=1\\
(cae\ell,HK)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\lambda_{g_{1}g_{4}H}\overline{\lambda_{g_{1}g_{3}K}}\,\psi(H)\overline{\psi}(\mp K)}{H^{\frac{1}{2}+s_{1}-s_{3}}K^{\frac{1}{2}+s_{2}-s_{4}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 36.135pt=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq H,K\ll\max\{Q^{\vartheta},X\}\\
g_{1}g_{4}H\leq Q^{\vartheta},\ g_{1}g_{3}K\leq Q^{\vartheta}\\
(H,g_{3})=(K,g_{4})=1\\
(cae\ell,HK)=1\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d|H\\
d|K\end{subarray}}\mu(d)\frac{\lambda_{g_{1}g_{4}H}\overline{\lambda_{g_{1}g_{3}K}}\,\psi(H)\overline{\psi}(\mp K)}{H^{\frac{1}{2}+s_{1}-s_{3}}K^{\frac{1}{2}+s_{2}-s_{4}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 36.135pt=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d\leq Q^{\vartheta}\\
(d,g_{3}g_{4}cae\ell)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(d)|\psi(d)|^{2}}{d^{1+s_{1}+s_{2}-s_{3}-s_{4}}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}H\leq Q^{\vartheta}/(dg_{1}g_{4})\\
(H,g_{3}cae\ell)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\lambda_{dg_{1}g_{4}H}\,\psi(H)}{H^{\frac{1}{2}+s_{1}-s_{3}}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}K\leq Q^{\vartheta}/(dg_{1}g_{3})\\
(K,g_{4}cae\ell)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\overline{\lambda_{dg_{1}g_{3}K}}\,\overline{\psi}(\mp K)}{K^{\frac{1}{2}+s_{2}-s_{4}}},

where in the last line we have made the change of variables
H\mapsto dH
and
K\mapsto dK
. From this, (
10.18
), the triangle inequality, and the fact that
\psi(\mp K)=\psi(\mp 1)\psi(K)
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}^{\pm}(c,a,e,\ell,\psi)\ll

\displaystyle(XQcae\ell)^{\varepsilon}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq g_{1},g_{2},g_{3},g_{4}\ll\max\{Q^{\vartheta},X\}\\
g_{1}g_{4}\leq Q^{\vartheta},\ g_{1}g_{3}\leq Q^{\vartheta},\ g_{2}\ll X\\
(g_{3},g_{4})=(ec,g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}g_{4})=1\\
a|g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}g_{4}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{g_{1}g_{2}^{1+\varepsilon}g_{3}^{1+\varepsilon}g_{4}^{1+\varepsilon}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d\leq Q^{\vartheta}\\
(d,g_{3}g_{4}cae\ell)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{d^{1+\varepsilon}}

\displaystyle\times\int_{(2\epsilon)}\int_{(2\epsilon)}\int_{(\epsilon)}\int_{(\epsilon)}\Bigg|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}H\leq Q^{\vartheta}/(dg_{1}g_{4})\\
(H,g_{3}cae\ell)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\lambda_{dg_{1}g_{4}H}\,\psi(H)}{H^{\frac{1}{2}+s_{1}-s_{3}}}\Bigg|\Bigg|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}K\leq Q^{\vartheta}/(dg_{1}g_{3})\\
(K,g_{4}cae\ell)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\overline{\lambda_{dg_{1}g_{3}K}}\,\overline{\psi}(K)}{K^{\frac{1}{2}+s_{2}-s_{4}}}\Bigg|

\displaystyle\times\Bigg\{1+\prod_{\alpha\in A}|L(\tfrac{1}{2}+s_{1}+\alpha,\psi)|\prod_{\beta\in B}|L(\tfrac{1}{2}+s_{2}+\beta,\overline{\psi})|\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\times|\mathcal{V}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3},s_{4})|\,|ds_{4}\,ds_{3}\,ds_{2}\,ds_{1}|.

From this and (
10.5
), we arrive at

(10.19)

\begin{split}\sum_{h,k\leq Q^{\vartheta}}\frac{\lambda_{h}\overline{\lambda_{k}}}{\sqrt{hk}}\mathcal{U}^{r}(h,k)&\ll\sum_{1\leq c\leq C}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}A,E,L\\
AL\ll XCQ^{\vartheta-1}\\
AEL\ll Y\end{subarray}}\sum_{A<a\leq 2A}\sum_{E<e\leq 2E}\frac{(CXQY)^{\varepsilon}}{AE^{2}L^{2}}\\
&\times\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq g_{1},g_{2},g_{3},g_{4}\ll\max\{Q^{\vartheta},X\}\\
g_{1}g_{4}\leq Q^{\vartheta},\ g_{1}g_{3}\leq Q^{\vartheta},\ g_{2}\ll X\\
(g_{3},g_{4})=(ec,g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}g_{4})=1\\
a|g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}g_{4}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{g_{1}g_{2}^{1+\varepsilon}g_{3}^{1+\varepsilon}g_{4}^{1+\varepsilon}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d\leq Q^{\vartheta}\\
(d,g_{3}g_{4}cae)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{d^{1+\varepsilon}}\\
&\times\Sigma_{c,a,e,d,g_{1},g_{2},g_{3},g_{4}}\ +\ (XCQY)^{\varepsilon}\frac{X^{2}CQ^{2\vartheta}}{Y},\end{split}

where
\Sigma_{c,a,e,d,g_{1},g_{2},g_{3},g_{4}}
is defined by

\displaystyle\Sigma_{c,a,e,d,g_{1},g_{2},g_{3},g_{4}}:=

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}L<\ell\leq 2L\\
(d,\ell)=1\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\psi\bmod ae\ell\\
\psi\neq\psi_{0}\end{subarray}}\int_{(2\epsilon)}\int_{(2\epsilon)}\int_{(\epsilon)}\int_{(\epsilon)}

\displaystyle\Bigg|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}H\leq Q^{\vartheta}/(dg_{1}g_{4})\\
(H,g_{3}cae\ell)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\lambda_{dg_{1}g_{4}H}\,\psi(H)}{H^{\frac{1}{2}+s_{1}-s_{3}}}\Bigg|\Bigg|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}K\leq Q^{\vartheta}/(dg_{1}g_{3})\\
(K,g_{4}cae\ell)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\overline{\lambda_{dg_{1}g_{3}K}}\,\overline{\psi}(K)}{K^{\frac{1}{2}+s_{2}-s_{4}}}\Bigg|

\displaystyle\Bigg\{1+\prod_{\alpha\in A}|L(\tfrac{1}{2}+s_{1}+\alpha,\psi)|\prod_{\beta\in B}|L(\tfrac{1}{2}+s_{2}+\beta,\overline{\psi})|\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\times|\mathcal{V}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3},s_{4})|\,|ds_{4}\,ds_{3}\,ds_{2}\,ds_{1}|.

We interchange the order of integration and then make the change of variables
s_{5}=s_{1}-s_{3}
and
s_{6}=s_{2}-s_{4}
to write

(10.20)

\begin{split}\Sigma_{c,a,e,d,g_{1},g_{2},g_{3},g_{4}}=&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}L<\ell\leq 2L\\
(d,\ell)=1\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\psi\bmod ae\ell\\
\psi\neq\psi_{0}\end{subarray}}\int_{(\epsilon)}\int_{(\epsilon)}\int_{(\epsilon)}\int_{(\epsilon)}|\mathcal{V}(s_{3}+s_{5},s_{4}+s_{6},s_{3},s_{4})|\\
&\times\Bigg|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}H\leq Q^{\vartheta}/(dg_{1}g_{4})\\
(H,g_{3}cae\ell)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\lambda_{dg_{1}g_{4}H}\,\psi(H)}{H^{\frac{1}{2}+s_{5}}}\Bigg|\Bigg|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}K\leq Q^{\vartheta}/(dg_{1}g_{3})\\
(K,g_{4}cae\ell)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\overline{\lambda_{dg_{1}g_{3}K}}\,\overline{\psi}(K)}{K^{\frac{1}{2}+s_{6}}}\Bigg|\\
&\times\Bigg\{1+\prod_{\alpha\in A}|L(\tfrac{1}{2}+s_{3}+s_{5}+\alpha,\psi)|\prod_{\beta\in B}|L(\tfrac{1}{2}+s_{4}+s_{6}+\beta,\overline{\psi})|\Bigg\}\\
&\times\,|ds_{6}\,ds_{5}\,ds_{4}\,ds_{3}|.\end{split}

Now GLH and the Phragmén-Lindelöf principle together imply that if
\varepsilon>0
then

L(s,\psi)\ll_{\varepsilon}(q(1+|t|))^{\varepsilon}

for all
s=\sigma+it
with
\frac{1}{2}\leq\sigma\leq 1
and real
t
and all non-principal Dirichlet characters
\psi
modulo
q
, where the implied constant depends only on
\varepsilon
. It follows from this and (
10.20
) that

(10.21)

\begin{split}\Sigma_{c,a,e,d,g_{1},g_{2},g_{3},g_{4}}\ll&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}L<\ell\leq 2L\\
(d,\ell)=1\end{subarray}}(ae\ell)^{\varepsilon}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\psi\bmod ae\ell\\
\psi\neq\psi_{0}\end{subarray}}\int_{(\epsilon)}\int_{(\epsilon)}\int_{(\epsilon)}\int_{(\epsilon)}|\mathcal{V}(s_{3}+s_{5},s_{4}+s_{6},s_{3},s_{4})|\\
&\times|s_{3}s_{4}s_{5}s_{6}|^{\varepsilon}\Bigg|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}H\leq Q^{\vartheta}/(dg_{1}g_{4})\\
(H,g_{3}cae\ell)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\lambda_{dg_{1}g_{4}H}\,\psi(H)}{H^{\frac{1}{2}+s_{5}}}\Bigg|\Bigg|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}K\leq Q^{\vartheta}/(dg_{1}g_{3})\\
(K,g_{4}cae\ell)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\overline{\lambda_{dg_{1}g_{3}K}}\,\overline{\psi}(K)}{K^{\frac{1}{2}+s_{6}}}\Bigg|\\
&\times\,|ds_{6}\,ds_{5}\,ds_{4}\,ds_{3}|.\end{split}

Our next task is to apply the bound (
10.11
) for
\mathcal{V}
. We will facilitate later estimations by choosing particular values of
j_{1},j_{2},j_{3},j_{4}
in (
10.11
) for specific ranges of
s_{5}
and
s_{6}
. To this end, we split the range of integration of the
s_{5}
- and
s_{6}
-integrals in (
10.21
) into dyadic segments to write

(10.22)

\begin{split}&\Sigma_{c,a,e,d,g_{1},g_{2},g_{3},g_{4}}\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\ll\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}L<\ell\leq 2L\\
(d,\ell)=1\end{subarray}}(ae\ell)^{\varepsilon}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\psi\bmod ae\ell\\
\psi\neq\psi_{0}\end{subarray}}\sum_{S_{5}}\sum_{S_{6}}\mathop{\int}_{\begin{subarray}{c}S_{5}\leq|s_{5}|\leq 2S_{5}\\
\re(s_{5})=\epsilon\end{subarray}}\mathop{\int}_{\begin{subarray}{c}S_{6}\leq|s_{6}|\leq 2S_{6}\\
\re(s_{6})=\epsilon\end{subarray}}\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\int_{(\epsilon)}\int_{(\epsilon)}|s_{3}s_{4}s_{5}s_{6}|^{\varepsilon}|\mathcal{V}(s_{3}+s_{5},s_{4}+s_{6},s_{3},s_{4})|\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\Bigg|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}H\leq Q^{\vartheta}/(dg_{1}g_{4})\\
(H,g_{3}cae\ell)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\lambda_{dg_{1}g_{4}H}\,\psi(H)}{H^{\frac{1}{2}+s_{5}}}\Bigg|\Bigg|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}K\leq Q^{\vartheta}/(dg_{1}g_{3})\\
(K,g_{4}cae\ell)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\overline{\lambda_{dg_{1}g_{3}K}}\,\overline{\psi}(K)}{K^{\frac{1}{2}+s_{6}}}\Bigg|\,|ds_{4}\,ds_{3}\,ds_{6}\,ds_{5}|,\end{split}

where each of
S_{5}
and
S_{6}
runs through the set
\{0\}\cup\{2^{\nu}:\nu\in\mathbb{Z},\nu\geq 0\}
. Here, we make an abuse of notation and interpret the condition
S_{5}\leq|s_{5}|\leq 2S_{5}
to mean
\epsilon\leq|s_{5}|\leq 1
when

, and similarly for
S_{6}
. We now apply (

). We choose

in every situation, while we choose

We arrive at

(10.23)

\begin{split}&\Sigma_{c,a,e,d,g_{1},g_{2},g_{3},g_{4}}\ll(XQ)^{\varepsilon}\left(\frac{LQ}{c}\right)\sum_{S_{5}>0}\sum_{S_{6}>0}\frac{1}{S_{5}^{j_{1}-\varepsilon}S_{6}^{j_{2}-\varepsilon}}\bigg(1+\frac{XcQ^{\vartheta-1}}{L}\bigg)^{j_{1}+j_{2}-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}L<\ell\leq 2L\\
(d,\ell)=1\end{subarray}}(ae\ell)^{\varepsilon}\\
&\times\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\psi\bmod ae\ell\\
\psi\neq\psi_{0}\end{subarray}}\int_{\epsilon-i2S_{5}}^{\epsilon+i2S_{5}}\int_{\epsilon-i2S_{6}}^{\epsilon+i2S_{6}}\Bigg|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}H\leq Q^{\vartheta}/(dg_{1}g_{4})\\
(H,g_{3}cae\ell)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\lambda_{dg_{1}g_{4}H}\,\psi(H)}{H^{\frac{1}{2}+s_{5}}}\Bigg|\Bigg|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}K\leq Q^{\vartheta}/(dg_{1}g_{3})\\
(K,g_{4}cae\ell)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\overline{\lambda_{dg_{1}g_{3}K}}\,\overline{\psi}(K)}{K^{\frac{1}{2}+s_{6}}}\Bigg|\,|ds_{6}\,ds_{5}|,\end{split}

where the values of
j_{1}
and
j_{2}
depend on
S_{5}
and
S_{6}
as described in Table

. Note that, for conciseness, we have bounded the term with
S_{5}=S_{6}=0
in (
10.22
) by the term with
S_{5}=S_{6}=1
in (
10.23
). We may do this because both terms have the same value of
j_{1}+j_{2}
by Table

. Similarly, we have bounded the sum of the terms with

and
S_{6}>0
in (

) by the sum of the terms with
S_{5}=1
and
S_{6}>0
in (
10.23
), and we have bounded the sum of the terms with
S_{5}>0
and
S_{6}=0
in (
10.22
) by the sum of the terms with
S_{5}>0
and
S_{6}=1
in (
10.23
).

In order to be able to apply the large sieve inequality, we use the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality to deduce from (
10.23
) that

\displaystyle\Sigma_{c,a,e,d,g_{1},g_{2},g_{3},g_{4}}\ll

\displaystyle(XQ)^{\varepsilon}\left(\frac{LQ}{c}\right)\sum_{S_{5}>0}\sum_{S_{6}>0}\frac{(aeLS_{5}S_{6})^{\varepsilon}}{S_{5}^{j_{1}}S_{6}^{j_{2}}}\bigg(1+\frac{XcQ^{\vartheta-1}}{L}\bigg)^{j_{1}+j_{2}-1}

\displaystyle\times\Bigg(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}L<\ell\leq 2L\\
(d,\ell)=1\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\psi\bmod ae\ell\\
\psi\neq\psi_{0}\end{subarray}}\Bigg\{\int_{\varepsilon-i2S_{5}}^{\varepsilon+i2S_{5}}\Bigg|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}H\leq Q^{\vartheta}/(dg_{1}g_{4})\\
(H,g_{3}cae\ell)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\lambda_{dg_{1}g_{4}H}\,\psi(H)}{H^{\frac{1}{2}+s_{5}}}\Bigg|\,|ds_{5}|\Bigg\}^{2}\Bigg)^{1/2}

(10.24)

\displaystyle\times\Bigg(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}L<\ell\leq 2L\\
(d,\ell)=1\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\psi\bmod ae\ell\\
\psi\neq\psi_{0}\end{subarray}}\Bigg\{\int_{\varepsilon-i2S_{6}}^{\varepsilon+i2S_{6}}\Bigg|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}K\leq Q^{\vartheta}/(dg_{1}g_{3})\\
(K,g_{4}cae\ell)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\overline{\lambda_{dg_{1}g_{3}K}}\,\overline{\psi}(K)}{K^{\frac{1}{2}+s_{6}}}\Bigg|\,|ds_{6}|\Bigg\}^{2}\Bigg)^{1/2}.

We now apply the hybrid large sieve inequality in the form of the following lemma.

Lemma 10.2
.

Let
R,T,N,\sigma
be real numbers with
T\geq 3
,
R,N\geq 1
, and
\sigma\geq 1/2
, and let
j
be a positive integer. If
\{a_{n}\}
is any sequence of complex numbers, then

\sum_{q\leq R}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\chi\bmod{qj}\\
\chi\neq\chi_{0}\end{subarray}}\Bigg(\int_{-T}^{T}\Bigg|\sum_{n\leq N}\frac{a_{n}\chi(n)}{n^{\sigma+it}}\Bigg|\,dt\Bigg)^{2}\ll_{\varepsilon}(jRNT)^{\varepsilon}(RNT+jR^{2}T^{2})\sum_{n\leq N}\frac{|a_{n}|^{2}}{n^{2\sigma}},

where the
\chi
-sum is over all non-principal Dirichlet characters
\chi
mod
qj
.

Proof.

The proof of the lemma is contained within the proof of Proposition 1 of
[
CIS19
]
. For full details, see Appendix
A
.
∎

From Lemma
10.2
with
R=2L
,
T=2S_{5}
,
N=Q^{\vartheta}/dg_{1}g_{4}
,
\sigma=\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon
, and
j=ae
, we deduce that

(10.25)

\begin{split}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}L<\ell\leq 2L\\
(d,\ell)=1\end{subarray}}&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\psi\bmod ae\ell\\
\psi\neq\psi_{0}\end{subarray}}\Bigg\{\int_{\varepsilon-i2S_{5}}^{\varepsilon+i2S_{5}}\Bigg|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}H\leq Q^{\vartheta}/(dg_{1}g_{4})\\
(H,g_{3}cae\ell)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\lambda_{dg_{1}g_{4}H}\,\psi(H)}{H^{\frac{1}{2}+s_{5}}}\Bigg|\,|ds_{5}|\Bigg\}^{2}\\
&\ll(aeLQS_{5})^{\varepsilon}\bigg(\frac{Q^{\vartheta}LS_{5}}{dg_{1}g_{4}}+aeL^{2}S_{5}^{2}\bigg)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}H\leq Q^{\vartheta}/(dg_{1}g_{4})\\
(H,g_{3}cae\ell)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{|\lambda_{dg_{1}g_{4}H}|^{2}}{H^{1+\varepsilon}}\\
&\ll(dg_{1}g_{4}aeLQS_{5})^{\varepsilon}(Q^{\vartheta}LS_{5}+aeL^{2}S_{5}^{2}),\end{split}

where the last line follows from the assumption
\lambda_{h}\ll_{\varepsilon}h^{\varepsilon}
. Note that, in using Lemma
10.2
here, we may assume without loss of generality that
2S_{5}\geq 3
since if not, then we may extend the interval of integration because the integrand is nonnegative. Similarly, Lemma
10.2
implies

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}L<\ell\leq 2L\\
(d,\ell)=1\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\psi\bmod ae\ell\\
\psi\neq\psi_{0}\end{subarray}}\Bigg\{\int_{\varepsilon-i2S_{6}}^{\varepsilon+i2S_{6}}\Bigg|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}K\leq Q^{\vartheta}/(dg_{1}g_{3})\\
(K,g_{4}cae\ell)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\overline{\lambda_{dg_{1}g_{3}K}}\,\overline{\psi}(K)}{K^{\frac{1}{2}+s_{6}}}\Bigg|\,|ds_{6}|\Bigg\}^{2}

\displaystyle\ll(dg_{1}g_{3}aeLQS_{6})^{\varepsilon}(Q^{\vartheta}LS_{6}+aeL^{2}S_{6}^{2}).

From this, (
10.25
), and (
10.24
), we arrive at

(10.26)

\begin{split}\Sigma_{c,a,e,d,g_{1},g_{2},g_{3},g_{4}}\ll&(XQLaedg_{1}g_{3}g_{4})^{\varepsilon}\left(\frac{LQ}{c}\right)\sum_{S_{5}>0}\sum_{S_{6}>0}\frac{1}{S_{5}^{j_{1}-\varepsilon}S_{6}^{j_{2}-\varepsilon}}\bigg(1+\frac{XcQ^{\vartheta-1}}{L}\bigg)^{j_{1}+j_{2}-1}\\
&\times\Big(Q^{\vartheta}LS_{5}+aeL^{2}S_{5}^{2}\Big)^{1/2}\Big(Q^{\vartheta}LS_{6}+aeL^{2}S_{6}^{2}\Big)^{1/2}.\end{split}

By our choices of the values of
j_{1}
and
j_{2}
described in Table
1
, if
M,N\in\{2^{\nu}:\nu\in\mathbb{Z},\nu\geq 0\}
are given, then the term on the right-hand side of (
10.26
) that corresponds to the pair
(S_{5},S_{6})=(M,N)
is equal to the term that corresponds to the pair
(S_{5},S_{6})=(N,M)
. Thus, the part of the right-hand side of (
10.26
) that has
S_{6}\leq S_{5}
is a bound for the left-hand side. In that part, we have
j_{2}=0
by Table
1
. Hence

(10.27)

\begin{split}\Sigma_{c,a,e,d,g_{1},g_{2},g_{3},g_{4}}\ll(XQLaedg_{1}g_{3}g_{4})^{\varepsilon}\left(\frac{LQ}{c}\right)\mathop{\sum_{S_{5}>0}\sum_{S_{6}>0}}_{S_{6}\leq S_{5}}\frac{S_{6}^{\varepsilon}}{S_{5}^{j_{1}-\varepsilon}}\bigg(1+\frac{XcQ^{\vartheta-1}}{L}\bigg)^{j_{1}-1}\\
\times\Big(Q^{\vartheta}LS_{5}+aeL^{2}S_{5}^{2}\Big).\end{split}

Recall that, as stated below (
10.22
), the variables
S_{5}
and
S_{6}
in (
10.27
) each run through the set
\{2^{\nu}:\nu\in\mathbb{Z},\nu\geq 0\}
. Moreover, as described in Table
1
, we have
j_{1}=1
for the terms in (
10.27
) that have
S_{5}\leq 1+XcQ^{\vartheta-1}/L
and
j_{1}=3
for the terms with
S_{5}>1+XcQ^{\vartheta-1}/L
. We may thus evaluate the
S_{5}
- and
S_{6}
-sums in (
10.27
) by writing

(10.28)

\sum_{0<S_{6}\leq S_{5}}S_{6}^{\varepsilon}\ll S_{5}^{\varepsilon}

for each
S_{5}
,

(10.29)

\sum_{0<S_{5}\leq 1+XcQ^{\vartheta-1}/L}\frac{Q^{\vartheta}LS_{5}+aeL^{2}S_{5}^{2}}{S_{5}^{1-\varepsilon}}\ll(XcQ)^{\varepsilon}\bigg(Q^{\vartheta}L+aeL^{2}\bigg(1+\frac{XcQ^{\vartheta-1}}{L}\bigg)\bigg),

and

\displaystyle\sum_{S_{5}>1+XcQ^{\vartheta-1}/L}

\displaystyle\frac{Q^{\vartheta}LS_{5}+aeL^{2}S_{5}^{2}}{S_{5}^{3-\varepsilon}}\bigg(1+\frac{XcQ^{\vartheta-1}}{L}\bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle\ll(XcQ)^{\varepsilon}\bigg(Q^{\vartheta}L+aeL^{2}\bigg(1+\frac{XcQ^{\vartheta-1}}{L}\bigg)\bigg).

From this, (
10.27
), (
10.28
), and (
10.29
), we deduce that

\Sigma_{c,a,e,d,g_{1},g_{2},g_{3},g_{4}}\ll(XQLcaedg_{1}g_{3}g_{4})^{\varepsilon}\left(\frac{LQ}{c}\right)\big(Q^{\vartheta}L+aeL^{2}+aeLXcQ^{\vartheta-1}\big).

From this and (
10.19
), we arrive at

\displaystyle\sum_{h,k\leq Q^{\vartheta}}\frac{\lambda_{h}\overline{\lambda_{k}}}{\sqrt{hk}}\mathcal{U}^{r}(h,k)\ll

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq c\leq C}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}A,E,L\\
AL\ll XCQ^{\vartheta-1}\\
AEL\ll Y\end{subarray}}\sum_{A<a\leq 2A}\sum_{E<e\leq 2E}\frac{(CXQY)^{\varepsilon}}{AE^{2}L^{2}}

\displaystyle\times\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq g_{1},g_{2},g_{3},g_{4}\ll\max\{Q^{\vartheta},X\}\\
g_{1}g_{4}\leq Q^{\vartheta},\ g_{1}g_{3}\leq Q^{\vartheta},\ g_{2}\ll X\\
(g_{3},g_{4})=(ec,g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}g_{4})=1\\
a|g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}g_{4}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{(g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}g_{4})^{1-\varepsilon}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d\leq Q^{\vartheta}\\
(d,g_{3}g_{4}cae)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{d^{1-\varepsilon}}

(10.30)

\displaystyle\times\left(\frac{LQ}{c}\right)\big(Q^{\vartheta}L+AEL^{2}+AELXCQ^{\vartheta-1}\big)\ +\ (XCQY)^{\varepsilon}\frac{X^{2}CQ^{2\vartheta}}{Y}.

Our final task for this section is to evaluate the right-hand side of (
10.30
). Observe that

(10.31)

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d\leq Q^{\vartheta}\\
(d,g_{3}g_{4}cae)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{d^{1-\varepsilon}}\cdot d\ll Q^{\varepsilon}.

To evaluate the
g_{1},g_{2},g_{3},g_{4}
-sum in (
10.30
), we group together terms with the same product
g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}g_{4}
and use the divisor bound to write

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq g_{1},g_{2},g_{3},g_{4}\ll\max\{Q^{\vartheta},X\}\\
g_{1}g_{4}\leq Q^{\vartheta},\ g_{1}g_{3}\leq Q^{\vartheta},\ g_{2}\ll X\\
(g_{3},g_{4})=(ec,g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}g_{4})=1\\
a|g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}g_{4}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{(g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}g_{4})^{1-\varepsilon}}

\displaystyle\ll\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu\ll XQ^{2\vartheta}\\
a|\nu\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{\nu^{1-\varepsilon}}

\displaystyle\ll\frac{(XQa)^{\varepsilon}}{a}\ll\frac{(XQA)^{\varepsilon}}{A}.

From this, (
10.31
), and (
10.30
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\sum_{h,k\leq Q^{\vartheta}}\frac{\lambda_{h}\overline{\lambda_{k}}}{\sqrt{hk}}\mathcal{U}^{r}(h,k)

\displaystyle\ll Q\sum_{1\leq c\leq C}\frac{1}{c}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}A,E,L\\
AL\ll XCQ^{\vartheta-1}\\
AEL\ll Y\end{subarray}}\frac{(CXQY)^{\varepsilon}}{AEL}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.06749pt\times\big(Q^{\vartheta}L+AEL^{2}+AELXCQ^{\vartheta-1}\big)+\ (XCQY)^{\varepsilon}\frac{X^{2}CQ^{2\vartheta}}{Y}.

The condition
AL\ll XCQ^{\vartheta-1}
implies that
AEL^{2}\ll AELXCQ^{\vartheta-1}
because
A\gg 1
. Moreover, we have
\sum_{c\leq C}(1/c)\ll C^{\varepsilon}
. Hence

\displaystyle\sum_{h,k\leq Q^{\vartheta}}\frac{\lambda_{h}\overline{\lambda_{k}}}{\sqrt{hk}}\mathcal{U}^{r}(h,k)\ll(CXQY)^{\varepsilon}Q\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}A,E,L\\
AL\ll XCQ^{\vartheta-1}\\
AEL\ll Y\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{AEL}\big(LQ^{\vartheta}+AELXCQ^{\vartheta-1}\big)

(10.32)

\displaystyle+\ (XCQY)^{\varepsilon}\frac{X^{2}CQ^{2\vartheta}}{Y}.

Recall that, as stated below (
10.5
), each of the summation variables
A,E,L
in (
10.32
) runs through the set
\{2^{\nu}:\nu\in\mathbb{Z},\nu\geq-1\}
. We may thus evaluate the
A,E,L
-sum in (
10.32
) by writing

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}A,E,L\\
AL\ll XCQ^{\vartheta-1}\\
AEL\ll Y\end{subarray}}\frac{Q^{\vartheta}}{AE}\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}A,E,L\\
AEL\ll Y\end{subarray}}4Q^{\vartheta}\ll Y^{\varepsilon}Q^{\vartheta}

and

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}A,E,L\\
AL\ll XCQ^{\vartheta-1}\\
AEL\ll Y\end{subarray}}XCQ^{\vartheta-1}\leq XCQ^{\vartheta-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}A,E,L\\
AEL\ll Y\end{subarray}}1\ll Y^{\varepsilon}XCQ^{\vartheta-1}.

We conclude that

(10.33)

\sum_{h,k\leq Q^{\vartheta}}\frac{\lambda_{h}\overline{\lambda_{k}}}{\sqrt{hk}}\mathcal{U}^{r}(h,k)\ll_{\varepsilon}(XCQY)^{\varepsilon}Q(Q^{\vartheta}+XCQ^{\vartheta-1})\ +\ (XCQY)^{\varepsilon}\frac{X^{2}CQ^{2\vartheta}}{Y}.

11.
Finishing the proof of Theorem
2.2

We put together our estimates and deduce from (
4.7
), (
5.2
), (
6.2.3
), (
7.1
), (
8.3.7
), and (
9.3.29
) that

(11.1)

\mathcal{S}(h,k)=\mathcal{I}_{0}(h,k)+\mathcal{I}_{1}(h,k)+\mathcal{E}(h,k),

where

(11.2)

\begin{split}\mathcal{E}(h,k)=\mathcal{L}^{r}(h,k)+\mathcal{U}^{r}(h,k)+O\bigg(\bigg(Q+\frac{Q^{2}}{C}\bigg)\frac{(XCQhk)^{\varepsilon}(h,k)}{\sqrt{hk}}\bigg)\\
+O\Big((XCQhk)^{\varepsilon}\big(XC+X^{-\frac{1}{2}}Q^{\frac{5}{2}}+Q^{\frac{3}{2}}+X^{2}hkQ^{-96}\big)\Big).\end{split}

For any
\vartheta>0
, we have

\sum_{h,k\leq Q^{\vartheta}}\frac{(hk)^{\varepsilon}(h,k)}{hk}=\sum_{h,k\leq Q^{\vartheta}}\frac{(hk)^{\varepsilon}}{hk}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d|h\\
d|k\end{subarray}}\phi(d)=\sum_{d\leq Q^{\vartheta}}\frac{\phi(d)}{d^{2-\varepsilon}}\Bigg(\sum_{j\leq Q^{\vartheta}/d}\frac{1}{j^{1-\varepsilon}}\Bigg)^{2}\ll Q^{\varepsilon},

\sum_{h,k\leq Q^{\vartheta}}\frac{(hk)^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{hk}}\ll Q^{\vartheta+\varepsilon},

and

\sum_{h,k\leq Q^{\vartheta}}\frac{(hk)^{\varepsilon}hk}{\sqrt{hk}}\ll Q^{3\vartheta+\varepsilon}.

From these bounds, (
7.1.2
), (
10.33
), and (
11.2
), we deduce that if
\vartheta>0
and
\{\lambda_{h}\}_{h=1}^{\infty}
is any sequence of complex numbers such that
\lambda_{h}\ll_{\varepsilon}h^{\varepsilon}
for all positive integers
h
, then

(11.3)

\begin{split}\sum_{h,k\leq Q^{\vartheta}}\frac{\lambda_{h}\overline{\lambda_{k}}}{\sqrt{hk}}\mathcal{E}(h,k)\ll(XCQ)^{\varepsilon}\bigg(Q^{1+\vartheta}+\frac{Q^{2}}{C}\bigg)+(XCQY)^{\varepsilon}(Q^{1+\vartheta}+XCQ^{\vartheta})\\
+(XCQY)^{\varepsilon}\frac{X^{2}CQ^{2\vartheta}}{Y}+(XCQ)^{\varepsilon}\big(XCQ^{\vartheta}+X^{-\frac{1}{2}}Q^{\frac{5}{2}+\vartheta}+Q^{\frac{3}{2}+\vartheta}+X^{2}Q^{-96+3\vartheta}\big).\end{split}

Recall our assumption that
X=Q^{\eta}
with
1<\eta<2
. We optimize the upper bound (
11.3
) by choosing

C=Q^{1-\frac{\vartheta}{2}-\frac{\eta}{2}},

which implies
Q^{2}/C=XCQ^{\vartheta}=Q^{1+\frac{\vartheta}{2}+\frac{\eta}{2}}
. We impose the condition

\vartheta<2-\eta

so that
C\gg Q^{\varepsilon}
. Note that
\vartheta<2-\eta
implies
\vartheta<\eta
since
\eta>1
. We also choose
Y
to be a large power of
Q
, say
Y=Q^{99}
. With these choices for
C
and
Y
and the condition
\vartheta<2-\eta
, we deduce from (
11.3
) that

(11.4)

\sum_{h,k\leq Q^{\vartheta}}\frac{\lambda_{h}\overline{\lambda_{k}}}{\sqrt{hk}}\mathcal{E}(h,k)\ll Q^{1+\frac{\vartheta}{2}+\frac{\eta}{2}+\varepsilon}+Q^{\frac{5}{2}-\frac{\eta}{2}+\vartheta+\varepsilon}.

We have thus proved that the conclusion of Theorem
2.2
holds under the additional assumption (
3.5
). To complete the proof of Theorem
2.2
, it is left to show that (
11.4
) holds for any multisets
A
and
B
of complex numbers with moduli
\leq C_{1}/\log Q
, where
C_{1}
is an arbitrary fixed positive constant. We do this by showing for each
\ell=0,1
that
\mathcal{I}_{\ell}(h,k)
is holomorphic in each of the variables
\alpha\in A
and
\beta\in B
in the region where
|\alpha|,|\beta|\leq C_{1}/\log Q
for all
\alpha\in A
and
\beta\in B
(or, more precisely, that the only singularities of
\mathcal{I}_{\ell}(h,k)
in this region are removable singularities). The holomorphy of
\mathcal{I}_{0}(h,k)
is immediate from (
4.3
) with
\ell=0
: if
\ell=0
then the integrand on the right-hand side of (
4.3
) is holomorphic in each of the variables
\alpha\in A
and
\beta\in B
so long as
\alpha,\beta\ll\varepsilon
for each
\alpha\in A
and
\beta\in B
. To prove the holomorphy of
\mathcal{I}_{1}(h,k)
, define
I_{E,F}(n)
for finite multisets
E,F
of complex numbers by the Dirichlet series expression

\frac{\prod_{\xi\in E}\zeta(\xi+s)}{\prod_{\rho\in F}\zeta(\rho+s)}=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{I_{E,F}(n)}{n^{s}}.

This definition implies that if
\alpha\in A
and
\re(s)
is sufficiently large, then

\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{I_{A\cup\{-\beta\},\{\alpha\}}(n)}{n^{s}}=\zeta(-\beta+s)\prod_{\hat{\alpha}\neq\alpha}\zeta(\hat{\alpha}+s).

From this and the uniqueness of Dirichlet coefficients, we deduce that if
\alpha\in A
, then

(11.5)

I_{A\cup\{-\beta\},\{\alpha\}}(n)={\tau}_{A\smallsetminus\{\alpha\}\cup\{-\beta\}}(n)

for every positive integer
n
. Similarly, if
\beta\in B
, then

(11.6)

I_{B\cup\{-\alpha\},\{\beta\}}(n)={\tau}_{B\smallsetminus\{\beta\}\cup\{-\alpha\}}(n)

for every positive integer
n
. Now we claim that if
A
and
B
have no repeated elements and the elements of
A\cup B
are distinct from each other and are
\ll 1/\log Q
, then

(11.7)

\begin{split}\mathcal{I}_{1}(h,k)&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q=1\\
(q,hk)=1\end{subarray}}^{\infty}W\left(\frac{q}{Q}\right)\sideset{}{{}^{\flat}}{\sum}_{\chi\bmod q}\frac{1}{(2\pi i)^{4}}\int_{(\epsilon)}\int_{(\epsilon)}\oint_{|z|=\epsilon/4}\oint_{|y|=\epsilon/4}X^{s_{1}+s_{2}}\widetilde{V}(s_{1})\widetilde{V}(s_{2})\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\mathscr{X}(\tfrac{1}{2}-z+s_{1})\mathscr{X}(\tfrac{1}{2}-y+s_{2})q^{z-s_{1}+y-s_{2}}\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\frac{\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in A\\
\beta\in B\end{subarray}}\zeta(1+\alpha+\beta+s_{1}+s_{2})\prod_{\alpha\in A}\zeta(1+\alpha+z)\prod_{\beta\in B}\zeta(1+\beta+y)}{\prod_{\alpha\in A}\zeta(1+\alpha+s_{1}-y+s_{2})\prod_{\beta\in B}\zeta(1-z+s_{1}+\beta+s_{2})}\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\zeta(1+y+z-s_{1}-s_{2})\zeta(1-y-z+s_{1}+s_{2})\prod_{p|q}P_{0}\\
&\hskip 3.61371pt\times\prod_{p|hk}\Bigg\{P_{0}\!\!\!\!\!\!\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m,n<\infty\\
m+\text{ord}_{p}(h)=n+\text{ord}_{p}(k)\end{subarray}}\!\!\!\!\!\!\frac{I_{A_{s_{1}}\cup\{y-s_{2}\},\{-z+s_{1}\}}(p^{m})I_{B_{s_{2}}\cup\{z-s_{1}\},\{-y+s_{2}\}}(p^{n})}{p^{m/2}p^{n/2}}\Bigg\}\\
&\hskip 3.61371pt\times\prod_{p\nmid qhk}\Bigg\{P_{0}\ \sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\frac{I_{A_{s_{1}}\cup\{y-s_{2}\},\{-z+s_{1}\}}(p^{m})I_{B_{s_{2}}\cup\{z-s_{1}\},\{-y+s_{2}\}}(p^{m})}{p^{m}}\Bigg\}\\
&\hskip 18.06749pt\times\,dy\,dz\,ds_{2}\,ds_{1},\end{split}

where
P_{0}
is defined by

\displaystyle P_{0}=P_{0}(z,y,s_{1},s_{2};A,B)

\displaystyle:=\left(1-\frac{1}{p}\right)^{-2}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+y+z-s_{1}-s_{2}}}\right)\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1-y-z+s_{1}+s_{2}}}\right)

\displaystyle\times\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in A\\
\beta\in B\end{subarray}}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+\alpha+\beta+s_{1}+s_{2}}}\right)\prod_{\alpha\in A}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+\alpha+z}}\right)\prod_{\beta\in B}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+\beta+y}}\right)

\displaystyle\times\prod_{\alpha\in A}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+\alpha+s_{1}-y+s_{2}}}\right)^{-1}\prod_{\beta\in B}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1-z+s_{1}+\beta+s_{2}}}\right)^{-1}.

To see this, we use the residue theorem to evaluate the
z
- and
y
-integrals. The Euler product on the right-hand side of (
11.7
) converges absolutely by an argument similar to the proof of Lemma
9.3
. Thus the poles of the integrand that are enclosed by the circles
|z|=\epsilon/4
and
|y|=\epsilon/4
are precisely the poles of the factors

\prod_{\alpha\in A}\zeta(1+\alpha+z)\prod_{\beta\in B}\zeta(1+\beta+y).

After evaluating the
z
- and
y
-integrals using the residue theorem, we may simplify each residue by using (
11.5
) and (
11.6
) to see that the right-hand side of (
11.7
) is equal to the right-hand side of (
4.3
) with
\ell=1
. This proves our claim that (
11.7
) holds if
A
and
B
have no repeated elements and the elements of
A\cup B
are distinct from each other. Now the right-hand side of (
11.7
) is holomorphic in each of the variables
\alpha\in A
and
\beta\in B
in any region with
\alpha,\beta\ll 1/\log Q
for each
\alpha\in A
and
\beta\in B
because the Euler product in its integrand converges absolutely. Hence, by analytic continuation, it follows that
\mathcal{I}_{1}(h,k)
is holomorphic in each of the variables
\alpha\in A
and
\beta\in B
in the region. As a side note, we remark that this argument can be generalized to show the holomorphy of
\mathcal{I}_{\ell}(h,k)
for each
\ell
with
0\leq\ell\leq\min\{|A|,|B|\}
.

We have now shown that
\mathcal{I}_{0}(h,k)
and
\mathcal{I}_{1}(h,k)
are each holomorphic in each of the variables
\alpha\in A
and
\beta\in B
in any given region such that
\alpha,\beta\ll 1/\log Q
for each
\alpha\in A
and
\beta\in B
. Now
\mathcal{S}(h,k)
is holomorphic in the same region since its definition (
2.1
) has only finitely many nonzero terms by the assumption that
W
and
V
are compactly supported. It follows from these and (
11.1
) that
\mathcal{E}(h,k)
is also holomorphic in the same region. Thus, since (
11.3
) holds for
A,B
satisfying the condition (
3.5
), the maximum modulus principle implies that (
11.3
) also holds for finite multisets
A,B
satisfying
|\alpha|,|\beta|\leq C_{0}/\log Q
for all
\alpha\in A
and
\beta\in B
, where
C_{0}
is the arbitrary positive constant in (
3.5
). This completes the proof of Theorem
2.2
.

Appendix A
Proof of Lemma
10.2

In this section, we give the details of the proof of Lemma
10.2
, which is an analogue of Proposition 1 of
[
CIS19
]
and likewise a consequence of the hybrid large sieve in the form of Theorem 9.12 of
[
IK04
]
.

Proof of Lemma
10.2
.

To apply Theorem 9.12 of
[
IK04
]
, we need to express each
\chi
mod
qj
in terms of a product of two characters, one with modulus
\tilde{q}
and the other with modulus
\tilde{\jmath}
, where
\tilde{q}
and
\tilde{\jmath}
are factors of
qj
such that
(\tilde{q},\tilde{\jmath})=1
. To this end, recall that each Dirichlet character
\chi
mod
qj
is induced by a unique primitive Dirichlet character modulo some divisor of
qj
. We may write this divisor uniquely as
\tilde{q}\tilde{\jmath}
, where
(\tilde{q},j)=1
and
\tilde{\jmath}
is composed only of primes that divide
j
. Note that if
\chi
is non-principal, then
\tilde{q}\tilde{\jmath}>1
. Since
\tilde{q}\tilde{\jmath}
is a divisor of
qj
, it holds that
qj=D\tilde{q}\tilde{\jmath}
for some positive integer
D
, and dividing both sides by
(j,\tilde{\jmath})
implies

q\frac{j}{(j,\tilde{\jmath})}=D\tilde{q}\frac{\tilde{\jmath}}{(j,\tilde{\jmath})}.

It follows that
j/(j,\tilde{\jmath})
divides
D
because
j/(j,\tilde{\jmath})
is relatively prime to both
\tilde{q}
and
\tilde{\jmath}/(j,\tilde{\jmath})
. Thus we may write
D=dj/(j,\tilde{\jmath})
for some positive integer
d
. Hence
q=d\tilde{q}\tilde{\jmath}/(j,\tilde{\jmath})
. We have thus shown that for each non-principal
\chi
mod
qj
, there is a unique quadruple
(\tilde{\jmath},d,\tilde{q},\tilde{\chi})
such that
\tilde{\jmath}
is a positive integer composed only of the primes dividing
j
,
\tilde{q}
is a positive integer with
(\tilde{q},j)=1
and
\tilde{q}\tilde{\jmath}>1
,
d
is a positive integer such that
q=d\tilde{q}\tilde{\jmath}/(j,\tilde{\jmath})
, and
\tilde{\chi}
is a primitive character modulo
\tilde{q}\tilde{\jmath}
such that
\chi=\tilde{\chi}\chi_{0}
, where
\chi_{0}
is the principal character modulo
qj
. Therefore we have

\displaystyle\sum_{q\leq R}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\chi\bmod{qj}\\
\chi\neq\chi_{0}\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\Bigg(\int_{-T}^{T}\Bigg|\sum_{n\leq N}\frac{a_{n}\chi(n)}{n^{\sigma+it}}\Bigg|\,dt\Bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{q\leq R}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq\tilde{\jmath}<\infty\\
p|\tilde{\jmath}\Rightarrow p|j\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq d,\tilde{q}<\infty\\
(\tilde{q},j)=1\\
\tilde{q}\tilde{j}>1\\
q=d\tilde{q}{\tilde{\jmath}}/{(j,\tilde{\jmath})}\end{subarray}}\,\sideset{}{{}^{*}}{\sum}_{\tilde{\chi}\bmod{\tilde{q}\tilde{\jmath}}}\Bigg(\int_{-T}^{T}\Bigg|\sum_{n\leq N}\frac{a_{n}\tilde{\chi}(n)\chi_{0}(n)}{n^{\sigma+it}}\Bigg|\,dt\Bigg)^{2}

because the summand is nonnegative, where the * notation indicates that the sum is over primitive characters. We substitute
q=d\tilde{q}\tilde{\jmath}/(j,\tilde{\jmath})
to write

\displaystyle\sum_{q\leq R}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\chi\bmod{qj}\\
\chi\neq\chi_{0}\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\Bigg(\int_{-T}^{T}\Bigg|\sum_{n\leq N}\frac{a_{n}\chi(n)}{n^{\sigma+it}}\Bigg|\,dt\Bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq\tilde{\jmath}<\infty\\
p|\tilde{\jmath}\Rightarrow p|j\end{subarray}}\sum_{d\leq R\frac{(j,\tilde{\jmath})}{\tilde{\jmath}}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\tilde{q}\leq R\frac{(j,\tilde{\jmath})}{d\tilde{\jmath}}\\
(\tilde{q},j)=1\\
\tilde{q}\tilde{\jmath}>1\end{subarray}}\,\sideset{}{{}^{*}}{\sum}_{\tilde{\chi}\bmod{\tilde{q}\tilde{\jmath}}}\Bigg(\int_{-T}^{T}\Bigg|\sum_{n\leq N}\frac{a_{n}\tilde{\chi}(n)\chi_{0}(n)}{n^{\sigma+it}}\Bigg|\,dt\Bigg)^{2},

where
\chi_{0}
denotes the principal character modulo
qj=d\tilde{q}j\tilde{\jmath}/(j,\tilde{\jmath})
. Now we may replace the function
\chi_{0}
on the right-hand side with the characteristic function of the condition
(n,dj)=1
. Indeed, if
(n,dj)>1
, then
n
and
d\tilde{q}j\tilde{\jmath}/(j,\tilde{\jmath})
are not relatively prime, and so
\chi_{0}(n)=0
. If
(n,dj)=1
and
(n,\tilde{q})>1
, then
\tilde{\chi}(n)\chi_{0}(n)=\tilde{\chi}(n)
because both quantities are zero. If
(n,dj)=1
and
(n,\tilde{q})=1
, then
n
and
d\tilde{q}j\tilde{\jmath}/(j,\tilde{\jmath})
are relatively prime, and so
\chi_{0}(n)=1
. Hence

\displaystyle\sum_{q\leq R}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\chi\bmod{qj}\\
\chi\neq\chi_{0}\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\Bigg(\int_{-T}^{T}\Bigg|\sum_{n\leq N}\frac{a_{n}\chi(n)}{n^{\sigma+it}}\Bigg|\,dt\Bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq\tilde{\jmath}<\infty\\
p|\tilde{\jmath}\Rightarrow p|j\end{subarray}}\sum_{d\leq R\frac{(j,\tilde{\jmath})}{\tilde{\jmath}}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\tilde{q}\leq R\frac{(j,\tilde{\jmath})}{d\tilde{\jmath}}\\
(\tilde{q},j)=1\\
\tilde{q}\tilde{\jmath}>1\end{subarray}}\,\sideset{}{{}^{*}}{\sum}_{\tilde{\chi}\bmod{\tilde{q}\tilde{\jmath}}}\Bigg(\int_{-T}^{T}\Bigg|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\leq N\\
(n,dj)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{a_{n}\tilde{\chi}(n)}{n^{\sigma+it}}\Bigg|\,dt\Bigg)^{2}.

To bound the
\tilde{q},\tilde{\chi}
-sum, we apply the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and then Theorem 9.12 of
[
IK04
]
. (There, take
k=\tilde{\jmath}
,
Q=R(j,\tilde{\jmath})/(d\tilde{\jmath})
,
T=T
,
N=N
,
a_{n}=a_{n}/n^{\sigma}
if
(n,dj)=1
, and
a_{n}=0
if
(n,dj)>1
. Note that we may apply the theorem because if
\tilde{\chi}
is a primitive Dirichlet character modulo
\tilde{q}\tilde{\jmath}
, then
\tilde{\chi}
equals the product of a primitive Dirichlet character modulo
\tilde{q}
and a primitive Dirichlet character modulo
\tilde{\jmath}
since
(\tilde{q},\tilde{\jmath})=1
.) This gives

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\tilde{q}\leq R{(j,\tilde{\jmath})}/{(d\tilde{\jmath})}\\
(\tilde{q},j)=1\\
\tilde{q}\tilde{\jmath}>1\end{subarray}}\,\sideset{}{{}^{*}}{\sum}_{\tilde{\chi}\bmod{\tilde{q}\tilde{\jmath}}}

\displaystyle\Bigg(\int_{-T}^{T}\Bigg|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\leq N\\
(n,dj)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{a_{n}\tilde{\chi}(n)}{n^{\sigma+it}}\Bigg|\,dt\Bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2T\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\tilde{q}\leq R{(j,\tilde{\jmath})}/{(d\tilde{\jmath})}\\
(\tilde{q},j)=1\\
\tilde{q}\tilde{\jmath}>1\end{subarray}}\,\sideset{}{{}^{*}}{\sum}_{\tilde{\chi}\bmod{\tilde{q}\tilde{\jmath}}}\int_{-T}^{T}\Bigg|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\leq N\\
(n,dj)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{a_{n}\tilde{\chi}(n)}{n^{\sigma+it}}\Bigg|^{2}\,dt

\displaystyle\ll T(\log(jRTN))^{3}\bigg(N+\frac{(j,\tilde{\jmath})^{2}R^{2}T}{d^{2}\tilde{\jmath}}\bigg)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\leq N\\
(n,dj)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{|a_{n}|^{2}}{n^{2\sigma}},

where the implied constant is absolute. Therefore

\displaystyle\sum_{q\leq R}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\chi\bmod{qj}\\
\chi\neq\chi_{0}\end{subarray}}\Bigg(\int_{-T}^{T}\Bigg|\sum_{n\leq N}\frac{a_{n}\chi(n)}{n^{\sigma+it}}\Bigg|\,dt\Bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle\ll\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq\tilde{\jmath}<\infty\\
p|\tilde{\jmath}\Rightarrow p|j\end{subarray}}\sum_{d\leq R{(j,\tilde{\jmath})}/{\tilde{\jmath}}}T(\log(jRTN))^{3}\bigg(N+\frac{(j,\tilde{\jmath})^{2}R^{2}T}{d^{2}\tilde{\jmath}}\bigg)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\leq N\\
(n,dj)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{|a_{n}|^{2}}{n^{2\sigma}}.

We may ignore the condition
(n,dj)=1
and then evaluate the
d
-sum to deduce that

\displaystyle\sum_{q\leq R}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\chi\bmod{qj}\\
\chi\neq\chi_{0}\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\Bigg(\int_{-T}^{T}\Bigg|\sum_{n\leq N}\frac{a_{n}\chi(n)}{n^{\sigma+it}}\Bigg|\,dt\Bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle\ll T(\log(jRTN))^{3}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq\tilde{\jmath}<\infty\\
p|\tilde{\jmath}\Rightarrow p|j\end{subarray}}\bigg(\frac{(j,\tilde{\jmath})RN}{\tilde{\jmath}}+\frac{(j,\tilde{\jmath})^{2}R^{2}T}{\tilde{\jmath}}\bigg)\sum_{n\leq N}\frac{|a_{n}|^{2}}{n^{2\sigma}}.

Now let
j=\prod_{p|j}p^{j_{p}}
be the prime factorization of
j
. Multiplicativity implies

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq\tilde{\jmath}<\infty\\
p|\tilde{\jmath}\Rightarrow p|j\end{subarray}}\frac{(j,\tilde{\jmath})}{\tilde{\jmath}}

\displaystyle=\prod_{p|j}\sum_{\nu=0}^{\infty}\frac{p^{\min\{j_{p},\nu\}}}{p^{\nu}}=\prod_{p|j}\bigg(j_{p}+\frac{1}{1-\frac{1}{p}}\bigg)\ll j^{\varepsilon}

and

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq\tilde{\jmath}<\infty\\
p|\tilde{\jmath}\Rightarrow p|j\end{subarray}}\frac{(j,\tilde{\jmath})^{2}}{\tilde{\jmath}}

\displaystyle\leq j\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq\tilde{\jmath}<\infty\\
p|\tilde{\jmath}\Rightarrow p|j\end{subarray}}\frac{(j,\tilde{\jmath})}{\tilde{\jmath}}\ll j^{1+\varepsilon}.

Hence

\displaystyle\sum_{q\leq R}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\chi\bmod{qj}\\
\chi\neq\chi_{0}\end{subarray}}\Bigg(\int_{-T}^{T}\Bigg|\sum_{n\leq N}\frac{a_{n}\chi(n)}{n^{\sigma+it}}\Bigg|\,dt\Bigg)^{2}\ll(jRNT)^{\varepsilon}(RNT+jR^{2}T^{2})\sum_{n\leq N}\frac{|a_{n}|^{2}}{n^{2\sigma}}.

∎
